<div class="page-rail-marker" id="page-411"><button type="button" class="folio-trigger" data-page="411" data-folio="319" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n410_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect scan for page 319">[p. 319]</button></div><h2 class="sec-h1">Instrucc̃ons<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-001" href="#sec-08-fn-001" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-001" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> giuen by vs the Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> &amp; Comittees for the Company of Marchauntę tradinge [into] the East Indies vnto Lawrence ffemale our principall ffactor and the rest of o<sup>r</sup> ffactors now ymployed in this sixt voyadge.</h2><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p"><strong>1  I</strong>NPRIMIS forasmuch as in o<sup>r</sup> generall Comission we haue largelie sett downe what we haue thought necessary to be obserued in o<sup>r</sup> generall busines and doe herew<sup>th</sup> deliver yo<sup>u</sup> a Coppy thereof praying yo<sup>u</sup> as much as in yo<sup>u</sup> lieth to obserue the contentę thereof, we haue thought yt fitt onlie<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-002" href="#sec-08-fn-002" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-002" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> for yo<sup>r</sup> pticulare direcc̃ons to recomend to yo<sup>r</sup> Considerac̃on this o<sup>r</sup> aduise followinge</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">2   And because we haue beene pswaded to appointe and nominate yo<sup>u</sup> o<sup>r</sup> principall and cheefe ffactor in this voyadge out of the good reportę of diuers yo<sup>r</sup> freindę and o<sup>r</sup> owne opinion of yo<sup>r</sup> honest behavio<sup>r</sup>   And the hope of yo<sup>r</sup> instruccious endevo<sup>r</sup> and skilfull experience in the manadgeing the affaires of m<sup>r</sup>chandize of soe greate ymportance as yo<sup>u</sup> knowe theis to be, we wish yo<sup>u</sup> that wheresoeu<sup>r</sup> yo<sup>u</sup> shall come or settle a ffactory, to inquier and informe yo<sup>r</sup> selfe sufficiently of the Condicons and manners of the people, and gou<sup>r</sup>nm<sup>t</sup> and what Comodity we may send from hence most vendable in those ptę, Espetially concerninge wollen Comodities, and other the natiue Comodities of o<sup>r</sup> owne Country whereby we may be able to dryue a trade w<sup>th</sup>out the transportac̃on of money w<sup>ch</sup> is the cheefe scope of o<sup>r</sup> desires   And of those sortę of Comodities aswell wollen as others the quallity quantity culler and price   And of Cloth whether highe shorne or lowe, strayned or vnstrayned,   And in lyke sorte to aduise of all other Comodities what yo<sup>u</sup> shall imagine needfull for o<sup>r</sup> further direcc̃ons.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">3   Alsoe wee desier to vnderstand what comodity yo<sup>u</sup> finde there w<sup>ch</sup> may bee vented to proffitt here or any other place wherevnto o<sup>r</sup> ships shall nauigate in this or in any other succeedinge voyadge.   As alsoe what trade those people haue w<sup>th</sup> other nations either remote or neare confineinge vnto<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-003" href="#sec-08-fn-003" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-003" role="doc-noteref">3</a></sup> them, <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-412" data-page="412" data-folio="320" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n411_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 320">[p. 320]</button> And wherein there traffique espetially consiste together w<sup>th</sup> the valua~on of their coynes, Their seu<sup>r</sup>all weightę and measures and what other occurrence needfull for vs to knowe, and thereof to endevo<sup>r</sup> by all possible meanes to giue vs intelligence overland, as by the way of the red sea to Cairo directing yo<sup>r</sup> łres to the ffrench Consull there Resident, To be sent to Mercellus<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-004" href="#sec-08-fn-004" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-004" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> inclosed to Henery Berry or any other Englishman there Resident, Alsoe by the Persian gulfe by any Carauan throughe Persia either bound for Constantinople, Aleppo, or Tripole directing yo<sup>r</sup> łres to the ymbassado<sup>r</sup> or Consull there resident to be Conveyed vnto vs, As alsoe by the Caspyan Sea throughe Muscovia vnto the English Agent there beinge, To w<sup>ch</sup> purpose we giue yo<sup>u</sup> an Alphabett of Characters wherein we wish yo<sup>u</sup> to write for more secrecy.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">4 Yf yo<sup>u</sup> should touche at Aden or Machowe<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-005" href="#sec-08-fn-005" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-005" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> in the red sea beinge in yo<sup>r</sup> way before yo<sup>u</sup> come at Suratte we wish yo<sup>u</sup> to inquier whether those of the fourth voyadge haue not setled a factory theare, yf they haue wee desier that they may receiue such comforte from yo<sup>u</sup> as yo<sup>u</sup> may Conveniently afford them and acquainte them of the intelligence we haue had by other mens łres of o<sup>r</sup> ships beinge at Machowe, and of the bartringe for Indico, But not haueinge any łres from them, w<sup>ch</sup> as they knowe wee gaue them in spetiall chardge &amp; to that purpose sent one or twoe to be sent from Aden overland, wee are ignorant whether there be any incourradgm<sup>t</sup> to maintaine trade in those partę, But yf by their łres or by any other meanes we shall vnderstand any hope of good trade, Althoughe yt be but for vent of o<sup>r</sup> english Comodities onelie we shall not omitt to frequent those porte<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-006" href="#sec-08-fn-006" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-006" role="doc-noteref">3</a></sup> w<sup>th</sup> o<sup>r</sup> next shippinge, In the meane tyme yf yo<sup>u</sup> may put of in any reasonable sorte either for syluer or gould, any of the Comodities we now send we wishe the same to be done as more att large in o<sup>r</sup> generall Comission is expressed.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">5 Att yo<sup>r</sup> comeing to Suratt informe yo<sup>r</sup> selfe whether m<sup>r</sup> W<sup>m</sup>: Hawkins or any other before yo<sup>r</sup> arriuall haue setled Capitula~ons w<sup>th</sup> the greate Magoule of that Country fitt and safe for o<sup>r</sup> quiett trade into his Territorie &amp; yf the same be not done in such sufficient manner as yo<sup>u</sup> deeme expedient, Then wee wishe yo<sup>u</sup> in <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-413" data-page="413" data-folio="321" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n412_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 321">[p. 321]</button> the meane tyme to procure such safe Conducte from the kinge of Cambaia, &amp; the other cheefe Officers of that Country as may incowrrage yo<sup>u</sup> to negotiate amongst them And w<sup>th</sup>all convenient expedi<span class="sc">c</span>õn to dispeede o<sup>r</sup> Ships from thence, accordinge as in o<sup>r</sup> Comission generall we haue att lardge directed yo<sup>u</sup> And that done then yo<sup>u</sup> may in comely sorte accompanied w<sup>th</sup> such others of o<sup>r</sup> ffactors as yo<sup>u</sup> shall thinke fitt, repair vnto the Courte of the greate Magull to establish further Capitula<span class="sc">c</span>õn for o<sup>r</sup> peaceable trade either at Suratt or any other porte or porte, place or places w<sup>th</sup>in any of his dominions alwaies remembringe the hono<sup>r</sup> of o<sup>r</sup> kinge and Country &amp; the reputacons of o<sup>r</sup> negotia<span class="sc">c</span>õns in those parte &amp; for the greater dignity of yo<sup>r</sup> ymploym<sup>t</sup> vnto him we haue ꝑcured his Ma<sup>ties</sup> lre vnto the Magull, the originall &amp; Coppy whereof we deliu<sup>r</sup> yo<sup>u</sup> wishinge that the deliu<sup>r</sup>y thereof may be w<sup>th</sup> that hono<sup>r</sup> that may be well fittinge soe greate a Monarche, &amp; as the oportunity of the place &amp; other yo<sup>r</sup> Convenience in those pte shall pmitt accompanyinge some honorable present of veluett Plate or whate ells more acceptable both vnto the greate Magull &amp; some spetiall men aboute him as in yo<sup>r</sup> good discree<span class="sc">c</span>õn shall seeme meete.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">6 And for yo<sup>r</sup> better instruc<span class="sc">c</span>õns we doe herew<sup>th</sup> deliu<sup>r</sup> yo<sup>u</sup> the Coppy of such priuiledge as S<sup>r</sup> Iames Lancaster obtayned from the kinge of Achine wherevnto yo<sup>u</sup> may ad or diminishe as occasion offereth &amp; the lyke Coppy we haue formerly deliu<sup>r</sup>ed to those of the third and fourth voyadge.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">7 ffurther we wish yo<sup>u</sup> diligently to informe yo<sup>r</sup> selfe whether we may not settle a ffactory at Dabull neare Suratt or Negratitte<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-007" href="#sec-08-fn-007" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-007" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> in the Ryver of Syndus or in any other porte towne, where o<sup>r</sup> shippinge may not be subiect to bard<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-008" href="#sec-08-fn-008" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-008" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> hauens as at Suratt nor in soe greate danger of the Portugalls And where we may aswell vent o<sup>r</sup> Comodities and make retornes in the Comodities of those Countries at reasonable rate.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">8 Alsoe inquier as much as yo<sup>u</sup> can of the depth, breadth, Ebbs, flowinge, Currante &amp; sholes of the porte &amp; havens thereaboute &amp; what the chardge of transporta<span class="sc">c</span>õn of Comodytie from porte to porte will cost by land as by Sea and whatt customes &amp; <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-414" data-page="414" data-folio="322" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n413_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 322">[p. 322]</button> duties are payable at each porte aswell for goode and money as ancoradge of shippinge &amp;c:</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">9 If any Çomodities either of the fourth voyadge shall be prouided at Aden or of the third or fourth voyadge at Suratt or any other poorte wherevnto yo<sup>r</sup> shippinge shall arriue w<sup>ch</sup> are to be sent for England we wish yo<sup>u</sup> to suffer the same to be laden abourd theis o<sup>r</sup> shipps vnder seu<sup>r</sup>all marke aswell w<sup>th</sup>in the Canvas or other Cou<sup>r</sup>ture asw<sup>th</sup>out for the better discou<sup>r</sup>y thereof, And that there be pticular Invoices &amp; bills of ladeinge of all the seu<sup>r</sup>all pcells that shall be laden for each accounte for avoydeinge Confusion in theis pticular accompte alsoe yf any remainders shall be lefte &amp; deliu<sup>r</sup>ed over vnto yo<sup>u</sup> we would haue a true and pfect Inventory thereof kept in a booke Couchant, wherein we will that all busines, either for buyinge sellinge bartringe exchandging shippinge receaueinge transportacõns and what ells needfull to be registred conc<sup>r</sup>ninge o<sup>r</sup> busines and yo<sup>r</sup> ymploym<sup>t</sup> be from tyme to tyme dulie trulie and fairelie kept and written and a true coppy thereof sent vs by eu<sup>r</sup>y shippinge both vnderwritten by o<sup>r</sup> cheefe Agent as alsoe by fower or fyue of those o<sup>r</sup> ffactor<sup>s</sup> w<sup>ch</sup> are in principall places of ymploym<sup>t</sup> vnder him.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">10 And for the better passinge of yo<sup>r</sup> busines wheresoeu<sup>r</sup> yo<sup>u</sup> recide we thinke yt fitt &amp; soe requier that yo<sup>u</sup> appointe out of the best quallified amongst those ffactor<sup>s</sup> w<sup>ch</sup> are vnder yo<sup>r</sup> gou<sup>r</sup>nm<sup>t</sup> One whoe [shall] keepe booke faire written, aswell Iournall as lidger after the order of Debitor &amp; Credito<sup>r</sup> of all o<sup>r</sup> busines there passed, and alsoe that some other there vnto most apt may be appointed Casheare to receiue and pay keepinge a Cashe booke very faire written w<sup>th</sup>out blottinge scrapeinge or interlyneinge and alsoe that yo<sup>u</sup> doe appointe eu<sup>r</sup>y other ffacto<sup>r</sup> in such other Offices and places as they are best capeable w<sup>th</sup> this Caution that yo<sup>u</sup> doe weekelie or oftener if be soe requisite call them to render a reason and giue accompte of their seu<sup>r</sup>all chardge and accordinge to their behavior<sup>s</sup> &amp; good pformance thereof either to contynew them in their chardge or to displace them w<sup>th</sup> such reprehension as yo<sup>u</sup> thinke needfull.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">11 And because there is noe meanes more prevalent to strengthen and Confirme the waies of the godlie in righteousnes <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-415" data-page="415" data-folio="323" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n414_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 323">[p. 323]</button> then the spiritt of God w<sup>ch</sup> is the guide vnto all good motions,<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-009" href="#sec-08-fn-009" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-009" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> and noe ayde more pregnant to support and vphould the siner from fallinge into wickednes then the grace of God w<sup>ch</sup> preserueth in all good worke and that almighty god out of his Mercy in Ihesus Christe hath promysed not onlie to here vs when we call vpon him, but alsoe to graunte vs his spiritt of grace to strengthen and assiste vs in all accõns w<sup>ch</sup> Sathan or the world can inforce against vs, wee exhorte yo<sup>u</sup> in the feare of god to be very carefull to assemble together yo<sup>r</sup> whole familie eu<sup>r</sup>y morninge and eveninge And to Ioyne together in all humility w<sup>th</sup> harty prayer to almighty god for his m<sup>r</sup>cifull proteccõn and favo<sup>r</sup> to yo<sup>u</sup> in all yo<sup>r</sup> proceedinge and for all other his graces needfull for a true Christian to desier.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">12 And for that Ciuell behavio<sup>r</sup> is very Requisite for the begettinge of loue and estimacõn amongst those heathenishe people we pray yo<sup>u</sup> to settle such modest and sober governm<sup>t</sup> in yo<sup>r</sup> houshould that neyther amongst themselues there bee contentious quarrells or other occasions of strife w<sup>ch</sup> may tend to the preiudice of o<sup>r</sup> affaires and be a scandull to o<sup>r</sup> profession and religion, As alsoe that none of yo<sup>r</sup> people giue iust cause to any stranger to Complaine of their misdemeanors.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">13 And because we desier the hono<sup>r</sup> of o<sup>r</sup> kinge and the reputac̃on of o<sup>r</sup> traffique amongst them we wishe yo<sup>u</sup> to comporte yo<sup>r</sup> selfe, both in yo<sup>r</sup> habitt and howse keepinge in such comelie and convenient manner as neyther may disparadge o<sup>r</sup> busines nor be accompted too excessiue in expences.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">14 And for that we imagine yf yo<sup>u</sup> settle at Surate yo<sup>u</sup> shall not soe speedilie finde dispacth of all the Comodities wee haue appointed to be there landed, but that there will be a greate parte thereof lefte to sell after the depture of o<sup>r</sup> Shipps from thence. Wee pray yo<sup>u</sup> to advance the price thereof to the vttermost, for by settinge a good estimac̃on of o<sup>r</sup> Comodities at the firste, we shall be the better able to Contynue the same heereafter w<sup>ch</sup> may well be done when we haue the trade onlie w<sup>th</sup> them.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">15 And for that o<sup>r</sup> stocke remaineinge behinde in yo<sup>r</sup> hande may be of some good value, we would haue yo<sup>u</sup> to prouide some <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-416" data-page="416" data-folio="324" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n415_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 324">[p. 324]</button> speciall Comodities at the best tyme of their cheepnes as yo<sup>u</sup> knowe are most vendible in England &amp; the same to haue in a readines againste o<sup>r</sup> next shippinge, w<sup>ch</sup> God willinge yf we haue incourradgm<sup>t</sup> shall be yearelie.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">16. And for that yo<sup>u</sup> shall not alwaies finde such Comodities as yo<sup>u</sup> desier, but may haue money lyinge in Chest, We expect yo<sup>u</sup> will make such proffitt thereof, either by ymploym<sup>t</sup> in Comodities to Resell at dearer markettę:<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-010" href="#sec-08-fn-010" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-010" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> or otherwyse as the trafficke of that Country shall afford, and soe as yo<sup>u</sup> may doe the same w<sup>th</sup> saffetie, And not to be prevented when yo<sup>u</sup> shall haue occasion to vse the same for to prouide Comodity for o<sup>r</sup> purpose herein wee must depend principally vpon yo<sup>r</sup> prouidence &amp; proffitable reguard to o<sup>r</sup> affaires.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">17 And for that there be many ffactor<sup>s</sup> abroad dispersed as wee Imagine in sundry places w<sup>ch</sup> haue beene sent out by the Adventurers aswell of the third voyadge as of the fourth, Wee will that yf yo<sup>u</sup> finde any of them in those places, wherevnto yo<sup>u</sup> shall come, w<sup>ch</sup> are more fittinge to remaine there, Then those that now goe in theis Shipps, That then yo<sup>u</sup> doe Contynewe them in such chardge as yo<sup>u</sup> thinke fittinge. Prouided that they are to expect noe increase of wages, other then what was formerlie agreed w<sup>th</sup> them here in England, As alsoe that they are not to requier<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-011" href="#sec-08-fn-011" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-011" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> their wages any more in the Country but here in England either at their retourne, or by their appointem<sup>t</sup> vnto their freindę onelie wee are Content to allowe them Tenn poundę or xx m<sup>r</sup>kę p̄ Annū, yf they desier the same to finde them app̄rell, And to be accompted to be receiued after 5<sup>s</sup> starlinge for the Riall of 8, &amp; not otherwyse. And whereas some fewe of them haue beene allowed to trade yearelie for some small stocke, wee doe now absolutelie disallowe<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-012" href="#sec-08-fn-012" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-012" role="doc-noteref">3</a></sup> thereof &amp; will noe longer p̄mitt any of them to exercise any priuate trade whatsoeu<sup>r</sup>.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">18 And therefore yf any of them shall dislike to contynewe there, vpon theis Condic̃ons we wish yo<sup>u</sup> to send him<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-013" href="#sec-08-fn-013" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-013" role="doc-noteref">4</a></sup> for England w<sup>th</sup> o<sup>r</sup> first shippinge and for that we shall desier to vnderstand from m<sup>r</sup> Hawkins more p̄ticuler adu<sup>r</sup>ticem<sup>t</sup> of those p̄ties we desier that he may come home in one of theis o<sup>r</sup> three <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-417" data-page="417" data-folio="325" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n416_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 325">[p. 325]</button> Shipps, In some convenient manner, respectinge the place we sent him forthe.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">19 And althoughe wee knowe yo<sup>u</sup> are not ignoraunt what yt is to deale w<sup>th</sup> people of faithlesse condicõn, yett wee thinke yt not amisse to put yo<sup>u</sup> in mynde to be very carefull to buy of the best sorte of eu<sup>r</sup>y Comodity, &amp; for thinge garbleable to haue them made very cleane for otherwyse both Coste, fraight, costume &amp; chardge is cleane loste, alsoe lett yo<sup>r</sup> goode be carefully packed and faierlie marked both w<sup>th</sup>in and w<sup>th</sup>out, and distincte, And Invoices pfect sent by eu<sup>r</sup>y Shipp w<sup>th</sup> bills of ladeinge and the accompte of the Coste and chardge of every Comoditie in m<sup>r</sup>chantlike order, as alsoe send the Inventory of all goode remaineinge vpon eu<sup>r</sup>y accompte &amp; the valuacõn thereof &amp; keepe seuerall<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-014" href="#sec-08-fn-014" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-014" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> accompte of eu<sup>r</sup>y voyadge.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">20 Wee vnderstand that in the Persian gulfe vpon the Coaste of Arabia over against Ormus there is greate store of pearle fished,<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-015" href="#sec-08-fn-015" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-015" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> whereof as of the needfull occurrances we pray yo<sup>u</sup> giue vs intelligence.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">21 When yo<sup>u</sup> shall repaier to the greate Magull or be otherwyse setled in his Countrie w<sup>th</sup> peaceable trade yo<sup>u</sup> may endevo<sup>r</sup> by the best meanes yo<sup>u</sup> can either by the Magull or some other his cheefe Comanders in his Country to procure what yo<sup>u</sup> may for the restitucõn of o<sup>r</sup> goode taken by the Portugall from vs w<sup>th</sup>in the streame<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-016" href="#sec-08-fn-016" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-016" role="doc-noteref">3</a></sup> of Suratt, As alsoe for the release of o<sup>r</sup> people at Goa, yf the same be not already done, or w<sup>th</sup>out hope to be<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-017" href="#sec-08-fn-017" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-017" role="doc-noteref">4</a></sup> obtayned in yo<sup>r</sup> Capitulacõns at Suratt yo<sup>u</sup> are amongst others to endevo<sup>r</sup> to procure to be free from paym<sup>t</sup> of Customes wholly, aswell of goode as for moneys both inwarde as outwarde yf yo<sup>u</sup> can obtaine the same. But yf yo<sup>u</sup> can not obtaine the same soe generally yet we doubt not but yo<sup>u</sup> shall agree for some reasonable Customes vpon goode onely &amp; that yo<sup>r</sup> moneys may be free of all dutie, of custome &amp; such lyke, and whatsoeu<sup>r</sup> yo<sup>u</sup> shall agree to pay yt may be for such goode yo<sup>u</sup> shall onlie land at Suratt,<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-018" href="#sec-08-fn-018" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-018" role="doc-noteref">5</a></sup> &amp; <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-418" data-page="418" data-folio="326" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n417_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 326">[p. 326]</button> that the rest vnladen<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-019" href="#sec-08-fn-019" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-019" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> may be free thereof, And for that we Imagine that the Portugalls will endevo<sup>r</sup> by all meanes possible as well by forces by Sea, As practises ashore to intercept yo<sup>u</sup> in yo<sup>r</sup> busines we pray yo<sup>u</sup> to be watchfull &amp; vigilent to prevent all their enterprices against you whatsoeu<sup>r</sup>.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">22 Wee deliver vnto yo<sup>u</sup> twoe gould Ringe sett w<sup>th</sup> Emraules being very faire stones the w<sup>ch</sup> yo<sup>u</sup> may either giue for presente at Suratt, yf yo<sup>u</sup> see good occasion or otherwyse to sell them for o<sup>r</sup> most advantage. And because nothinge is more c<sup>r</sup>teine then death, and vnc<sup>r</sup>teine then the tyme thereof, we would willingly establishe such a course in the Succession of o<sup>r</sup> cheefe ffacto<sup>r</sup> as may giue vs good hope of the good accomplishm<sup>t</sup> of o<sup>r</sup> affaires And take away all occasion of Emulac̃on from amongst yo<sup>u</sup>, And therefore wee doe resolue and ordeine, that yf yo<sup>u</sup> m<sup>r</sup> Lawrence ffemell shall happen to depte this lyfe before such tyme as yo<sup>u</sup> shall be settled at Suratt That then Iohn ffowler doe succeede yo<sup>u</sup> in yo<sup>r</sup> place vntill their arriuall thither, And yf he happen to die then consequentlie Hughe ffraine,<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-020" href="#sec-08-fn-020" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-020" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> Beniamyn Greene, Robert Larkyn,<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-021" href="#sec-08-fn-021" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-021" role="doc-noteref">3</a></sup> Thomas Glemon,<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-022" href="#sec-08-fn-022" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-022" role="doc-noteref">4</a></sup> Iohn Williams,<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-023" href="#sec-08-fn-023" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-023" role="doc-noteref">5</a></sup> ffrancis Slany Robert Weeb<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-024" href="#sec-08-fn-024" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-024" role="doc-noteref">6</a></sup> Iohn Stoughton &amp; Baylie Ball and W<sup>m</sup>: Poulter &amp; W<sup>m</sup>: Reynolde to be attendant vpon the cheefe ffacto<sup>r</sup> whosoeu<sup>r</sup>, But when God shall send yo<sup>u</sup> Iohn ffowler and the rest to Suratt &amp; that m<sup>r</sup> ffemall be depted this lyfe before, Then wee thinke fitt that yf either m<sup>r</sup> W<sup>m</sup>: Hawkins, or w<sup>m</sup> ffinche bee there resident &amp; liueinge<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-025" href="#sec-08-fn-025" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-025" role="doc-noteref">7</a></sup> That then m<sup>r</sup> Hawkins be o<sup>r</sup> cheefe Agent duringe his aboade there, And w<sup>m</sup> ffinche to succeede him and soe consequentlie Iohn ffowler, Hughe ffraine and the rest accordinge as they are by vs heere inrancked, But yf m<sup>r</sup> ffemalle shall liue vntill his arriuall &amp; settlinge at Suratt or ells where in the East <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-419" data-page="419" data-folio="327" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n418_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 327">[p. 327]</button> Indies then o<sup>r</sup> desier is in case of his death, that he would make choise aswell amongst o<sup>r</sup> other ffactor<sup>s</sup> there resident as of theis we now send, of such a man whoe for his honest conu<sup>r</sup>sation &amp; best vnderstandinge to mannadge soe waightie a busines as this chardge is he shall deeme most fitt wishinge herein that noe p<em>ar</em>tiall respect be had of any but the p<em>er</em>son most worthiest may be appointed to be both o<sup>r</sup> cheefe Agent and super intendant to the rest of o<sup>r</sup> ffactor<sup>s</sup> vnder him straightlie chardgeinge eu<sup>r</sup>y one of yo<sup>u</sup> that are interteyned in this o<sup>r</sup> seruice as you tender o<sup>r</sup> favor<sup>s</sup> and yo<sup>r</sup> owne preferm<sup>t</sup>. That yo<sup>u</sup> giue due respect of priority aswell to o<sup>r</sup> cheefe Agent as to all other o<sup>r</sup> ffactor<sup>s</sup> vnder him accordinge to their degrees and places &amp; that there be noe swellinge ambition amongst yo<sup>u</sup>, Envieinge to giue p<sup>r</sup>sidency to such as either by o<sup>r</sup> selues o<sup>r</sup> by o<sup>r</sup> cheefe Agent shalbe placed before yo<sup>u</sup> but eu<sup>r</sup>y one in his seu<sup>r</sup>all place &amp; accordinge to his place to endevo<sup>r</sup> o<sup>r</sup> benefitt in yo<sup>r</sup> ymploym<sup>t</sup> in o<sup>r</sup> seruice and the mutuall preseruac̃on of Christian loue amongst yo<sup>u</sup>, And althoughe by accidentall<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-026" href="#sec-08-fn-026" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-026" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> succession of place may fall vpon some one of yo<sup>u</sup> whose wages is but small yett we will not yeald to any Succession of Sallary or increase of wages, but retaine that power in o<sup>r</sup> selues to reward where we shall finde the same convenient, there is one Baylie Ball whoe hath beene a houshoulder<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-027" href="#sec-08-fn-027" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-027" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> &amp; reputed an honest man, But now content to goe as Steward in the Pepp Corne whose name we haue added amongst the rest of o<sup>r</sup> ffactor<sup>s</sup>, for that we imaginne he may fitt for better place yf yo<sup>u</sup> keepe, him in the Country w<sup>ch</sup> we leaue to yo<sup>r</sup> discrecc̃on.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">23. Iff yo<sup>u</sup> shall finde in yo<sup>r</sup> trauaile or can convenientlie come by any rare byrde<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-028" href="#sec-08-fn-028" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-028" role="doc-noteref">3</a></sup> or beaste or any other thinge fitt for vs to present to his ma<sup>tie</sup>: or any of the noble Lorde that are o<sup>r</sup> honorable freinde we pray and requier yo<sup>u</sup> to be carefull to prouide and send the same w<sup>th</sup> espetiall chardge to some carefull man or men that retourne to see them carefully kept &amp; deliu<sup>r</sup>ed to vs. And w<sup>th</sup>all yf yo<sup>u</sup> shall knowe any of the Mariners or other to haue any such w<sup>ch</sup> yo<sup>u</sup> can not procure from them for reson then that yo<sup>u</sup> send vs notice thereof to the end we may take order<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-029" href="#sec-08-fn-029" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-029" role="doc-noteref">4</a></sup> therein accordinglie.</p></div><div class="page-rail-marker" id="page-420"><button type="button" class="folio-trigger" data-page="420" data-folio="328" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n419_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect scan for page 328">[p. 328]</button></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">In London the day of March 1609.</aside></div><p class="prose-p">S<sup>r</sup> Thomas Smyth<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-030" href="#sec-08-fn-030" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-030" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup>      Lawrence ffemell Henry Boothby      Iohn Williams W<sup>m</sup> Speede   ffr: Slany   Hughe ffraine Edward Langley      Iohn Stoughton William Reynolde      George Cockaine Rowland Webb. Beniamyn Greene Hughe Greete Thomas Glenham Robert Larkyn A Comission<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-031" href="#sec-08-fn-031" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-031" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> sett downe by Vs the Governor Deputy &amp; Comittees for the Marchaunte of London tradeinge to the East Indies for better direcc̃õn of o<sup>r</sup> loueinge freind S<sup>r</sup> Henry Middleton Generall the Leiutenante &amp; others the Marchaunte, M<sup>rs</sup>: &amp; Mariners in this o<sup>r</sup> intended VI<sup>th</sup> voyadge [to] the East Indies w<sup>ch</sup> we humblie beseech Almightie God to blesse w<sup>th</sup> a happie Concluc̃õn.</p></div><figure class="archival-plate" id="fig-018"><img src="/figures/eic-first-letter-book-1600-1619/fig-018_p0420.webp" alt="Decorative woodcut initial letter I." loading="lazy"></figure><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">I.</aside></div><p class="prose-p"><span class="sc">Nprimis</span> whereas wee the Marchaunte abouesaid w<sup>th</sup> o<sup>r</sup> greate chardge &amp; trouble haue p<sup>r</sup>pared twoe shipps and apynnace (to say) the Trades Increase Gyles Thornton<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-032" href="#sec-08-fn-032" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-032" role="doc-noteref">3</a></sup> m<sup>r</sup>. The Pepper Corne Abraham <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-421" data-page="421" data-folio="329" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n420_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 329">[p. 329]</button> Lawes<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-033" href="#sec-08-fn-033" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-033" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> M<sup>r</sup> &amp; the little Darlinge William Pemerton M<sup>r</sup>: for a voyadge by Godę grace in them to be made to the East Indies [and] haue furnished the same sufficientlie &amp; in plentifull manner w<sup>th</sup> men tackle victualls &amp; all other needfull ꝑuisions, fitt for such a voyadge  Of an espetiall trust &amp; confidence we haue of the Integrity, wisdome &amp; resoluc̃on of o<sup>r</sup> loueinge freind S<sup>r</sup> Henry Middleton knight doe appointe &amp; authorize him Generall to goe in the <em>Trades increase</em> &amp; to Comand all o<sup>r</sup> said Ships and the men in them, haueinge ꝑcured him sufficient authority from o<sup>r</sup> Sou<sup>r</sup>eigne Lord the kingę ma<sup>tie</sup>: for that purpose<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-034" href="#sec-08-fn-034" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-034" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> not doubtinge but that he will soe behaue himselfe in his chardge as he may bee both feared &amp; loued,  And that he will w<sup>th</sup>all his diligence &amp; endevo<sup>r</sup> prosequute o<sup>r</sup> designes &amp; labo<sup>r</sup> to bringe this costly voyadge to a happie end.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">2  Item wee doe appointe and ordeine o<sup>r</sup> loueinge freind m<sup>r</sup> Nicholas Downton to be Leiuetenant of the said Shipps and men and to take his passadge in the Pepꝑ Corne. &amp; Beniamyn Greene to be cheefe Marchaunt in the Little Darlinge as alsoe o<sup>r</sup> good freind m<sup>r</sup> Lawrence ffemell o<sup>r</sup> cheefe Agent for o<sup>r</sup> busines at Suratt in the East Indies  And Mathewe Mollenoux to be Pilott maio<sup>r</sup> for this voyadge, whome we appointe to take passadge in the Trades Increase.  All w<sup>ch</sup> Comanders Marchauntę ffactor<sup>s</sup> M<sup>rs</sup>: &amp; Mariners we trust will soe carry them selues w<sup>th</sup> due respect one to another &amp; obedience to eu<sup>r</sup>y one of their Superior<sup>s</sup> as loue and kindnes may be Contynued on all sides &amp; all wholie apply their wittę and endevo<sup>rs</sup> for the due ꝑformance of this voyadge in the best manner they may for the good of the Company.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">3.  Item: And for that religious governm<sup>t</sup> doth best binde men to p̄forme their duties yt is principally to be cared for, that prayers be said eu<sup>r</sup>y morneinge &amp; eveninge in eu<sup>r</sup>y Shipp &amp; the whole Company called therevnto w<sup>th</sup> diligent Eyes that none be wantinge soe as all may ioyntlie w<sup>th</sup> reverence &amp; humility pray vnto Almightie God to blesse and preserue them from all dangers in this longe and tedious voyadge.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">4  Item That noe blasphemeinge of God, Swearinge, Thefte, Drunkennes, or otherlyke disorders be vsed but that the same be severelie ponished, &amp; that noe diceinge or other vnlawfull games <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-422" data-page="422" data-folio="330" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n421_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 330">[p. 330]</button> be admitted for that most comonlie the same is the begyninge of quarrellinge and many tymes occasions of murthers a iust provokac̃on of Gode wrath &amp; vengeance (from w<sup>ch</sup> the Lord deliu<sup>r</sup> vs all) therefore yt shall be needfull that such orders be sett vp and published in eu<sup>r</sup>y Shipp &amp; such ponishm<sup>t</sup> appointed for such Offences as may giue notice to eu<sup>r</sup>y man what he ought to avoied.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">5 Item that spetiall order be giuen that noe liquor be spilt in the ballast of the Shipps or filthines be lefte w<sup>th</sup>in bourde w<sup>ch</sup> in heate breadeth noysome smells &amp; infec̃cõn but that there be a diligent care to keepe the overlopps &amp; other places of the Shipps cleane &amp; sweete w<sup>ch</sup> is a notable preseruãcõn of health wherein thofficers of o<sup>r</sup> Shipps in former voyadge haue beene much to be blamed, to the greate spoile of o<sup>r</sup> Shipps &amp; breedinge of sicknes w<sup>ch</sup> we trust in this will be more carefully reguarded.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">6 Item that the Generall Leiuetenante &amp; m<sup>rs</sup>: of the Shipps doe take due notice by Inventory from eu<sup>r</sup>y Officer of the Shipps before they depte out of the riuer of Thames of the seu<sup>r</sup>all puisions deliu<sup>r</sup>ed into each Shipp and soe soone as they shall come to Sea &amp; not before to settle the same in the chardge of the Officers appointed for that purpose in each Shipp vntill w<sup>ch</sup> tyme all stores are to remaine vnder locke and key and that the same be entred in the Pursars booke vnderwritten by the said Officers to thend that such thinge as are prouided for their needfull supplies may not be ymbecilled &amp; that nothinge thereof of any valewe be ymployed for any vse whatsoeu<sup>r</sup> w<sup>th</sup>out a warrant vnder the hand of the Generall, Leiuetenante or cheefe Marchant of each Shipp respectiuely directed to the Officer that hath such chardge w<sup>ch</sup> warrant shall serue for their dischardge in their accompte ells otherwyse to be awnswerable for the same w<sup>th</sup> their wages: And eu<sup>r</sup>y such warrant to be registred in the purcers booke, the day of the monneth &amp; thoccãcõn &amp; this vpon forfecture of soe much out of the Purcers wages this order to be obserued in each Shipp as aforesaid, that at the retourne of the said Shipps a true accompt may be yealded howe and in what manner the same haue beene vsed and ymployed.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">7 Item That the Generall &amp; m<sup>r</sup> of the Shipp doe sett downe &amp; deliver such instrucc̃õns vnto the Comanders of thother Shipps that they may keepe company together for the better releife and <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-423" data-page="423" data-folio="331" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n422_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 331">[p. 331]</button> comforte one of an other &amp; yf seperac̃on of them should happen by Constrainte of weather or tempest that they shall agree of a course beforehand &amp; of a place whether to apply them selues to finde the rest.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">8. Item That contynuall &amp; true Iournalls be kept of eu<sup>r</sup>y dayes Nauigac̃on duringe the whole voyadge, w<sup>th</sup> a true relac̃on of eu<sup>r</sup>y materiall thinge that passeth &amp; this not onlie to be done by the Leiuetenant but alsoe by the Marchauntę pursar Pilotte<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-035" href="#sec-08-fn-035" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-035" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> M<sup>r</sup> &amp; M<sup>rs</sup> matę &amp; that some of the principalls in each Shipp may conferr their obseruac̃ons together at convenient tymes once or twyce eu<sup>r</sup>y weeke at the least to the end yf any haue forgotten what an other hath obserued the same may be added, soe as a p̄fect discourse<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-036" href="#sec-08-fn-036" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-036" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> may be sett downe to be presented to the Gouerno<sup>r</sup> &amp; Company when God shall grante them a safe retourne to be kept for better direcc̃on of posterytie.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">9. Item we Comend vnto yo<sup>u</sup> the Generall principally &amp; to the rest of the Comanders and masters of a speciall care, that at eu<sup>r</sup>y place where yo<sup>u</sup> shall water &amp; refresh yo<sup>r</sup> menn yo<sup>u</sup> call the Companies together geueing them expresse warninge to behaue themselues peaceably &amp; ciuilly towardę the people of those places the better to procure their freindshipp towardę the supply of yo<sup>r</sup> wantę, &amp; the lyke in eu<sup>r</sup>y place where yo<sup>u</sup> come least the losse of yo<sup>r</sup> liues &amp; ou<sup>r</sup>throwe of this voyadge pay for their disorders besidę an vtter discreditt to o<sup>r</sup> nac̃on.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">10 Item And that yo<sup>r</sup> men doe vse a discreete meane in eatinge of fruitę &amp; fresh victualls least they surfett &amp; fall into diseases whereof we haue had too much experience And the better to refraine vnruly appetitę we hould yt convenient that the Generall appointe some of the discreetest in the Companies to pcure &amp; prouide in such places what yo<sup>u</sup> want &amp; the same beinge prouided &amp; brought to the Generall, by him to be reparted to each Company what may be needfull &amp; that none presume to doe the Contrary w<sup>th</sup>out seveare ponishm<sup>t</sup>. And that espetiall care be had to releiue the Sicke w<sup>th</sup> such fresh meatę &amp; other comfortable thingę wherew<sup>th</sup> we haue furnished each Shipp for that purpose &amp; not otherwyse to be spent in Ryott &amp; banquettinge &amp; soe the sicke pishe for want of thingę needfull.</p></div><div class="page-rail-marker" id="page-424"><button type="button" class="folio-trigger" data-page="424" data-folio="332" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n423_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect scan for page 332">[p. 332]</button></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">11 And whereas the straglinge of yo<sup>r</sup> men in any place where they shall arriue or come, may not onlie proue the losse of such as shall soe straggle notw<sup>th</sup>standinge they should be armed but alsoe thereby much hurte might happen to the residue of o<sup>r</sup> people. Yt is ordered that yo<sup>u</sup> doe not pmitt any man in any place to straggle or goe from the rest of yo<sup>r</sup> Companies vnles vpon espetiall occasion yo<sup>u</sup> giue warrant therevnto vpon paine of seveare ponishm<sup>t</sup> to be inflicted vpon such as shall doe to the Contrary still doubtinge the worste &amp; standinge vpon yo<sup>r</sup> guard w<sup>th</sup>out giueinge any trust to the people of the place.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">12 Item yt is expreslie ordered that by a generall consent of yo<sup>u</sup> the Generall Leiuetenante Marchauntę ffactors<sup>rs</sup> Masters &amp; Mariners some good order be by yo<sup>u</sup> taken and sett downe in writeinge soe soone as yo<sup>u</sup> come to Sea conc<sup>r</sup>ninge the disposeinge of dead mens goodę in the voyadge, either by comittinge the same by Inventory to the pticuler keepinge of some men in the Shipps &amp; this vpon some convenient consideračõn for keepinge the same or by sale of soe much thereof at the maine maste as may not exceede a third parte of the buyers wages for either buyinge or borroweinge to prevent such preiudice to o<sup>r</sup> Company as we haue felte, by Mariners buyinge soe much as their whole wages came vnto whereby they haue not onlie forsaken o<sup>r</sup> shipps before their dischardge but alsoe depriued vs of meanes to redresse such wrogę as they haue many waies done vs in the voyadge. And to order what sallarie shall be geven the Pursar for keepinge accompt thereof to avoied question &amp; controuersie heereafter Alsoe that there be noe dealeinge by Exchãdge betwixt pty &amp; pty neither money for money Comoditee for money, or Comodity for Comodity neither outwardę nor homewardę for such Exchandge giueth way for them to bringe more then they should or take &amp; purloyne of o<sup>r</sup> goodę they ought not, for prevenčõn whereof wee order that neither the Pursar or any other doe register witnes or take knowledge of any such Contracte by Exchandge. And noe man to be admitted meanes to bringe goodę aboard in any the Shipps more then their Chest appointed them will Containe &amp; the same goodę to be pticularly entred into the Pursers bookę vpon paine of forfecture to the Company: that w<sup>ch</sup> shall not be soe entred eu<sup>r</sup>y Mariner and <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-425" data-page="425" data-folio="333" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n424_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 333">[p. 333]</button> other of the said Shipps to sett their hande or marke to the said orders mentioned in this 12<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-037" href="#sec-08-fn-037" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-037" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> article. And yf any ffactor<sup>r</sup> shall happen to die, his goode Booke accompte and other thinge are to be ordered<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-038" href="#sec-08-fn-038" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-038" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> of the Generall &amp; Leiuetenante safely reserued &amp; brought come for England, an Inventory first taken thereof &amp; registred in the purcers booke, to be witnessed by 2 or 3 other of the Marchaunte.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">13 Item for better preseruac̃on of health o<sup>r</sup> order is that from tyme to tyme as the water Caske shall be emptied of fresh water yt be forthw<sup>th</sup> againe filled w<sup>th</sup> salt water for prevenc̃on of leakeinge<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-039" href="#sec-08-fn-039" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-039" role="doc-noteref">3</a></sup> and mustines.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">14 Item In yo<sup>r</sup> passadge betweene the Island of S<sup>t</sup> Lawrence &amp; the Coaste of Sophola<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-040" href="#sec-08-fn-040" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-040" role="doc-noteref">4</a></sup> beware of the flatte of Iudea<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-041" href="#sec-08-fn-041" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-041" role="doc-noteref">5</a></sup> vpon w<sup>ch</sup> flatte it is said that the Currant doth strongly run, whereby many shipps haue pished, therefore w<sup>th</sup> greate care to be avoieded.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p"><sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-042" href="#sec-08-fn-042" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-042" role="doc-noteref">6</a></sup>Item for the fittest place for refreshinge whether at Saldania or at the Bay of S<sup>t</sup> Augustine in S<sup>t</sup> Lawrence Island we referr the same to yo<sup>r</sup> discrec̃on to doe therein as occasion may vrdge still fixinge yo<sup>r</sup> thoughte &amp; resoluc̃on to arriue at the Barr of Suratt by thend of September next accordinge to yo<sup>r</sup> designes w<sup>ch</sup> is the maine and principall scope of this o<sup>r</sup> voyadge w<sup>th</sup> this further aduise that wheresoeu<sup>r</sup> yo<sup>u</sup> shall water yo<sup>u</sup> make noe longer stay then necessetie shall inforce, to thend yo<sup>u</sup> may haue some oportunity for freshm<sup>t</sup> at Succatora there to sett vp o<sup>r</sup><sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-043" href="#sec-08-fn-043" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-043" role="doc-noteref">7</a></sup> Pynnasse and buy some Alloes Sacatrina to the quantity of 2 or 3 Tonns, where we pray yo<sup>u</sup> to take notice that in the third voyadge W<sup>m</sup>: Kealeing bought there twenty fower hundred waight at 20 Ryalls of 8 the hundred pound w<sup>ch</sup> is 104<sup>li</sup> of o<sup>rs</sup>.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">15 Item wee desier that<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-044" href="#sec-08-fn-044" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-044" role="doc-noteref">8</a></sup> rather yo<sup>u</sup> touch at Succatora for that we hope yo<sup>u</sup> shall meete w<sup>th</sup> some Guzaratte<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-045" href="#sec-08-fn-045" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-045" role="doc-noteref">9</a></sup> at that place from whome yo<sup>u</sup> may receiue some Intelligence whither W<sup>m</sup>: <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-426" data-page="426" data-folio="334" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n425_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 334">[p. 334]</button> Hawkins and the rest of o<sup>r</sup> ffacto<sup>rs</sup> lefte at Suratte out of the Hecto<sup>r</sup> are liueinge or not, and in what state o<sup>r</sup> busines there standeth whereby yo<sup>u</sup> may the better there gouerne yo<sup>r</sup> selues for pformance of o<sup>r</sup> desiers, But yf the tyme of the Monson be soe farr spent that yo<sup>u</sup> cannot arriue at the Barr of Suratt by thend of Septemb̃ or middle of October next at the farthest, then we will yo<sup>u</sup> to take the first oportunytie eyther to sayle to Aden or Mocha alias Mochow<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-046" href="#sec-08-fn-046" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-046" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> w<sup>th</sup>in the mouth of the red Sea aboute some ten leagues distant from Aden as we vnderstand, Att w<sup>ch</sup> porte of Mocha we haue heard the Shipps of the fourth voyadge haue traded. And where we doubt not, yo<sup>u</sup> may sell Cloth, lead, Iron, Tynn, Quicksylver &amp; some red caps for money or gould, for that wee thinke Comodities will be too deare in that place, Consideringe o<sup>r</sup> purpose that yo<sup>u</sup> goe to Suratt except yo<sup>u</sup> may buy Cloues maces or nutmegg<em>e</em> at a reasonable rate w<sup>ch</sup> are said to be good Comodities at Suratt as well as here in England In this place yo<sup>u</sup> must spend the tyme till oportunity doe serue to goe for Suratt w<sup>ch</sup> will not be till toward<em>e</em> the end of August followinge, soe as yo<sup>u</sup> may arriue there by the end of Septemb̃ w<sup>ch</sup> we pray yo<sup>u</sup> to endevo<sup>r</sup> by all possible meanes, But stayinge at Aden or Mocha haue espetiall care that yo<sup>r</sup> men giue noe offence to the people there, still keepinge good watch and standinge vpon yo<sup>r</sup> guard &amp; that out of any danger of any Castle, fforte, or Gallies to prevent mischeefe</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">16 Item being arriued at Suratt we desier yo<sup>u</sup> to vse the best meanes yo<sup>u</sup> can p<sup>r</sup>sently to vnderstand of the state of W<sup>m</sup>: Hawkins &amp; the rest of o<sup>r</sup> people there and heareinge of their saffeties either to send vp yo<sup>r</sup> longe boate w<sup>th</sup> yo<sup>r</sup> Indian Iohn or some other whome yo<sup>u</sup> thinke more fitt, or finde some meanes to cause some of the ffacto<sup>rs</sup> come abourd yo<sup>u</sup>, either the said m<sup>r</sup> Hawkins: W<sup>m</sup>: ffinche, Iohn Dorchester or any other of o<sup>r</sup> ffacto<sup>rs</sup> there, of whome take due informac̃on of the state of the Country, howe they haue beene accepted of by the kinge, howe entreated by the people what priuiledges they have gott, what Comodities of o<sup>rs</sup> are there requested, what they haue of that Country Comodities vpon their hand<em>e</em> to be laden acquaintinge <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-427" data-page="427" data-folio="335" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n426_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 335">[p. 335]</button> them alsoe what Comodities yo<sup>u</sup> haue brought and the quantities and takeinge knowledge of their worth there as alsoe what Comodities are there presentlie to be had and at what prices aswell fittinge for England as Priaman Bantam Banda &amp; the Molloccos &amp; whether the same may be had w<sup>th</sup> such speede as yo<sup>u</sup><sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-047" href="#sec-08-fn-047" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-047" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> may take hould of the Monson to goe to those p<sup>te</sup>. What forces the Portugalls<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-048" href="#sec-08-fn-048" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-048" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> haue howe ready the Guzaratte are to helpe o<sup>r</sup> nation &amp; of all other needfull intelligences, by whome yf yo<sup>u</sup> be incourradged we wish yo<sup>u</sup> to send in the Darlynge and in her m<sup>r</sup> Lawrence ffemell o<sup>r</sup> principall Agent w<sup>th</sup> such Comodities as may be most vendible w<sup>th</sup>all speede geueing them warneing to be ready to w<sup>th</sup>stand any Enimies and keepe good watch to prevent any mischeefe of fyre or otherwyse w<sup>ch</sup> lett them be sure the Portugalls will practize against them.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">17   Item for that we haue prouided (as yo<sup>u</sup> knowe) and laden in theis shipps sundry Comodities of good valew principally for that place as Leade, Tynn, Elaphaunte teeth, Quicksylver, sword blade vermillion red lead Cloth kersies lookinge glasses and some red caps accordinge to an Invoice<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-049" href="#sec-08-fn-049" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-049" role="doc-noteref">3</a></sup> thereof herew<sup>th</sup> deliu<sup>r</sup>ed yo<sup>u</sup> wee hould yt convenient that the same be laden in such frigatte as may be prouided for that purpose and the same sent vp to Suratt w<sup>th</sup>all speede wafted w<sup>th</sup> the Pepp Corne yf yo<sup>u</sup> finde Incouradgm<sup>t</sup> as aforesaid. And for better pformance of what is to be done before o<sup>r</sup> Agent goe vp to Suratt, we pray yo<sup>u</sup> and him to Consult &amp; take good aduise w<sup>th</sup> the rest for the deliu<sup>r</sup>y of his ma<sup>te</sup> lres now sent. what presente are to be deliu<sup>r</sup>ed, to whome, by whome, &amp; in what manner the same to be done w<sup>th</sup> that speede &amp; dilligence that shall be requisite Consideringe the shortnes of the tyme in keepinge the Monson for obtayneinge yo<sup>r</sup> other porte, ffor w<sup>ch</sup> presente we haue sent in the Trades Increase 13 peece of velvette of diu<sup>r</sup>s Colors<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-050" href="#sec-08-fn-050" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-050" role="doc-noteref">4</a></sup> as appeth by the Invoice out of w<sup>ch</sup> yo<sup>u</sup> are to take for quantity &amp; Colo<sup>r</sup> what shall be <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-428" data-page="428" data-folio="336" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n427_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 336">[p. 336]</button> thought needfull for that purpose the rest to be sould for o<sup>r</sup> Companies benefitt Alsoe we haue sent (as yo<sup>u</sup> knowe[]) in the same Shipp 228 <sup>8</sup>/<sub>20</sub><sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-051" href="#sec-08-fn-051" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-051" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> ounces of guilte plate out of w<sup>ch</sup> yf yo<sup>u</sup> thinke requisite some alsoe may be geven for presente accordinge to aduise of m<sup>r</sup> Hawkins &amp; the rest, yf any thinge be presented to the kinge, Prince or greate Potentate, yt is to be done (as yo<sup>u</sup> knowe) w<sup>th</sup> state and Comlines, for presente for meaner psonns we wish yo<sup>u</sup> to chuse out amonge o<sup>r</sup> Clothes such Cullers as may be acceptable vnto them for veste for better pformeinge of this busines &amp; accompanyinge o<sup>r</sup> cheefe Agent, we haue appointed Iohn ² ffowler Hughe ³ ffraine Beniaymyn ⁴ Greene Thomas ⁵ Glenham Rowland ⁶ Webb Roƀt ⁷ Larkyn Iohn ⁸ Williams ffrancis ⁹ Slany &amp; Iohn ¹⁰ Stoughton<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-052" href="#sec-08-fn-052" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-052" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> to remaine there as ffactors<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-053" href="#sec-08-fn-053" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-053" role="doc-noteref">3</a></sup> yf yt shall be thought fitt to leaue there soe many as the occasions in yo<sup>r</sup> discrec̃ons may be serued and W<sup>m</sup> Powlter &amp; W<sup>m</sup> Reynolde for his attendaunte.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">18 Item yf o<sup>r</sup> former ffactors haue not procured a convenient howse to lodge all o<sup>r</sup> people &amp; Containe all o<sup>r</sup> goode wee wish o<sup>r</sup> cheefe Agent to Conferr w<sup>th</sup> them how they may obtaine a larger and such a one as may be stronge, and of defence soe as both they and o<sup>r</sup> goode may remaine in saffety</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">19 Item the busines aforesaid beinge done, Considerac̃on is to be had for the ladinge and dispatch of o<sup>r</sup> Shipps and whether yt will be requisite to make sale of o<sup>r</sup> goode at the then currant prices for the pformance of the said ladinge, or whether yt will be better to expect higher prices, by keepinge the same till fitter oportunitie remembringe that what prices shall now be sett of o<sup>r</sup> goode will be a president for heereafter &amp; therefore needfull<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-054" href="#sec-08-fn-054" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-054" role="doc-noteref">4</a></sup> to hould o<sup>r</sup> goode in spetiall reputac̃on, for m<sup>r</sup>chandize sodenlie sould doe comonlie giue small aduancment in price, therefore in this pointe yo<sup>u</sup> are to be well aduized, yf then yo<sup>u</sup> shall hould yt more beneficiall to keep o<sup>r</sup> goode for better m<sup>r</sup>kette yo<sup>u</sup> may out <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-429" data-page="429" data-folio="337" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n428_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 337">[p. 337]</button> of such moneys w<sup>ch</sup> we haue now sent (w<sup>ch</sup> are alsoe mentioned in o<sup>r</sup><sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-055" href="#sec-08-fn-055" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-055" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> Invoice) prouide such thinge as yo<sup>u</sup> thinke necessary to be retourned for England w<sup>ch</sup> together w<sup>th</sup> the goode remaineinge vpon yo<sup>r</sup> hande formerly bought, wee wish yo<sup>u</sup> to lade aboard the Pepp Corne and the Darlynge yf sufficient to loade them both may there be had, or but one of them yf yo<sup>u</sup> shall finde reason &amp; w<sup>th</sup>out preiudice to o<sup>r</sup> trade wherein obserue this as agenerall order that the goode of eu<sup>r</sup>y voyadge may be m<sup>r</sup>ked and nombred seu<sup>r</sup>ally and soe expressed in yo<sup>r</sup> Cargasons &amp; accompte that the goode of each voyadge may be knowne by them selues for that they doe not Concerne the selfe same aduenturers. And hereof therefore yo<sup>u</sup> are to be very carefull to prevent wronge in ladinge of goode wee note theis aspetiall Comodities w<sup>ch</sup> we desier Viz: Indico, of the very best aswell flatt as round soe muchas yo<sup>u</sup> may (w<sup>th</sup> o<sup>r</sup> moneys) well Compasse, other Comodities being alsoe to be had but lett yo<sup>r</sup> care be extraordinary to buy the best that may be had for money aswell Indico as of all other Comodities. Callicoes white tasselled booke, good quantity &amp; some of the courser sorte. Callicoes light colored alsoe as carnačõn light watchette strawe Cullers &amp; other<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-056" href="#sec-08-fn-056" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-056" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> light Colo<sup>rs</sup> but none of them course or sad cullers in any manner in theis yo<sup>u</sup> may ymploy to the valewe of twoe thousand Rialls of eight. Shashes<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-057" href="#sec-08-fn-057" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-057" role="doc-noteref">3</a></sup> of all sorte for a thowsand Rialls of 8. Synamon of Zelan the best that can be gotten well packed vp in skynns <em>twenty Tonns</em><sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-058" href="#sec-08-fn-058" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-058" role="doc-noteref">4</a></sup> but of noe other sorte neither Scaveson nor Cowchin. Cotton yerne of the finest 20 <em>Tonns</em>. Cotton yearne somewhat courser <em>aboute fyue tonns</em>. Greene Ginger of the best and largest such as that of Battecala 4 <em>Tonns</em> red Saunders wood <em>aboute</em> 3 Tonns. Turbith 2 <em>Tonns</em>. Opium <em>fiue hundred pounde weight</em>. Beniamyn 10 <em>Tonns</em>.<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-059" href="#sec-08-fn-059" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-059" role="doc-noteref">5</a></sup> Sall Armoniake 2 <em>Tons</em> Olibanū <em>yf good</em> 10 <em>Tonns</em>. Lignum Alloes as much as yo<sup>u</sup> can gett. Worme seede clensed as much as possiblie may be from the Chaffe &amp; dust by sifteinge &amp; winnoinge for that the dust and chaffe is here worth nothinge, <em>to the quantity</em> <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-430" data-page="430" data-folio="338" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n429_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 338">[p. 338]</button> of <em>fower</em><sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-060" href="#sec-08-fn-060" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-060" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> <em>hundred kintolls</em>. Gum lack: as much as yo<sup>u</sup> can gett of the best &amp; second sorte but none of the worst of any hande Sugers of the best some <em>twenty cheste</em> for a triall. Silke of persia yf to be had agood quantity. Theis Comodities being bought &amp; packed w<sup>th</sup> m<sup>r</sup>chantlike care to prevent damadge m<sup>r</sup>ked &amp; nombred accordinge to former direcõon both of the inside and outside for better satisfacõon of the Company in the difference of the sorte and contente, the rather for that the outmost m<sup>r</sup>ke may be by longe lying at Sea worne out, the same to be laden &amp; the shipps to be dispatched away w<sup>th</sup>all expediõon for England.<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-061" href="#sec-08-fn-061" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-061" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> And now as a first busines in dispatch to be remembred we wish yo<sup>u</sup> to prouide such Callicoes and other Comodities as may be fittinge for Pryaman, Bantam, Banda, &amp; the Molloccoes &amp; the same being orderly packed to be shipped w<sup>th</sup>all speede in the Trades Increase for those places w<sup>ch</sup> w<sup>th</sup> <em>fifty</em> thowsand rialls of eight that we wish yo<sup>u</sup> to receiue for<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-062" href="#sec-08-fn-062" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-062" role="doc-noteref">3</a></sup> ymploym<sup>t</sup> at Suratt as alsoe the Iron we now send <em>Twenty</em> tonns of lead in cheste, <em>three</em> bales w<sup>th</sup> Cloth<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-063" href="#sec-08-fn-063" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-063" role="doc-noteref">4</a></sup> prouided for Bantam the said shipp to take her first oportunity to sayle for Pryaman, together w<sup>th</sup> that shipp of the other twoe not dispatched for England.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">20 Item beinge arriued at Priaman to make there sale of soe much of yo<sup>r</sup> Iron, Callicoes, &amp; Pintadoes as yo<sup>u</sup> shall finde fittinge, soe much for gould as yo<sup>u</sup> may &amp; the rest for large pepper wherein haue a spetiall care to buy none greene for breath in<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-064" href="#sec-08-fn-064" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-064" role="doc-noteref">5</a></sup> waight but such as shall be dry and cleane<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-065" href="#sec-08-fn-065" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-065" role="doc-noteref">6</a></sup> from dust to the quantety of fower hundreth Tonns or thereaboute &amp; some ten tonns of Beniamyn theis Comodities to be weighed by yo<sup>r</sup> owne waighte into the Shipp &amp; the Purser to keepe due notice of the same with such of the ffactors as shall be appointed for that purpose that they may be able to giue vs agood accompt thereof, In execuõon of this busines take this for aduise, that yo<sup>u</sup> make shewe to be gone from thence w<sup>th</sup> a purpose to buy noe pepp, for that when they pceaue yo<sup>u</sup> purpose to depte they will offer yo<sup>u</sup> pepp at much lower rate than otherwyse, therefore yt is fitt to seeme vnwilling <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-431" data-page="431" data-folio="339" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n430_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 339">[p. 339]</button> to buy and some tymes to make refusall will not be amisse, beside make shewe that yo<sup>u</sup> haue but few Callicoes or other Cōmodities to sell, the better to giue them edge to buy, In the pformance of this busines remember still yo<sup>r</sup> purpose to goe for Bantam and Banda that yo<sup>u</sup> slip not the oportunity of the Monson, And soe w<sup>th</sup> what yo<sup>u</sup> haue there taken in and restinge in the Shipp for Bantam there dischardge both Iron, leade, Callicoes &amp; Cloth &amp;c: prepared for that place for a stocke to prouide Lamkin silke of the finest &amp; whitest (but noe Canton<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-066" href="#sec-08-fn-066" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-066" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> silke at any hand) w<sup>th</sup> what other thinge yo<sup>u</sup> shall hould needfull against yo<sup>r</sup> retourne from Banda whether we wish yo<sup>u</sup> to make all possible haste takeinge w<sup>th</sup> yo<sup>u</sup> such facto<sup>rs</sup> to leaue there as in yo<sup>r</sup> iudgm<sup>t</sup> shall be thought meete, and for stocke of the fyfty thowsand rialle before mentioned what yo<sup>u</sup> thinke requisite and such China stuffe and dishes as yo<sup>u</sup> may learne at Bantam to be fittinge for the place.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">21 Item being arriued at Banda present such guifte to the Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> or Savander<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-067" href="#sec-08-fn-067" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-067" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> there as in yo<sup>r</sup> discrecōn shall seeme fittinge and there prouide three hundreth Tonns of Nutmegge of the best and soundest that may be gotten, freed from dust and rumps all that may be, remembringe that the same will not be worth the freight &amp; chardge Alsoe 20 Tons of Maces the lardgest and brightest that may be gotten but none that is darke Colored red maces, w<sup>ch</sup> are called femenyn maces &amp; here little worth, for accomplishinge hereof as alsoe for to leaue stocke convenient there for puiicon for a future voyadge, keepe in yo<sup>r</sup> shipp soe much money &amp; Callicoes as yo<sup>u</sup> may deeme sufficient. And yf being arriued at Banda yo<sup>u</sup> hold it possible &amp; find yt convenient to goe to the Maluccos whilest the Nutmegge &amp; maces are puideinge, to seeke for Cloues, and haueing gotten to yo<sup>r</sup> content therein, to retourne to Banda there to take in the mace &amp; nutmegge &amp; w<sup>th</sup> speede to retourne to Bantam leaueing a Convenient nomber of ffacto<sup>rs</sup> &amp; stocke as shall be needfull at Banda as aforesaid. And beinge retorned to Bantam &amp; haueinge brought atrue pticular of what is lefte at Banda vnder the hande of the ffacto<sup>rs</sup> there, then to take in such silke and other thinge as shall in yo<sup>r</sup> absence be prouided there for London, where haueing setled the busines &amp; <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-432" data-page="432" data-folio="340" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n431_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 340">[p. 340]</button> factors in good order &amp; taken a pticular relacõn of o<sup>r</sup> accompte and Comodities there lefte, the Coppies whereof to be signed by o<sup>r</sup> ffactors &amp; to be brought alonge w<sup>th</sup> you, and order geven by you to keepe accompt of eu<sup>r</sup>y voyadge by yt selfe &amp; soe of the retornes in lyke manner that the Adventurers vpon eu<sup>r</sup>y voyadge may haue their right as they Consist of diu<sup>r</sup>sity of Adventurers, then in Gode name w<sup>th</sup> all due speede to retorne for England.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">And haueing thus farr pceeded in a direct course we hould yt fitt to looke backe to the Barr at Suratt and there to Consider yf thinge fall out contrary to expectacõn that neither o<sup>r</sup> people should be there in saffetie nor the place fitt for o<sup>r</sup> trade ne yett incouradgm<sup>t</sup> to pursue the same any farther In reguard of the Comodities prouided fitt for the seruice of that place &amp; Aden &amp; not elswhere w<sup>th</sup>in o<sup>r</sup> knowledge o<sup>r</sup> expresse order is that vpon notice of the premisses you forthw<sup>th</sup> w<sup>th</sup> the Monson retourne for Aden or Mocha in the red Sea yf in yo<sup>r</sup> passadge to Suratt you haue not visited the same &amp; done what might be done for the vent of o<sup>r</sup> Comodities &amp; there to try for dispatch &amp; vent of o<sup>r</sup> said Marchandize w<sup>ch</sup> beinge effected according to o<sup>r</sup> desires, yf possiblie you may be fitted by the Iuncke arriueing there from diu<sup>r</sup>s porte out of the Indies w<sup>th</sup> m<sup>r</sup>chandize fitting for England,<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-068" href="#sec-08-fn-068" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-068" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> to lade either one twoe or all of o<sup>r</sup> Shipps w<sup>th</sup> the same goode brought thether as namelie smooth pepp, drugge of all sorte fitting for this place, Indico of the best sorte, Nutmegge, Maces, Synamond Callicoes Beniamyn, Olibanum, Mirr, Opyum, red Sanders lignum Alloes and such like &amp; the Ship or shipps soe laden w<sup>th</sup> speede to direct their course for England, And yf any of the Shipps remaine vnladen, then w<sup>th</sup> such Callicoes as you can buy of the Iuncke fittinge for Pryaman Bantam or Banda or the Molloccos to saile directlie for Pryaman and there to pursue trade &amp; from thence further as aforesaid as in yo<sup>r</sup> discrecõn shall seeme best.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">22 Item we thinke yt not amisse further to aduise you that findinge faile of trade at Suratt you endevo<sup>r</sup> whilest you are there to inuite the Guzaratte w<sup>th</sup> such Comodities as you looke for to repaier to the porte of Delisha or Tamarie at the Iland of Socotara where you may agree to deale w<sup>th</sup> them for the same, <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-433" data-page="433" data-folio="341" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n432_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 341">[p. 341]</button> w<sup>ch</sup> yf yo<sup>u</sup> doe y<sup>u</sup> &amp; they alsoe shall saue much Customes yo<sup>u</sup> shall pay in other porte to the Governo<sup>rs</sup> thereof, yt is to be vnderstoode yf noe trade be<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-069" href="#sec-08-fn-069" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-069" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> to be had at Suratt there shall be noe cause to settle any ffactory there, In such case we appointe m<sup>r</sup> Lawrence ffemell &amp; the rest of o<sup>r</sup> ffacto<sup>rs</sup> now sent to proceede w<sup>th</sup> yo<sup>u</sup> to Aden or Mocha or to such other place as yo<sup>u</sup> shall thinke fitt to direct yo<sup>r</sup> course for benefitt of trade &amp; findinge any such place as may be beneficiall to o<sup>r</sup> Company aswell for vent of Cloth and other o<sup>r</sup> English m<sup>r</sup>chandizes as for Comodities of those Countries fittinge for this wee referr to yo<sup>r</sup> discrec̄on the setlinge of o<sup>r</sup> ffactory there and the appointem<sup>t</sup> of any of o<sup>r</sup> ffacto<sup>rs</sup> to reside there w<sup>th</sup> such stocke as in yo<sup>r</sup> iudgment may seeme Competent to defray their chardge &amp; make puision of Comodities w<sup>ch</sup> that Country may yeald against the arriuall of a second supply And in that place m<sup>r</sup> ffemell to be o<sup>r</sup> principall Agent But findinge noe such place &amp; proceeding to Bantam o<sup>r</sup> order is that the ymploym<sup>t</sup> of o<sup>r</sup> Shipps for p̄formance of their ladeinge be at yo<sup>r</sup> <em>direcc̄on</em>, and that M<sup>r</sup> ffemell shall there be lefte cheefe Agent and soe many of the rest of o<sup>r</sup> ffacto<sup>rs</sup> now sent as yo<sup>u</sup> and he shall deeme fitt, the rest to be placed at Banda &amp; all o<sup>r</sup> ould ffacto<sup>rs</sup> to be brought home w<sup>th</sup> yo<sup>u</sup> from thence excepting Augustine Spauldinge and twoe more such as haue that languadge the out<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-070" href="#sec-08-fn-070" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-070" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> ould ffacto<sup>rs</sup><sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-070-2" href="#sec-08-fn-070" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-070" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> to deliu<sup>r</sup> vp their chardge by accompte &amp; Inventory to o<sup>r</sup> said Agent &amp; to bringe home a Coppy of the same to be deliu<sup>r</sup>ed to the Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> and Comittees of the Company then beinge.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">23 Item we ordaine that such of the ffacto<sup>rs</sup> as shall remaine in the ships at the buyinge of any goode shall be ymployed to weigh the same, pay the money, and keepe due accompt thereof &amp; that nothinge be done either in buyinge or sellinge of goode w<sup>th</sup>out their priuity that they may write the same into their booke &amp; be abell to giue vs reason for what is bought or sould either by writeing or at their retourne for England, and that they shall acquaynte the Generall &amp; cheife Comander in eu<sup>r</sup>y shipp eu<sup>r</sup>y night w<sup>th</sup> what they haue done that day &amp; that the Pursars booke &amp; theirs shall then be compared together, soe as yt may appeare whether all that hath beene bought hath beene receiued <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-434" data-page="434" data-folio="342" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n433_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 342">[p. 342]</button> into the Shipp &amp; there weighed aswell as on the Shore. notice to be geven<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-071" href="#sec-08-fn-071" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-071" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> what is wantinge &amp; enquiry<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-072" href="#sec-08-fn-072" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-072" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> what is become thereof &amp; Coppies of eu<sup>r</sup>y of the said accompte to be sent home by Sondry Shipps, &amp; the same accompte for what hath beene then done at Pryaman, Bantam, Banda, &amp; the Molloccoes be alsoe registred in o<sup>r</sup> booke at Bantam.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">24 Item that all thinge bought and sould in eu<sup>r</sup>y place by<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-073" href="#sec-08-fn-073" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-073" role="doc-noteref">3</a></sup> by some of o<sup>r</sup> ffactors for that purpose residinge there faier written in o<sup>r</sup> booke w<sup>ch</sup> we now send signed by the Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> &amp; Comittees, w<sup>ch</sup> booke we order to be deliu<sup>r</sup>ed one for o<sup>r</sup> ffactors at Suratt, one for the ffactors at Banda, one for the ffactors at Bantam, and thother for them at Pryaman or ells where where ffactors shall recide for vs, And the same booke there in the seu<sup>r</sup>all places to be kept as recorde of those that shall succeede and that out of the same we may haue Coppies of what wee shall requier for the Companies better satisfaccõn. Alsoe that none of o<sup>r</sup> ffactors whomsoeu<sup>r</sup> be suffered to bringe from thence any booke of accompte either passed by him selfe or by any others, other then Coppies for him selfe or for vs subscribed by him that shall succeede in that chardge and twoe more of o<sup>r</sup> ffactors there the said Coppies soe sent to be sealed vpp. And in theis yo<sup>r</sup> passadges and traffiques o<sup>r</sup> desier is that yo<sup>u</sup> should informe yo<sup>r</sup> selfe of safe harbors and places of repaier for the vent of o<sup>r</sup> English Comodities either aboute the porte of <em>Sabull</em><sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-074" href="#sec-08-fn-074" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-074" role="doc-noteref">4</a></sup> nere Suratt or the riuer Syndus &amp; Laree,<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-075" href="#sec-08-fn-075" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-075" role="doc-noteref">5</a></sup> or the Cape of <em>Rosalgat</em><sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-076" href="#sec-08-fn-076" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-076" role="doc-noteref">6</a></sup> or any Coste of the porte<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-077" href="#sec-08-fn-077" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-077" role="doc-noteref">7</a></sup> of Arabia where yo<sup>u</sup> may haue kinde interteynem<sup>t</sup> of<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-078" href="#sec-08-fn-078" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-078" role="doc-noteref">8</a></sup> freedome from Enimies, to w<sup>ch</sup> places yo<sup>u</sup> may assay to invite Mores Guz<sup>r</sup>atte or any other people tradeinge in those parte, w<sup>th</sup> such m<sup>r</sup>chandize as may be fitt for this Country and be rendred for such as we shall send at such tymes as yo<sup>u</sup> may suppose o<sup>r</sup> shippinge may w<sup>th</sup> Conveniencie arriue there, amongst w<sup>ch</sup> porte we hould the porte aforesaid in the Iland of Socotora <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-435" data-page="435" data-folio="343" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n434_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 343">[p. 343]</button> &amp;c: to be most fittest aswell for the neernes of the place as saueinge the Customes &amp;
