the highe way for the Guzaratte and other tradinge into the red Sea at their ordinary seasons   And thus haueing sett downe what we thinke needfull for o<sup>r</sup> busines of m<sup>r</sup>chandizeinge we thinke yt fittinge to sett downe some what for order in the generall gou<sup>r</sup>nm<sup>t</sup> in o<sup>r</sup> shippinge &amp;c:</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">25   Item we sett downe for o<sup>r</sup> absolute order and resoluc̃on that noe pson whatsoeu<sup>r</sup> w<sup>ch</sup> shall succeede by appointem<sup>t</sup> or otherwyse in any place of Comand or Office either in any of o<sup>r</sup> shipps, or in any of o<sup>r</sup> ffactories at Bantam Banda Suratt or any other place whatsoeu<sup>r</sup> shall take or be allowed the wages of his p<sup>r</sup>dicesso<sup>rs</sup> but though he be remoued preferred and come to any such place or office he shall content himselfe w<sup>th</sup> such wages as we haue formerlie agreed w<sup>th</sup> him for w<sup>th</sup>out any further demand or encroachment vpon the Company for the same vnlesse the Governo<sup>r</sup> and Comittees of o<sup>r</sup> Company for the tyme beinge shall be pleased to graunte the same, or any other allowance of gratitude or rewarde vpon their deserte.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">26   Item for that the Company accompteth yt presumption for any o<sup>r</sup> ffacto<sup>rs</sup> to assume to them selues the name of Captaine w<sup>th</sup>out their expresse order, we ordeyne that they shall come home as priuate Marchaunte &amp; onlie reteyne that title prayinge yo<sup>u</sup> the Generall to forbeare to Conferr any other title or dignity vpon them.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">27   Item That noe Cabens be altered nor pticions pulled downe in any of o<sup>r</sup> Shipps or chandged from the state they now are in at the settinge out of the same w<sup>ch</sup> in former voyadges haue beene done to the greate hurte of o<sup>r</sup> Company and discontent &amp; hazard of all.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">28   Item that noe wages to noe pson whatsoeu<sup>r</sup> be increased or giuen contrary to what wee haue already here appointed but the pties to be referred to be rewarded by the Company at their retourne as they shall be found to haue deserued:   Nor m<sup>rs</sup>: ffacto<sup>rs</sup>, nor other Officers of speciall place in o<sup>r</sup> Shipps to be remoued w<sup>th</sup>out Consent of such of the Counsell as we haue appointed to aduise w<sup>th</sup> yo<sup>u</sup> the Generall vpon all o<sup>r</sup> occasions, to w<sup>ch</sup> end we haue thought yt convenient &amp; soe ordered, that m<sup>r</sup> Nicholas Downton, m<sup>r</sup> Lawrence ffemell and such others as yo<sup>u</sup> <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-436" data-page="436" data-folio="344" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n435_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 344">[p. 344]</button> the Generall shall thinke good to call vnto yo<sup>u</sup> shall vpon any occasion consult &amp; aduise w<sup>th</sup> yo<sup>u</sup> for the disposeinge of any busines of ymportance in this voyadge whose psons and counsells we pray yo<sup>u</sup> loueinglie to ymbrace, And all them same in the Councell aforenamed excepting them lefte in the Countrie as ffactor<sup>s</sup> to be in lyke manner of the Councell w<sup>th</sup> yo<sup>u</sup> homewardę yf they or any of them doe retourne w<sup>th</sup> yo<sup>u</sup>. And not soe to thinke vpon aucthority sett vpon yo<sup>u</sup> that the same should serue for any yo<sup>r</sup> pticular respect knowinge that a wise man is best tried when he is put in authority &amp; that he is accompted of more fortitude that over cometh himselfe then he that prevaileth against a multitude.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">29 Item as yo<sup>u</sup> knowe this voyadge being exceeding troublesome &amp; costlie will not admitt any priuate trade but whatsoeu<sup>r</sup> is achiued by this generall &amp; greate chardge should be applyed to the benefitt of o<sup>r</sup> whole Company. Therefore we sett yt downe as an absolute order that noe man neither Generall, Leiutenant, Marchaunt, Master or Mariner or any other pson or psons whatsoeu<sup>r</sup> in any theis o<sup>r</sup> Shipps doe trade or deale for any m<sup>r</sup>chandize whatsoeu<sup>r</sup> w<sup>ch</sup> we shall prouide for o<sup>r</sup> retourne or any other sorte of m<sup>r</sup>chandize more then will be conteyned w<sup>th</sup>in his proportioned chest of allowance for this voyadge and the same to be registred in the pursars booke to thend that yf any pson die vpon the voyadge their freinde &amp; executor<sup>s</sup> may knowe what they had &amp; take notice what is become of the same houlding yt most reasonable<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-079" href="#sec-08-fn-079" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-079" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> that those whoe lyue by o<sup>r</sup> ymploym<sup>t</sup> should deale w<sup>th</sup> the Comodities w<sup>ch</sup> we labo<sup>r</sup> to gett to produce meanes for paym<sup>t</sup> of their wages and defrayinge of chardges of this o<sup>r</sup> greate and costlie voyadge.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">30 Item we forbid all vnnecessary expence of shooteing of Ordinance at drinking of healthes<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-080" href="#sec-08-fn-080" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-080" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> or at the Captaines goeing <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-437" data-page="437" data-folio="345" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n436_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 345">[p. 345]</button> ashore or such lyke for that thereby much poulder is wastfully consumed and o<sup>r</sup> shipp and goode put in much hazard &amp; danger &amp; lykewyse by yt way is geven to the Gunners vnder culler thereof to ymbecille the same.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">31   Item for avoieding Confusion in the gou<sup>r</sup>nm<sup>t</sup> of o<sup>r</sup> people &amp; for that nothing is soe vncertaine as the state of mans lyfe. Wee ordaine that yf God shall call yo<sup>u</sup> S<sup>r</sup> Henry Middleton (during this voyadge) out of this world w<sup>ch</sup> God defend then that m<sup>r</sup> Nicholas Downton shall succeede yo<sup>u</sup> &amp; excercyse yo<sup>r</sup> chardge whome we trust will soe behaue himselfe therein as shall be agreable to the good reporte of sundry men of worth giuen of him: But yf yt shall please God that the said m<sup>r</sup> Nicholas Downton shall depte this lyfe then we Comitt the government of o<sup>r</sup> Shipps &amp; the Mariners therein to such as we haue nominated in a writeing subscribed by the Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> &amp; sealed w<sup>th</sup> the seale of o<sup>r</sup> Company put vpp into a boxe sealed w<sup>th</sup> hard waxe &amp; deliu<sup>r</sup>ed to S<sup>r</sup> Henrie Middleton to be kept close and not opened soe longe as the said S<sup>r</sup> Henry Middleton<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-081" href="#sec-08-fn-081" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-081" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> &amp; Nicholas Downton or either of them shall lyue: but yf yt shall please God to take both of them out of this world Then we ordaine that all o<sup>r</sup> M<sup>rs</sup> &amp; Mariners in o<sup>r</sup> said Shipps shall submitt them selues to the gouernm<sup>t</sup> and Comand of such pson or psons as we haue nominated therevnto in o<sup>r</sup> said writeinge sealed vp in a Boxe as aforesaid. Prouided alwaies that noe pson what soeu<sup>r</sup> w<sup>ch</sup> shall succeede in the aforesaid place of government shall demand be allowed or expect the wages of his predicessor but shall content himselfe w<sup>th</sup> his first wages promysed him by the Company.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">32   Item for that in sondry voyadges heretofore the Company haue beene much wronged by the Generalls Captaines &amp; m<sup>rs</sup> of their Shipps in takeing diu<sup>r</sup>s psons into the same shipps &amp; carryinge them alonge w<sup>th</sup> them in the said voyadge to the East Indies for their pticuler gaine and proffitt &amp; haue lefte the same pties amonge o<sup>r</sup> Company ffacto<sup>rs</sup> in the Indies where they haue driuen and handled for them selues &amp; their freinde a pticular trade to the greate chardge &amp; hindrance<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-082" href="#sec-08-fn-082" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-082" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> of o<sup>r</sup> Company, &amp; haue <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-438" data-page="438" data-folio="346" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n437_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 346">[p. 346]</button> practised w<sup>th</sup> o<sup>r</sup> ffactors there to deceaue vs, and to convey<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-083" href="#sec-08-fn-083" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-083" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> sundry of the ritchest Cōmodities of those ptę to their priuate vses. It is therefore o<sup>r</sup> expresse o<sup>r</sup>der &amp; direcčon to yo<sup>u</sup> S<sup>r</sup> Henry Middleton Generall of this o<sup>r</sup> ffleete &amp; to Nicholas Downton Leiuetenant of the same &amp; to all other the Marchauntę ffactors m<sup>rs</sup> Pursars &amp; Mariners and eu<sup>r</sup>y other pson or psons enterteyned, and hired to serue in this said voyadge that neither yo<sup>u</sup> or any of yo<sup>u</sup> shall admitt or suffer to passe in, or receiue into any of o<sup>r</sup> said Shipps in this o<sup>r</sup> now intended voyadge any pson or psonns of what degree or quality soeu<sup>r</sup> other then such pson or psons as are interteyned &amp; hired by the Governo<sup>r</sup> and Comittees of o<sup>r</sup> said Company to passe therein.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">33. Item yf duringe yo<sup>r</sup> Contynuance in the ptę of the Indies vpon good aduise &amp; Consideračon yo<sup>u</sup> finde yt more proffitable for the Company to haue one of theis Shipps remaine in the Country there to trade from place to place then to retorne home laden w<sup>th</sup> the rest, Then we referr the same to be ordered by yo<sup>u</sup> as shall seeme good in yo<sup>r</sup> discrecõn to make choise of one of the smaller Shipps to be employed for that purpose.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">34 Item. And lastly to prevent disorders &amp; misbehaviors of the Mariners of theis o<sup>r</sup> shipps either in their passadge out, or retourne home vpon o<sup>r</sup> owne Coastę wee ordaine that yo<sup>u</sup> sufferr none of o<sup>r</sup> foresaid Shipps either outwardę or homewardę to goe into any of the portę of ffalmouth Plymouth or Darthmouth vnlesse necessity compell yo<sup>u</sup> therevnto.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">And soe haueing remembred what we thinke needfull for yo<sup>r</sup> direcčon in this voyadge &amp; relyinge vpon yo<sup>r</sup> dilligence &amp; care in pursueing o<sup>r</sup> busines to o<sup>r</sup> most aduantage wee Comitt yo<sup>u</sup> to the protecčon of Almighty God whome we beseech to blesse yo<sup>r</sup> endeavo<sup>rs</sup> &amp; graunte yo<sup>u</sup> a saffe passadge and saffe retourne to ali o<sup>r</sup> Comfortę.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">Considering that vnto m<sup>r</sup> Sharpey Generall of the fourth voyadge Comission was geven to seeke for trade at Aden Mocha Suratt Cambaia Laree, Nigertuttey in the Ryver Syndus, at Priaman Banda or the Molluccoes &amp; where he found yt most convenient &amp; proffitable that he should there settle a factory for <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-439" data-page="439" data-folio="347" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n438_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 347">[p. 347]</button> increase of trade to the benefitt of the Generall Company, forasmuch as yt is credibly reported that they haue traded at Mocha we suppose that they haue there setled a factory, wherefore we pray yo<sup>u</sup> at yo<sup>r</sup> arriuall at Socotora carefully to enquire what is become of them and hereing that they haue left ffactor<sup>s</sup> at Mocha we pray yo<sup>u</sup> vse yo<sup>r</sup> best meanes to send o<sup>r</sup> tres vnto them w<sup>ch</sup> herew<sup>th</sup> are deliu<sup>r</sup>ed yo<sup>u</sup>,<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-084" href="#sec-08-fn-084" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-084" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> that they may receiue some comforte or there findinge noe good<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-085" href="#sec-08-fn-085" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-085" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> conveyaunce we Suppose at Suratt yo<sup>u</sup> shall not onlie heare of them, but from thence by the Iunckę bound to Moccha yo<sup>u</sup> may send o<sup>r</sup> tres, &amp; advise them whether yt be fitt to stay there or come to Suratt to be employed in the Companies seruice or retourne in one of the first Shipps<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-085-2" href="#sec-08-fn-085" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-085" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> &amp; bringe w<sup>th</sup> them in some good Comodities soe much as is remaineinge in their handę for thaccompt of the fourth voyadge, but yf yo<sup>u</sup> heare not of them in any of those places,<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-086" href="#sec-08-fn-086" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-086" role="doc-noteref">3</a></sup> then we pray yo<sup>u</sup> to enquire for them at Priaman Bantam Banda or<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-087" href="#sec-08-fn-087" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-087" role="doc-noteref">4</a></sup> the Molloccoes yf yo<sup>u</sup> goe thither, and findinge them deliu<sup>r</sup> o<sup>r</sup> tres whereby they shall pceiue that it is o<sup>r</sup> pleasure: they shall followe the direcc̃ons w<sup>ch</sup> yo<sup>ur</sup> selfe &amp; those appointed to Consult w<sup>th</sup> yo<sup>u</sup> in this voyadge and the said ffactor<sup>s</sup> shall vpon due deliberac̃on conclude vpon.<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-088" href="#sec-08-fn-088" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-088" role="doc-noteref">5</a></sup></p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">Henry Middleton</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">Beniamyn Greene</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">Nicholas Downton</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">Gyles Thornton</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">Abraham Lawse</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">postscriptũ</aside></div><p class="prose-p">W<sup>m</sup>: Pemberton If you shall finde in yo<sup>r</sup> trauaile or can convenientlie come by any faire<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-089" href="#sec-08-fn-089" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-089" role="doc-noteref">6</a></sup> byrdę, or beastę or any other thinge fitt [for] vs to p<sup>r</sup>sent to his Ma<sup>tie</sup>: or any of the noble <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-440" data-page="440" data-folio="348" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n439_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 348">[p. 348]</button> Lorde that are o<sup>r</sup> honorable freinde, we pray and requier yo<sup>u</sup> to be carefull to prouide and send the same w<sup>th</sup> espetiall chardge to some carefull man or men.<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-090" href="#sec-08-fn-090" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-090" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup></p></div><h3 class="sec-h2">Most highe and mighty kinge<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-091" href="#sec-08-fn-091" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-091" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup></h3><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">Twoe of this draught were written w<sup>th</sup> Blanckes</aside></div><p class="prose-p"><span class="sc">As</span> there is nothing w<sup>ch</sup> increaseth more the glorie and dignity of Sovereigne Princes vpon earth, then to extend their renowne vnto farr dissident nations; Soe havinge vnderstoode of late yeares from some of o<sup>r</sup> loueing Subiecte that haue traded into diu<sup>r</sup>s Countries neere adioyneinge vnto yo<sup>rs</sup>, of the reputacõn and greatnes of yo<sup>r</sup> power and Dominion, Wee have incouradged o<sup>r</sup> said Subiecte to vndertake a voyadge into yo<sup>r</sup> Country aswell to sollicite yo<sup>r</sup> freindshipp &amp; amity w<sup>th</sup> vs, as to enterteyne such Comodities of each others Countryes as may be most of vse the one to the other, beinge nothinge doubtfull but such will be yo<sup>r</sup> Princelie magnanimity &amp; disposicõn to embrace this o<sup>r</sup> desier, &amp; not onelie to receiue o<sup>r</sup> people w<sup>th</sup> yo<sup>r</sup> accustomed benignity and favo<sup>r</sup>, but for their better encourradgm<sup>t</sup> to afford yo<sup>r</sup> Ryall proteccõn for the setling of a factorie there, w<sup>th</sup> such security &amp; liberty of comerce as shall be most Convenient for the aduancm<sup>t</sup> of the mutuall proffitt and Comoditie of each others Subiecte: wherein for o<sup>r</sup> parte we doe willingly offer o<sup>r</sup> selues &amp; the libertie of o<sup>r</sup> kingdomes &amp; Countryes whensoeu<sup>r</sup> any of yo<sup>r</sup> Subiecte shall vndertake to haue comunication w<sup>th</sup> vs. And in the meane tyme as a pledge of o<sup>r</sup> Royall inclynacõn &amp; desier towarde yo<sup>u</sup> wee haue sent yo<sup>u</sup> a small <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-441" data-page="441" data-folio="349" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n440_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 349">[p. 349]</button> present by o<sup>r</sup> servant S<sup>r</sup> Henry Middleton knight w<sup>ch</sup> we pray yo<sup>u</sup> to accept, as an introducc̃on to that freindshipp w<sup>ch</sup> we hope will fructifye and increase to each others Contentm<sup>t</sup>. Geven at o<sup>r</sup> Pallace of Westminst<sup>r</sup> the &amp;c:</p></div><h2 class="sec-h1">Iames by the grace of Almightie God kinge of greate Brittaine ffraunce &amp; Ireland defendor of the Christian faith &amp;c: To the highe and myghty Prince the greate kinge of Cambaia, Empero<sup>r</sup> of Mogores &amp;c: greetinge</h2><h3 class="sec-h2">Most highe &amp; mighty Empero<sup>r</sup>.</h3><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">K: of Cambaia.</aside></div><p class="prose-p">WEE Cannot but expresse vnto yo<sup>u</sup> the highe Contentm<sup>t</sup> we haue receiued, at the kinde reception &amp; interteynem<sup>t</sup> of o<sup>r</sup> seruant Captaine William Hawkins, and his Company at yo<sup>r</sup> porte of Suratt, where they haue receiued soe many demonstrations by yo<sup>r</sup> principall Officers and Comanders there, of yo<sup>r</sup> desier to embrace o<sup>r</sup> loue and freindshipp &amp; to interteyne a Correspondencie of peaceable trade, and Comerce w<sup>th</sup> o<sup>r</sup> Subiecte, as we cannot but thankfully acknowledge the same, &amp; reciprocally offer the lyke effecte towarde yo<sup>u</sup> and yo<sup>r</sup> people, whensoeur they shall vndertake to haue any Comunicãcon of trade w<sup>th</sup> o<sup>r</sup> kingdomes and Dominions: But we were sorry to vnderstand of that vnhappy accident, w<sup>ch</sup> befell o<sup>r</sup> Subiecte in their retourne from yo<sup>r</sup> Coaste towarde their Shipps,<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-092" href="#sec-08-fn-092" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-092" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> by being vnworthilie surprized w<sup>th</sup>in yo<sup>r</sup> Streames by diu<sup>r</sup>s ffriggotte of the Portugalls of Goa whoe vniustlie haue taken from them nyneteene of o<sup>r</sup> Subiecte beside agreate quantity of their marchandize, and detayne them prisonners at Goa, vnder this sole pretext, as yf both yo<sup>r</sup>selfe and yo<sup>r</sup> Countreys were assubiected to the Crowne of Spaygne and therefore not lawfull for any other Christian people to trade w<sup>th</sup> yo<sup>u</sup>: w<sup>ch</sup> indignity thoughe Almighty God hath geven vs power enoughe to revenge agaynst them, yett because yt hath relãcon to the greatnes of yo<sup>r</sup> estate as beinge done w<sup>th</sup>in <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-442" data-page="442" data-folio="350" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n441_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 350">[p. 350]</button> the Compasse of yo<sup>r</sup> power &amp; aucthority, Wee haue thought good, first to represent yt vnto yo<sup>u</sup>; and to seeke redresse of yt, by yo<sup>r</sup> meanes, then to enter into any course of hostility in those partę contrary to the peaceable inclyna<span class="sc">c</span>õn of trade w<sup>ch</sup> our Subiectę are willinge to entertayne w<sup>th</sup>all other Nations; Notw<sup>th</sup>standing w<sup>ch</sup> hard successe o<sup>r</sup> Subiectę are noe whit discourradged from vndertakeinge (as now they doe) another voyadge into yo<sup>r</sup> Countreys, w<sup>th</sup> three good shipps laden w<sup>th</sup> such Comodities of o<sup>r</sup> kingdomes as may be most of vse to yo<sup>r</sup> people, being Confident of yo<sup>r</sup> further favo<sup>r</sup> and protec<span class="sc">c</span>õn, w<sup>th</sup> such Immunities and priuiledge for their trade and Comerce w<sup>th</sup> yo<sup>r</sup> people, as may incourradge them more and more to fixe and Contynue a firme Correspondencie there to the increase of proffitt of each others Subiectę. And for a pledge of o<sup>r</sup> Royall inclina<span class="sc">c</span>õn towardę yo<sup>u</sup> Wee haue sent yo<sup>u</sup> a small present by o<sup>r</sup> Servaunt S<sup>r</sup> Henry Middleton knight, Generall of this o<sup>r</sup> voyadge w<sup>ch</sup> wee pray yo<sup>u</sup> to accept in good parte &amp; to vse him w<sup>th</sup>all courtesie and respect, as is befittinge the quallity of his place. Geven at o<sup>r</sup> Pallace of Westminst<sup>r</sup> this &amp;c:</p></div><h2 class="sec-h1"><em>Iames by the grace of Almightie God kinge of greate Brittaine ffraunce and Ireland defendo<sup>r</sup> of the Christian faith &amp;c: To the right Honorable the cheefe Commander of the greate Citty of Cambaia greetinge</em></h2><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote"><span class="sc">Commander of Cambaia</span></aside></div><p class="prose-p"><span class="sc">Where</span>[<span class="sc">as</span>] wee haue vnderstoode from some of o<sup>r</sup> Subiectę, what greate courtesies they haue receiued, and daylie doe receiue at yo<sup>r</sup> haundę, in their arriuall and settlement of their trade in the greate Citty of Cambaia, We could not omytt att this second voyadge thither, but to expresse vnto yo<sup>u</sup> our thankfull acknowledgment for the same, w<sup>th</sup> o<sup>r</sup> Constant hope of yo<sup>r</sup> further endevo<sup>rs</sup> towardę them aswell for their future saffeguard &amp; protecc̃on in those places, against the Malice and violence of any that should attempt to wronge them, as for their better incourradgment to establish and increase <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-443" data-page="443" data-folio="351" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n442_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 351">[p. 351]</button> (as now they haue begun alreadie) a firme Correspondencie of trade in those parte, wherein for o<sup>r</sup> parte, wee will not omytt any thinge that may tend to the furtheringe of soe greate a good for the aduancm<sup>t</sup> of o<sup>r</sup> Subiecte.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">As for the iniury and losse w<sup>ch</sup> o<sup>r</sup> Subiecte latelie receaued by the Portingalls of Goa (as is well knowen vnto yo<sup>u</sup>) thoughe we want noe meanes to revenge o<sup>r</sup> selues against them, yett because we would be loath to interrupt the peaceable course of trade in those parte, and for that the Iniury doth alsoe redound to yo<sup>u</sup>, as being done w<sup>th</sup>in the lymitte of yo<sup>r</sup> Comand, Wee haue thought good first to desier yo<sup>r</sup> peaceable Intercession for the redresse thereof, before o<sup>r</sup> Subiecte should attempt any thinge hostilly agaynst them, wherein yo<sup>u</sup> shall doe a worke worthy of yo<sup>r</sup> selfe, and by w<sup>ch</sup> yo<sup>u</sup> shall oblige o<sup>r</sup> people more and more vnto yo<sup>u</sup>. And for o<sup>r</sup> good inclina͠con towarde yo<sup>u</sup>, wee haue sent yo<sup>u</sup> atoken of o<sup>r</sup> loue and frendship by o<sup>r</sup> servant S<sup>r</sup> Henrie Middleton knight, Generall of this o<sup>r</sup> voyadge.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">Geven at o<sup>r</sup> Pallace of Westminst<sup>r</sup> this</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">Iames by the grace of Almighty God kinge of greate Brittane ffraunce &amp; Ireland, defendo<sup>r</sup> of the Christian faith &amp;c:</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">To the kinge of Succatora.</aside></div><p class="prose-p">To the highe &amp; mighty Prince the kinge of Succatora Geeteinge.</p></div><h3 class="sec-h2">Most mighty kinge</h3><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">HAUEINGE vnderstoode from some of o<sup>r</sup> loueinge Subiecte, that in their course of Navigation &amp; traffique into diu<sup>r</sup>s parte of the East Indies, they haue alsoe visited yo<sup>r</sup> Coaste &amp; Territories where they haue not onlie refreshed themselues of such necessaries, as are belonginge to soe greate a Nauiga͠con, but haue alsoe receiued the best interteynem<sup>t</sup> that could be expected; wee hould yt o<sup>r</sup> parte to acknowledge the same w<sup>th</sup> thanke, and to ymbrace yo<sup>r</sup> amity and freindshipp w<sup>th</sup>all the respecte of hono<sup>r</sup>, that may tend to the establishinge and encreaseing of the same to o<sup>r</sup> mutuall <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-444" data-page="444" data-folio="352" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n443_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 352">[p. 352]</button> contentacõn &amp; aduancem<sup>t</sup> of proffitt of each others Subiecte; Wherein for o<sup>r</sup> parte, as we doe readily offerr o<sup>r</sup> selues and any thinge that shall be in o<sup>r</sup> power, soe wee doe expect the lyke effecte from yo<sup>u</sup>, to redound vpon o<sup>r</sup> Subiecte, in yo<sup>r</sup> good vsage of them, and affording them yo<sup>r</sup> princelie favo<sup>r</sup> and proteccõn against such as should goe aboute to harme them. And for a pledge of this o<sup>r</sup> good inclinaẽõn towarde yo<sup>u</sup>, wee haue sent yo<sup>u</sup> this small Present by o<sup>r</sup> servant S<sup>r</sup> Henrie Middleton knight, w<sup>ch</sup> wee pray yo<sup>u</sup> to accept in good parte. Given at o<sup>r</sup> Pallace of Westm: this &amp;c:</p></div><h2 class="sec-h1">Iames by the grace of Almighty God kinge of greate Brittaine ffraunce and Ireland defendo<sup>r</sup> of the Christian faith &amp;c: To the most highe &amp; mightie Prince the kinge of Bantham Greetinge</h2><h3 class="sec-h2">Right highe and mighty Prince</h3><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">Kinge of Bantham</aside></div><p class="prose-p">I T hath beene noe small contentm<sup>t</sup> vnto us to vnderstand from o<sup>r</sup> Subiecte retourned from their last voyadge into yo<sup>r</sup> Countries, both howe favorablie they haue beene vsed by yo<sup>u</sup>, and yo<sup>r</sup> people, &amp; howe willinge yo<sup>u</sup> haue beene to interteyne o<sup>r</sup> loue &amp; freindshipp, both w<sup>ch</sup>, yo<sup>u</sup> haue beene Contented to confirme vnto vs by a present of fower Balls<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-093" href="#sec-08-fn-093" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-093" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> of silke w<sup>ch</sup> yo<sup>u</sup> haue sent vnto vs, ffor w<sup>ch</sup> wee can not but retourne vnto yo<sup>u</sup> o<sup>r</sup> harty thanke w<sup>th</sup>all the kinde demonstrations of o<sup>r</sup> reciprocall desier to increase o<sup>r</sup> amity &amp; correspondency w<sup>th</sup> yo<sup>u</sup>, as far as the same may be honorable vnto vs, &amp; proffitable to each others Subiecte; In confidence whereof o<sup>r</sup> Subiecte haueinge vndertaken a new voyadge into yo<sup>r</sup> Country for the reestablishinge of their trade &amp; traffique w<sup>th</sup> yo<sup>r</sup> Subiecte, wee hope yo<sup>u</sup> will Contynewe vnto them yo<sup>r</sup> accustomed favo<sup>rs</sup>, and afford them such liberties &amp; ymunities there, as they may be thereby both incourradged in their greate adventures, and secured from oppression or wronge of any that would goe aboute <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-445" data-page="445" data-folio="353" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n444_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 353">[p. 353]</button> to harme them. And in thankfulnes of theis yo<sup>r</sup> kindnesses to o<sup>r</sup> people, wee haue sent yo<sup>u</sup> a token by this bearer o<sup>r</sup> Servant S<sup>r</sup> Henry Middleton knyght, Generall of o<sup>r</sup> present voyadge Giuen at o<sup>r</sup> Pallace of Westminst<sup>r</sup> this &amp;c:</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">K. of Tedore</aside></div><p class="prose-p">Iames by the grace of Almighty God kinge of greate Brittaine ffraunce and Ireland defendo<sup>r</sup> of the Christian faith &amp;c: To the most highe and mightie Prince the kinge of <em>Tedore</em>, Greetinge</p></div><h4 class="sec-h3">Most highe &amp; mighty kinge</h4><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">AS wee haue taken greate contentm<sup>t</sup> to vnderstand from o<sup>r</sup> Marchauntę att their retourne from their last voyadge out of yo<sup>r</sup> Country, howe kindlie &amp; favorably they haue beene interteyned by yo<sup>u</sup> and yo<sup>r</sup> people in their peaceable trade, soe we Cannot but reiteratiuely expresse o<sup>r</sup> thanke for the same, w<sup>th</sup> all the respecte of hono<sup>r</sup> that can be expected from vs, being readie both to ymbrace yo<sup>r</sup> freindshipp and amity &amp; to establish a constant entercourse of trade &amp; traffique betwixt each others Subiecte for w<sup>ch</sup> purpose, o<sup>r</sup> Subiecte haueing vndertaken a new voyadge into yo<sup>r</sup> Country, wee could not but take occasion to salute yo<sup>u</sup>, &amp; to pray yo<sup>u</sup> to Contynewe yo<sup>r</sup> favo<sup>r</sup> towarde them, w<sup>th</sup> such Conditions of saffetie &amp; ymunitie to their p̱sons, Shipps and goode, as they may be both encoradged in their iust and honest course, and protected against any wronge and vyolence of any that would goe aboute to harme them, And for a pledge of o<sup>r</sup> Princelie disposic̃on towarde yo<sup>u</sup>. Wee haue sent yo<sup>u</sup> a token by o<sup>r</sup> servant S<sup>r</sup> Henrie Middleton knight Generall of o<sup>r</sup> Marchauntę present voyadge Geven at o<sup>r</sup> Pallace of Westminst<sup>r</sup> this &amp;c:</p></div><div class="page-rail-marker" id="page-446"><button type="button" class="folio-trigger" data-page="446" data-folio="354" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n445_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect scan for page 354">[p. 354]</button></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">to the Governor of Aden</aside></div><p class="prose-p">Iames by the grace of Almightie God, kinge of greate Brittaine ffraunce &amp; Ireland defendo<sup>r</sup> of the Christian faith &amp;c: To the right honorable lord, the Governo<sup>r</sup> of the Citty of <em>Aden</em> Greetinge</p></div><h3 class="sec-h2">Right honorable Lord.</h3><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">THE Entercouse of amyty and freindshipp w<sup>ch</sup> hath beene theis many yeares betweene vs &amp; o<sup>r</sup> Predecesso<sup>rs</sup>, and the mighty howse of <em>Ottonan</em>, confirmed by soe many demonstrations of free Comerce &amp; libertie of trade throughe each others kingdomes &amp; domynions, hath incourradged o<sup>r</sup> Marchauntę to frequent alsoe those partę, where yo<sup>u</sup> haue chardge, and the rather because they haue lately vnderstoode that twoe of their Shipps in their course of Nauigation into diu<sup>r</sup>s partę of the East Indies, haue alsoe beene in the mouth of the <em>red</em> Sea &amp; found opportunity of traffique by enterteyneinge each others Comodities w<sup>ch</sup> hath induced them nowe to vndertake this voyadge, and prayed o<sup>r</sup> intercession vnto yo<sup>u</sup> for them, that they might be favorably receaued, and inioye in those partę alsoe such liberty of trade w<sup>th</sup> saffetie and good vsage for their persons, Shipps and goodę, as they now haue and inioye in the hether partę of the <em>Grand Signors</em> domynions, w<sup>ch</sup> wee doe not doubt but yo<sup>u</sup> will be willinge to afford vnto them, and the rather because they bringe w<sup>th</sup> them a safe <em>Conduct</em> from the Grand <em>Signier</em> in that behalfe. And of o<sup>r</sup> good will &amp; inclination towardę yo<sup>u</sup>, wee haue sent yo<sup>u</sup> a small token by o<sup>r</sup> Servant S<sup>r</sup> Henry Middleton knight, w<sup>ch</sup> wee pray yo<sup>u</sup> to accept in good parte. And soe we Comitt yo<sup>u</sup> to the protecc̃on of the most highe &amp; almighty God the Creater &amp; Preseruer of all thinge: ffrom o<sup>r</sup> Pallace at Westminst<sup>r</sup> this &amp;c:</p></div><div class="page-rail-marker" id="page-447"><button type="button" class="folio-trigger" data-page="447" data-folio="355" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n446_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect scan for page 355">[p. 355]</button></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">Sabander of Nero.</aside></div><p class="prose-p">Iames by the grace of Almightie God kinge of greate Brittaine ffraunce and Ireland Defendo<sup>r</sup> of the Christian faith &amp;c: To the right honorable the <em>Sabander of Nero</em>,<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-094" href="#sec-08-fn-094" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-094" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> Greeteinge.</p></div><h4 class="sec-h3">Right honorable Lord</h4><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">ALTHOUGHE wee haue heretofore by o<sup>r</sup> lres vnto yo<sup>u</sup> expressed o<sup>r</sup> acknowledgem<sup>t</sup> for the favorable vsage &amp; interteynem<sup>t</sup> yo<sup>u</sup> haue giuen to o<sup>r</sup> Subiecte in their trade and traffique w<sup>th</sup>in yo<sup>r</sup> Countries, yett they haueing now vndertaken a lyke voyadge thither againe, wee cannot but reiterate o<sup>r</sup> former acknowledgm<sup>t</sup> as a matter honorable to yo<sup>u</sup>, and acceptable to vs, And the rather because wee doe not doubt but thereby yo<sup>u</sup> will be more &amp; more induced to afford vnto o<sup>r</sup> Subiecte such further favo<sup>r</sup> &amp; pteccõn w<sup>th</sup>all Condicons of hono<sup>r</sup> and saffety to their persons, Ships &amp; goode, as may encoradg them to contynewe their trade &amp; Comerce w<sup>th</sup> yo<sup>r</sup> people; wherein yo<sup>u</sup> shall doe a worke worthy of yo<sup>r</sup> selfe, and much advantagious to the proffitt of each others Subiecte. And for a demonstrac̃on of o<sup>r</sup> favorable inclynation towarde yo<sup>u</sup>, and embraceinge of yo<sup>r</sup> loue &amp; freindshipp, wee haue sent yo<sup>u</sup> atoken by o<sup>r</sup> servant S<sup>r</sup> Henrie Middleton knight, Generall of this o<sup>r</sup> voyadge, w<sup>ch</sup> wee pray yo<sup>u</sup> to accept in good parte, and to hono<sup>r</sup> and respect him accordinge to the quallity of his place. Given at o<sup>r</sup> Pallace of Westminst<sup>r</sup> this &amp;c:</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">A warrant for transportacon of 300li into Irel:</aside></div><p class="prose-p">AFTER<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-095" href="#sec-08-fn-095" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-095" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> my hartie Comendac̃ons wheras the Companye of m<sup>r</sup>chante trading to the <em>East Indyes</em>, being now buylding a Shippe in <em>Ireland</em>, and hauing occasion to vse some readye monye, for prouiding of sundrie thinge ther, necessarye for the finishing of the said shippe haue made suite for lycense to transport from hence, the some of Thre hundred pounde sterling, Theis are therfore to wyll and require y<sup>u</sup> to suffer any <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-448" data-page="448" data-folio="356" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n447_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 356">[p. 356]</button> p̄sons aucthorized by thē to shippe forth the said some of Thre hundred pounde. Prouided that y<sup>u</sup> take bond for deliu<sup>r</sup>ye of the said mony in <em>Ireland</em>, ther to be employed as aforesaid. And for so doing this (being grounded vppō his ma<sup>te</sup> warrant vnto me in that behalf) shalbe yo<sup>r</sup> sufficyent discharge. ffrom <em>Whitehall</em> the 4 of Octob̄r 1610.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">Yo<sup>r</sup> louing friend</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">To my louinge friende, the <em>R</em> <span class="sc">Salisberye</span></p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">A Commyssion from his Mat<sup>ie</sup> to S<sup>r</sup> Henry M<sub>y</sub> ddleton knight</aside></div><p class="prose-p">JAMES,<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-096" href="#sec-08-fn-096" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-096" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> by the grace of God, king of <em>England</em>, <em>Fraunce</em> and <em>Ireland</em>, defend<sup>r</sup> of the faith &amp;c. TO our trustye and welbeloued seru<sup>a</sup>nt <em>Henry Myddleton</em> knight, Greting. WHERAS, dyuers of o<sup>r</sup> louing subiecte, by the name of the <em>Gouerno<sup>r</sup> and Companye of Marchants of London trading into the East Indyes</em>, at ther owne aduentures, coste and charge, for the hono<sup>r</sup> of this o<sup>r</sup> Realme of <em>England</em>, and for the encrease and aduauncement of trade, and m<sup>r</sup>chandize w<sup>th</sup>in the same, haue set forth sundrie voyage to the <em>East Indies</em>, and alreadye discouered and begunne to settle a trade into those parte, and intending the contynuinge therof, aswell by frequenting those place alreadye setled, as by furth<sup>r</sup> discouerye of other parte, w<sup>ch</sup> may be fitt for the enterchaunging of the Comōdityes of our kingdomes, w<sup>th</sup> the Comōdityes of those place, such as may be of spīall vse and benefitte to vs and o<sup>r</sup> subiecte, hauing to this purpose now p<sup>r</sup>pared and sett forth thre Shippes to the said <em>East Indyes</em>, and the place adioyning therunto AND WHERAS our said Subiecte the Aduenturer<sup>s</sup> of those voyage, haue chosen y<sup>u</sup>, our said seru<sup>a</sup>nt <em>Henry Myddleton</em> knight, to be y<sup>e</sup> principall Gouerno<sup>r</sup> or Generall of all the m<sup>r</sup>chante, maryners and oth<sup>r</sup> our Subiecte, w<sup>ch</sup> are or shalbe shipped in the said thre Shippes, WE graciouslye fauouring the<sup>r</sup> intended voyage, and approuinge and allowinge of <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-449" data-page="449" data-folio="357" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n448_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 357">[p. 357]</button> ther choice of y<sup>u</sup> to the same gouernm<sup>t</sup>, being wylling to furnishe y<sup>u</sup> w<sup>th</sup> all fitt and conuenyent power and aucthoritye, to rule &amp; gouerne all and euerye our Subiecte, employed in this voyage, by a due obedyence to be by them yeilded vnto yo<sup>u</sup> in the obseruinge and executing of all such good orders, and constitucõns, as y<sup>u</sup> shall thinke conuenyent to ordeine and make for the furtheraunce of y<sup>e</sup> said voiage according to such pticler direccõns and enstruccõns, as y<sup>u</sup> shall receiue from y<sup>e</sup> said Gouerno<sup>r</sup> and Companye of m<sup>r</sup>chante, the Aduenturers in this voyage, Straightlye charging and comãunding all and euerye pson or psons employed in this voyage, and shipped in the said thre Shippes, to giue all due obedyence and respect vnto y<sup>u</sup> during the said voyage, and to beare themselues one towarde anoth<sup>r</sup> in good ord<sup>r</sup> and quyetnes for auoyding any occasion that might breede mutinye, quarrells or dissencoñ amongst them, to the hinderaunce of the good successe, w<sup>ch</sup> is to be hoped for by Gode prouidence of this said voyage. AND in default of such duitie and obedyence to be pfourmed towarde y<sup>u</sup>, for the correcting and quenching of all such mutinyes, quarrells or dissencons̃ that may arise, by the euyll and disorderlye disposicoñ of any pson whatsoeuer, WE do herbye aucthorize y<sup>u</sup> our said seruãnt <em>Henry Middleton</em> knight during the said voyage, or during so long tyme as y<sup>u</sup> shall liue in the said voyage, to chastice correct and punishe all Offendo<sup>rs</sup> and transgressors in that behalf according to the qualitye of ther offences, w<sup>th</sup> such punishm<sup>te</sup> as are com̃onlye vsed in all Armyes at Sea, when they are not Capitall. AND for Capitall offence as wilfull murth<sup>r</sup>, w<sup>ch</sup> is hatefull in the sight of god, or mutinye w<sup>ch</sup> is an offence w<sup>ch</sup> may tend to the ouerthrowe of the said voyage, the same being dulye and iustlye proued against any of the pson or psons aforesaid. WE do herby giue vnto y<sup>u</sup> o<sup>r</sup> said seruãnt <em>Henry Myddleton</em> knight, during all the tyme of the said voyage, or during so long tyme as y<sup>u</sup> shall liue in the said voyage, full power and aucthoritye to vse and put in execuc̃on our lawe, called Lawe Marciall in that behalf, And these our łres shalbe vnto y<sup>u</sup> sufficyent warrant and discharge, for the doing and executing of all and singłer the p<sup>r</sup>misses. AND forasmuch as at this p<sup>r</sup>sent tyme, we are in amitye w<sup>th</sup> all Christian Princes, and are vnwylling that any o<sup>r</sup> Subiecte should giue occasion of breach or <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-450" data-page="450" data-folio="358" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n449_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 358">[p. 358]</button> hinderaunce therof towardę any o<sup>r</sup> Confederates friendę or Allyes, And because we are not ignoraunt of the emulac̃on and enuye w<sup>ch</sup> doth accompanye the discouerye of Countryes and trades, and of the quarrells and contenc̃ons w<sup>ch</sup> do many tymes fall out betwene the Subiectę of dyuers Princes when they mete the one w<sup>th</sup> the oth<sup>r</sup> in forreigne and farre remote Countryes in prosecuting the course of the<sup>r</sup> discoueries, and being desirous that our Subiectę should forbeare to moue or beginne any quarrell or contenc̃on vppō the Subiectę of o<sup>r</sup> Confederates friendę or Allies, eith<sup>r</sup> in ther going forth or retourning from any of ther voyage WE THERFORE do hereby e straightlye charge and com̃aund y<sup>u</sup> o<sup>r</sup> said seru<sup>a</sup>nt <em>Henrye Myddleton</em> knight, and all other vnder yo<sup>r</sup> gouernm<sup>t</sup>, that neith<sup>r</sup> in yo<sup>r</sup> voyage outward or homeward, or in any Countrye, Island, Port or place, wher y<sup>u</sup> shall abyde or come, during the tyme of yo<sup>r</sup> being abroad, out of o<sup>r</sup> kingdomes or domynions, wher y<sup>u</sup> meete w<sup>th</sup> any the Subiectę of y<sup>e</sup> king of <em>Spaine</em>, or of any oth<sup>r</sup> o<sup>r</sup> Confederates, friendę, or Allies, or of any other nac̃on or people, ther Shippes, Vessells, goodę or m<sup>r</sup>chandizes, y<sup>u</sup> do not attempt or go about to sett vppō, take or surprize their ꝑsons, Shippes, vessells, goodę or m<sup>r</sup>chandizes, or offer any iniurye or discourtesye vnto them, as y<sup>u</sup> wyll aunswer the contrarye, at yo<sup>r</sup> vtt<sup>r</sup>most ꝑills. Except y<sup>u</sup> shalbe by them first therunto iustlye ꝑuoked or driuē, eith<sup>r</sup> in the iust defence of yo<sup>r</sup> owne ꝑsons, Shippes, vessells, goodę or m<sup>r</sup>chandizes, by any ther disturbaunce or hinderaunce w<sup>t</sup>soeu<sup>r</sup> in yo<sup>r</sup> quyett course of trade, or for recompence and recou<sup>r</sup>ye of the ꝑsons, Shippes, goodę or m<sup>r</sup>chandizes of any o<sup>r</sup> Subiectę, that are alreadye in or neare the <em>East Indyes</em>, or for any oth<sup>r</sup> iust cause of yo<sup>r</sup> defence, In w<sup>ch</sup> cases so excepted, if y<sup>u</sup> attempt, surprize, and take the ꝑsons, Shippes and goodę of any Prince or State, by whose Subiectę y<sup>u</sup> shall susteine any wrong, or losse in manner as aforesaid, y<sup>u</sup> shall not for any such acte or actę, grounded vppō the occasions aboue menc̃oned, be in daung<sup>r</sup> or subiect to the ꝑill and penalties of o<sup>r</sup> lawes, Alwayes ꝑsupposing and so requiring y<sup>u</sup> to vse all possible endeuo<sup>rs</sup> by all meanes whatsoeu<sup>r</sup>, to suffer no spoile to be made of any goodę or m<sup>r</sup>chandizes, nor imbeaseling of any thing whatsoeu<sup>r</sup> that shalbe recou<sup>r</sup>ed by y<sup>u</sup>, but to see thē saufelye brought home w<sup>th</sup> ther Bylles of lading and Chart<sup>r</sup> pties, to the intent that <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-451" data-page="451" data-folio="359" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n450_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 359">[p. 359]</button> there may be restitucõn made by vs, so soone as y<sup>u</sup> shall receiue satisfaccõn from that State or people, by whome y<sup>u</sup> are dampnifyed of what Natiō soeuer. AND forasmuch as o<sup>r</sup> said Subiectę the m<sup>r</sup>chantę and oth<sup>r</sup> the Aduenturer<sup>s</sup> of this voyadge, out of prouydence and foresight yf any mortalitye eith<sup>r</sup> by sicknes or otherwise (w<sup>ch</sup> God forbyd) should befall vnto o<sup>r</sup> seru<sup>a</sup>nt <em>Henry Myddleton</em> knight, haue made choise of o<sup>r</sup> trustie and louing subiecte Cap<sup>n</sup><sup>e</sup> <em>Nichās Dounton</em>, to succeede the said <em>Henry Myddleton</em> knight, in the charge and com̄aund of principall Gouerno<sup>r</sup> or Gen<sup>r</sup>all of all the ꝑsons employed in this voyage as aforesaid WE do alsoe (in case such an accydent should happen) allowe and approue of ther choyce in that behalf, And do herby giue full power and aucthoritye to the said Cap<sup>n</sup><sup>e</sup> <em>Nichās Dounton</em>, to do and execute all and euerye thing, incydent to the charge of principall Gouerno<sup>r</sup> or Generall of all the ꝑsons employed in this voyage as aforesaid in as ample manner and forme, and to all intentę and purposes, as is before com̄ytted and imposed vppō our Seru<sup>a</sup>nt <em>Henrye Mydleton</em> knight, And these o<sup>r</sup> łres shalbe also sufficyent warrant and discharge to the said Cap<sup>n</sup><sup>e</sup> <em>Nichās Dounton</em>, for the doing and executing of all and singuler the p<sup>r</sup>misses as afores<sup>d</sup>. IN WYTNES wherof we haue caused these o<sup>r</sup> łres to be made Patentę. WITNES our self at Westm̄ the seauententh day of March in the seauenth yeare of our Raigne of <em>England</em>, <em>Fraunce</em> and <em>Ireland</em>, and of <em>Scotland</em> the thre and forteth <em>per breue de priuato Sigillo.</em> COPPIN.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">A Commyssion from his Matie vnto Anth: Hippon Capne.</aside></div><p class="prose-p">JAMES,<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-097" href="#sec-08-fn-097" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-097" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> by the grace of God, king of England, Scotland, ffraunce and Ireland, defend<sup>r</sup> of the faith &amp;c TO OUR trustye and welbeloued <em>Anthonye Hippon</em>,<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-098" href="#sec-08-fn-098" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-098" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> greting, WHERAS dyuers of our louing subiectę, by the name of the Gouern<sup>r</sup> and Companye of m<sup>r</sup>chantę of <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-452" data-page="452" data-folio="360" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n451_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 360">[p. 360]</button> Londō, trading into the <em>East Indyes</em>, at ther owne aduentures coste and charges, for the hono<sup>r</sup> of this o<sup>r</sup> Realme of England, and for the encrease and aduancem<sup>t</sup> of trade, and m<sup>r</sup>chandize w<sup>th</sup>in the same, haue set forth sundrie voyage to the <em>East Indyes</em>, and alreadye discouered, and begunne to settle a trade into those parte, and intending the contynuinge therof, aswell by frequenting those pte alreadie setled, as by furth<sup>r</sup> discouerye of oth<sup>r</sup> pte w<sup>ch</sup> may be fitt, for ent<sup>r</sup>chaunging of the Com̃odityes of o<sup>r</sup> kingdomes, w<sup>th</sup> the com̃odityes of those place, such as may be of speciall vse and benefitte to vs and o<sup>r</sup> subiecte, hauing to this purpose now p<sup>r</sup>pared and sett forth the good Shippe called the <em>Globe</em>, AND wheras our said subiecte, the Aduenturer<sup>s</sup> of those voyage, haue chosen y<sup>u</sup> the said <em>Anthonye Hippō</em>, to be Gouerno<sup>r</sup> or Captaine of all the m<sup>r</sup>chante, maryners, and all oth<sup>rs</sup>, as well our Subiecte, as of what Natiō soeu<sup>r</sup> that shall goe in the said Shippe, we graciouslye fauouring the intended voyage, and approuing and allowing of ther choyce of y<sup>u</sup> to the same gouernm<sup>t</sup>, being wylling to furnishe y<sup>u</sup> w<sup>th</sup> all fytt and conuenyent power &amp; aucthoritye, to rule and gouerne all and euerye our Subiecte and others whatsoeu<sup>r</sup>, employed in this voyage by a due obedyence to be by thē yeilded vnto y<sup>u</sup> in the obseruing and executing of all such good orders and constitucõns as y<sup>u</sup> shall thinke conuenyent to ordeine and make for the furtheraunce of the said voyage, according to such pticłer direccõns and enstruccõns as y<sup>u</sup> shall receiue from the said Gouerno<sup>r</sup> and Companye of m<sup>r</sup>chante the Aduenturer<sup>s</sup> in this voyage, Straightlye charging and com̃aunding all and eu<sup>r</sup>ye pson and psons employed in this voyage, and shipped in the said Shippe to giue all due obedyence and respect vnto y<sup>u</sup>, during the said voyage, And to beare themselues one towarde anoth<sup>r</sup> in good order and quietnes, for auoyding any occasion that might breede mutinye, quarrells or dissençõns amongst thē to the hinderaunce of the good successe, w<sup>ch</sup> is to be hoped for, by gode puidence of this said voyage AND in default of such duitye and obedyence to be pfourmed towarde y<sup>u</sup>, for the correcting and quenching of all such mutinyes, quarrells and dissençõns, that may arise by the euyll and disorderlye disposic̃on of any pson whatsoeu<sup>r</sup>, we do herbye <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-453" data-page="453" data-folio="361" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n452_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 361">[p. 361]</button> aucthorize y<sup>u</sup>, our said subiect <em>Anthonye Hippon</em>, during the said voyage, or during so long tyme as y<sup>u</sup> shall lyue in the said voyage, to chastize correct and punishe all offend<sup>rs</sup> and transgressor<sup>s</sup> in that behalf according to the qualitye of ther offence; w<sup>th</sup> such punishm<sup>te</sup> as are com̄onlye vsed in all armyes at Sea, when they are not capitall, And for Capitall offence, or<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-099" href="#sec-08-fn-099" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-099" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> wilfull murth<sup>r</sup>, (w<sup>ch</sup> is hatefull in the sight of god) or mutynie, (w<sup>ch</sup> is an offence which may tend to the ouerthrowe of the said voyadge) the same being iustlye and dulye proued, against any of the pson or psons aforesaid WEE do hereby giue vnto y<sup>u</sup> o<sup>r</sup> said subiect <em>Anthonye Hippon</em>, during all the tyme of the said voyage, or during so long tyme, as y<sup>u</sup> shall lyue in the said voyage, full power and aucthoritye to vse and put in execuc̃on our Lawe called Lawe Martiall in that behalf, and these o<sup>r</sup> łres shalbe yo<sup>r</sup> sufficyent warrant, and discharge for the doing and executing all and singłer the p<sup>r</sup>misses. AND forasmuch as at this pn̄t tyme we are in Amitye w<sup>th</sup> all xpian prince, and are vnwylling that any o<sup>r</sup> Subiecte, should giue occasiō of breach or hinderaunce therof, towarde any o<sup>r</sup> confederate, friende or allyes, And because y<sup>u</sup><sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-100" href="#sec-08-fn-100" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-100" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> are not ignoraunt of the emulac̃on and enuye w<sup>ch</sup> doth accompanye the discouerye of Countryes, and Trades, and of the quarrells and contenc̃ons w<sup>ch</sup> do many tymes fall out betwene the Subiecte of dyuers Prince when they meete the one w<sup>th</sup> the oth<sup>r</sup> in forreigne and farre remote Countryes, in psecuting the course of ther discoueryes; and being desirous that o<sup>r</sup> Subiecte should forbeare or<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-101" href="#sec-08-fn-101" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-101" role="doc-noteref">3</a></sup> beginne to<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-101-2" href="#sec-08-fn-101" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-101" role="doc-noteref">3</a></sup> moue any quarrells or contenc̃ons, vppō the subiecte of o<sup>r</sup> confederate, friende or allyes, eith<sup>r</sup> in ther going forth or retourning from any the voyage WEE therfore do straightlye charge and com̄aund y<sup>u</sup>, o<sup>r</sup> said subiect <em>Anthonye Hippon</em>, and all oth<sup>r</sup> vnder yo<sup>r</sup> gouernm<sup>t</sup>, That neith<sup>r</sup> in yo<sup>r</sup> voyage outward or homeward, or in any Countrye, Island, Port or place, wher y<sup>u</sup> shall abide or come during the tyme of yo<sup>r</sup> being abroad out of o<sup>r</sup> kingdome or domynions, wher y<sup>u</sup> meete w<sup>th</sup> any the Subiecte of the king of <em>Spayne</em>, or of any oth<sup>r</sup> o<sup>r</sup> Confederates, friende or Allyes, or of any oth<sup>r</sup> Natiō or people, ther shippes, vessells, goode or m<sup>r</sup>chandizes, yow do not attempt or <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-454" data-page="454" data-folio="362" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n453_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 362">[p. 362]</button> go about to sett vppō, take or surprize, ther psons, shippes, vessells, goode or mʳchandizes, or offer any Iniurye or discourtesye vnto thē, as yᵘ wyll aunswer the contrarye at yoʳ vttermost pills Except yᵘ shalbe by them first therunto puoked or drawne, eithʳ in the iust defence of yoʳ owne psons, shippes, vessells, goode or mʳchandize, by any their disturbaunce or hinderaunce wᵗsoeuʳ in yoʳ quiett course of trade, or for recompence, and recouerye of yᵉ psons, shippes, goode, or mʳchandizes of any oʳ Subiecte that are alreadie in or neare the <em>East Indyes</em>, Or for any othʳ iust cause of yoʳ defence, In wᶜʰ causes so excepted, if yᵘ attempt, surprize and take, the psons shippes and goode of any Prince or State, by whose Subiecte yᵘ shall susteyne, any wrong or losse in manner as aforesaid, yᵘ shall not for any such Act or Acte, (grounded vppō the occasions aboue menčōned,) be in daungʳ or subiect to the pill and penaltyes of oʳ Lawes, Alwayes pʳsupposing, and so requiring yᵘ to vse all possible endeuoʳˢ by all meanes whatsoeuʳ, to suffer no spoyle to be made, of any goode or mʳchandizes nor embezzilling of any goode whatsoeuʳ that shalbe recouʳed by yᵘ, But to see them saufelye brought home, wᵗʰ ther bylles of lading, and Chartʳ pties, to the intent that there may be restitučōn made by vs so soone as yᵘ shall receyue satisfaccōn from that State or people, by whome yᵘ are dampnifyed, of what Natiō soeuʳ AND forasmuch as oʳ said Subiecte, the mʳchante and other the Aduenturerˢ of this voyage, out of prouydence and foresight of any mortalitye, eithʳ by sicknes or otherwise, wᶜʰ god forbyd should befall, vnto our Subiect <em>Anthonye Hyppon</em>, haue made choyce of oʳ trustie and welbeloued Subiect <em>Robert Browne</em>,<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-102" href="#sec-08-fn-102" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-102" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> to succede the said <em>Anthonye Hyppon</em> in yᵉ charge and com̄aund of Gouernoʳ and Captaine of all the psons employed in this voyage WEE do also in case such an accydent should happē allowe and approue of ther choyce in that behalf, And do herby giue full power and auchoritye to the said <em>Robert Browne</em>, to do and execute all and euerye thing incydent to the charge of Gouernoʳ and Capⁿᵉ of all the psons employed in this voyage, as aforesaid, in as ample manner and forme, vnto all intente and purposes, as is before com̄ytted and imposed vppō oʳ Subiect <em>Anthonye Hyppon</em>, And <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-455" data-page="455" data-folio="363" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n454_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 363">[p. 363]</button> these o<sup>r</sup> łres shalbe also a suffycyent warrant and discharge to the said <em>Robert Browne</em>, for y<sup>e</sup> doing and executing of all and singłer the p<sup>r</sup>misses as aforesaid IN WYTN̄ES whereof, we haue caused these o<sup>r</sup> łres to be made Patenṯe, WYTN̄ES our self at <em>Westmynster</em>, the fourth day of Decemƀr, in the Eight yeare of our Raigne of <em>England Fraunce &amp; Ireland</em>, and of <em>Scotland</em> the fower and fortith.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">COPPIN <em>Per breue de priuato Sigillo</em></p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">Articles indented &amp; agreed vpon the thirtenth day of Decemƀ in the yeare of o<sup>r</sup> Lord 1610. Betweene S<sup>r</sup> Thomas Smyth knight Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> of the Company of Marchaunṯe of London, tradeinge to the East Indies, and Certeine Committees of that Company appointed by order of Courte for that purpose, on thone parte. And <em>Peter Floris &amp; Lucas Athewmes</em>,<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-103" href="#sec-08-fn-103" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-103" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> Dutch Marchaunṯe on thother parte. In manner &amp; forme followinge.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">INPRIMIS. Itt is agreed that a shipp shall be prouided by the said Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> and Companie of the burthen of fower hundred Tunns or thereabouṯe To be readie to sett saile from hence in this instant Decemƀ, or in Ianuarie next, w<sup>th</sup> fower score men (Marchaunṯe &amp; all others) &amp; victualled for thirtie monnethes for a voyadge (by godę grace) to be made therew<sup>th</sup> into the said East Indies.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">Item: ffor the p̄formance thereof, as alsoe for a competent <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-456" data-page="456" data-folio="364" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n455_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 364">[p. 364]</button> stocke of 12000<sup>li</sup> or thereaboutes is appointed to be leuied, whereof 1500<sup>li</sup> is to be putt into the said stocke by the foresaid <em>Peter Floris &amp; Lucas Antheumes</em> for their aduenture And 600<sup>li</sup> of the said 1500<sup>li</sup>, is by them presentlie to be paid, to such a Casheare as shalbe therevnto by the said Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> &amp; Companie chosen And the other 900<sup>li</sup> to be likewise paid to the said Chasheare in Iune or Iulie next followinge the date of theis presentes (w<sup>ch</sup> the said Companie doe acknowledge to haue receiued). The rest of the said stocke of 12000<sup>li</sup> is to be supplied by the said Company.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">Item. That of the foresaid 12000<sup>li</sup> intended for the said stocke, the chardge of the Shippe, victuallinge &amp; such other necessaries shalbe supplied, &amp; then the Remainder of the said some shall be imployed as a stocke by them for this voyadge.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">Item. That the said <em>Pieter Floris, Lucas Atheumes &amp; Robert Browne</em> shall be the principall ffactors for sale &amp; imploym<sup>t</sup> of the said stocke, duringe the said voyadge, And that whatsoeuer shalbe by them bought or sould in the tyme of this their said voyadge shalbe registred and written by some English Marchauntę to be appointed by the said Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> &amp; Companie for that purpose. And alsoe the said <em>Pieter Floris, &amp; Lucas Atheumes</em> doe promyse that they will neither buie nor sell any Marchaundize or Comodities whatsoeu<sup>r</sup>, nor make any priuate, nor any other ꝭgaines before hand, but such and att those tymes when the said <em>Robert Browne;</em> and other Englishe Marchauntę shall be by att the makeinge of them. Promysinge further to vse their best dilligence &amp; endevo<sup>rs</sup> to informe teach &amp; enstruct the said Englishe Marchauntę in all the misteries &amp; secreetę belonginge to the said trade and busines.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">Item. The said <em>Pieter Floris &amp; Lucas Athemmes</em> doe promyse that they will not intermeddle nor haue any dealinges w<sup>th</sup> any other busines then buyinge &amp; sellinge of Marchaundize &amp; such other Comodities w<sup>th</sup>out the Consent of the Captaine and <em>Robert Browne</em> w<sup>th</sup> the rest of the said English Marchauntę: Nor w<sup>th</sup> setlinge any ffactory in any place, or w<sup>th</sup> any state whatsoeu<sup>r</sup> w<sup>th</sup>out the Consent &amp; resoluc̃on first had of the said Capt &amp; englishe Marchauntę as aforesaid. Prouided &amp; yt is agreed that the said Capt &amp; englishe Marchauntę shall cause the shipp to sayle, &amp; <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-457" data-page="457" data-folio="365" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n456_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 365">[p. 365]</button> direct their course to all such hauens and portes as the said <em>Pieter Ploris &amp; Lucas Atheumes</em> shall from tyme to tyme direct them vnto.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">Item The money and Marchandize appointed for the foresaid stocke shall be m<sup>r</sup>ked w<sup>th</sup> the Companies marke, &amp; remaine in the Custodie and chardge of the said Captaine &amp; english Marchauntes vntill there be tyme of ymploym<sup>t</sup>, and then to be deliu<sup>r</sup>ed to the said <em>Pieter Ploris</em>, <em>Lucas Antheumes &amp; Robert Browne</em> as occasion shall serue, and by them required. And att what tyme soeu<sup>r</sup> the said Marchandize, or any parte thereof shall be sould &amp; ymployed in other wares &amp; goode the same shall presentlie be brought abourd &amp; putt into the hould of the said shipp (marked w<sup>th</sup> the said Companies marke expressed in the margent). And this to be done as often as any chaunge, sale or ymploym<sup>t</sup> shall be made of the said wares goode or marchandize. And due accompte to be kept by the said englishe Marchaunte from tyme to tyme of the doinge thereof, soe as the Gou<sup>r</sup>nor &amp; Company (for the tyme beinge) may receiue true informacõn of eu<sup>r</sup>y pticuler buyinge and sellinge w<sup>th</sup> the places where the same was done, &amp; the tymes when.</p></div><figure class="archival-plate" id="fig-019"><img src="/figures/eic-first-letter-book-1600-1619/fig-019_p0457.webp" alt="East India Company merchant&#x27;s mark showing a cross over a heart/circle device containing initials G, C, and E." loading="lazy"></figure><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">Item Thatt for the doinge &amp; pformaunce of this busines the said Gou<sup>r</sup>nor &amp; Company doe promise to allowe to the said <em>Pieter Ploris</em> and <em>Lucas Anthumes</em>, for their factorie vpon the nette pceede of the said stocke (Marriners wages &amp; all other chardges deducted []) as followeth.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">If there be vnder Centum p Cento, nette proffitt made, by the said voyadge, then they are to receiue noe factorage.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">If there be <em>Centum</em> p <em>Cento</em> nette proffitt made as for example 24000<sup>li</sup> for 12000<sup>li</sup> then they are to haue 4 p Centū.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">If one hundred and a halfe be made, nett proffitt vpon the hundred as 30000<sup>li</sup> for 12000<sup>li</sup> then they are to haue 5 p Cent.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">If Twoe hundred be made nette proffitt vpon the hundred as 36000<sup>li</sup> for 12000<sup>li</sup> then they are to haue 6 p Cent.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">If twoe hundred &amp; a halfe be made nette proffitt vpon the hundred as 42000<sup>li</sup> for 12000<sup>li</sup> then they are to haue 7 p Centū. <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-458" data-page="458" data-folio="366" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n457_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 366">[p. 366]</button> If three hundred be made nette proffitt vpon the hundred as 48000<sup>li</sup> for 12000<sup>li</sup> then they are to haue 8 <span class="sc">p</span> Centū.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">If three hundred &amp; a halfe be made nette proffitt vpon the hundred as 54000<sup>li</sup> for 12000<sup>li</sup> then they are to have 9 <span class="sc">p</span> Centū.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">If ffower hundred be made nette pffitt vpon the hundred as 60000<sup>li</sup> for 12000<sup>li</sup> then they are to haue 10 <span class="sc">p</span> Centū. And noe more what increase soeu<sup>r</sup> they shall retourne of proffitt or gaine, they shall haue noe more then 10 <span class="sc">p</span> Centū.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">Item That duringe the said voyadge they shall neither by themselues, nor any other for them or to their vse directlie or indirectlie, vse or exercise any priuate trade or Marchandizeinge whatsoeu<sup>r</sup> But shall applie themselues &amp; their whole endevo<sup>rs</sup> for the good &amp; aduancm<sup>t</sup> of the gen<sup>r</sup>all stocke, And the said Gou<sup>r</sup>nor &amp; Companie, doe promise to vse their best care &amp; diligence to p<sup>r</sup>uent the like priuate trade or Marchandizinge to be made by the said Captaine, englishe Marchauntę or any other Marriners whatsoeu<sup>r</sup>.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">Item that the said <em>Peter Ploris</em> &amp; <em>Lucas Antheumes</em> shall become bound vnto the said Gou<sup>r</sup>nor &amp; Comp: in 2000<sup>li</sup> for their true &amp; faithfull dealeinge w<sup>th</sup> the said Gou<sup>r</sup>nor &amp; Comp: in the said voyadge, and for the due pformance of whatt is for them to be pformed abouesaid. As alsoe for the deliu<sup>r</sup>inge vp in writeinge of a true and iust accompt of all there pceedinge in the said voyadge.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">Item yf God shall call the said <em>Peter Floris</em> &amp; <em>Lucas Atheumes</em> out of this lyfe, before that they shall retourne backe into the porte of London, then the benefitt of their factorage as abouesaid, and nette pceede of their aduenture pporcionablie w<sup>th</sup> the rest of the Aduenturers shall be trulie paid and satisfied vnto their heires Executors or Administrators.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">Item yt is further agreed That yf the said <em>Peter Floris</em> or the said <em>Lucas Antheumes</em> or either of them doe fortune to die, &amp; the other retourne that then the suruiuo<sup>r</sup> shall stand for both, &amp; be accomptable in the behalfe of both, for all that hath past in the said voyadge accordinge to the true meaneinge of theis Articles.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">Item. Whereas certaine Articles of Agreem<sup>t</sup> were made and concluded, betwixt the said S<sup>r</sup>: Thomas Smyth Gou<sup>r</sup>nor of the <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-459" data-page="459" data-folio="367" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n458_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 367">[p. 367]</button> said Companie &amp;c: on the one parte: And Peter <em>Floris</em> &amp; <em>Lucas Antheumes</em> on thother parte bereinge date the 27<sup>th</sup> day of March 1610. for c<sup>r</sup>teine couenantę to haue beene pformed on either parte: Itt is nowe agreed vpon that the said Articles shall be disannulled and cancelled, &amp; whatsoeu<sup>r</sup> is conteyned in them to be of noe strength nor power.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">In Witnes whereof the said <em>Pieter Floris</em> and <em>Lucas Antheumes</em> haue sett to their handę and seales to thone parte, and the said Companie haue fixed their Comon seale vnto theis presentę to thother parte the day &amp; yeare first aboue written</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">Whereas mention is made in the precedent Articles of a stocke of 12000<sup>li</sup> to be ymployed by the foresaid <em>Pieter Floris</em> &amp; <em>Lucas Antheumes</em>, for w<sup>ch</sup> they are to receiue factorie accordinge to the said Articles of agreem<sup>t</sup> betwixt the Companie and them; They nowe findinge that by the increase of chardges the Capitall will be raysed vnto 14000<sup>li</sup> or thereaboutes, doe desier that the Companie would be pleased to graunt them considerac̃on in there factorye for the said encrease, w<sup>ch</sup> the Company are willinge to yeald vnto in manner and forme followinge as is sett downe (accordinge to their owne desier) <em>on thother side of this Roll. Viz.</em> as followeth.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p"><em>Pieter Floris: Lucas Antheumes:</em></p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">Consideringe that the settinge forth of the Shipp cometh to more then was att the first supposed yt would haue done, In reguard whereof the Capitall is encreased w<sup>th</sup> Marchandize and money to 14000<sup>li</sup> or thereaboutę: Wee therefore the Gou<sup>r</sup>nor Deputy &amp; Comittees for the foresaid Companie (att the earnest request of the forenamed Peter <em>Floris</em> and Lucas Antheumes) are willinge and contented for their satisfacc̃on to graunt that there prom̃tio and factorie shall be reckoned as followeth.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">If they bring in retourne the nette some of 24000<sup>li</sup> then there prom̃tio shall begyn att 4 p Centū, and soe to Continue to 29000<sup>li</sup></p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">Butt yf it soe happen that they bringe in nette retourne 30000<sup>li</sup> or aboue the same some shall be diuided by 12000<sup>li</sup>, &amp; the prom̃tio to be reckoned as aforesaid.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">And in like manner the same some shall be diuided by 14000<sup>li</sup> <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-460" data-page="460" data-folio="368" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n459_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 368">[p. 368]</button> or as much as the nette Capitall shall come vnto &amp; the prom̄tio reckoned <em>pro rato</em> of 12000<sup>li</sup>.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">Nowe the difference betweene the twoe pm̄tions shall be equally diuided thone halfe to <em>Peter Floris</em> &amp; <em>Lucas Antheumes</em> &amp; thother halfe to the Companie.</p></div><h4 class="sec-h3"><em>Example.</em></h4><div class="ledger-table-block" id="tbl-016"><div class="table-toolbar"><span class="table-id-badge">TBL-016 · FOLIO 460</span><span class="table-csv-links"><a href="/downloads/eic-first-letter-book-1600-1619/datasets/tables/tbl-016_p0460.csv" download>Diplomatic CSV</a> · <a href="/downloads/eic-first-letter-book-1600-1619/datasets/tidy/tbl-016_p0460.tidy.csv" download>Tidy CSV</a></span></div><div class="ledger-table-wrap" role="region" aria-label="Statistical table" tabindex="0"><table class="ledger-table"><caption><em>Example.</em></caption><tbody><tr class="row-data"><td>12000<sup>li</sup> make 33000<sup>li</sup> is 2½ proffitt the pm̄tion 5 p̄ Cent is —</td><td class="num">1650<sup>li</sup></td></tr><tr class="row-data"><td>14000<sup>li</sup> make 33000<sup>li</sup> is 2 proffitt, the prom̄tio 4 p̄ Centū is — y<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-104" href="#sec-08-fn-104" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-104" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup></td><td class="num">1320<sup>li</sup></td></tr><tr class="row-data"><td>The difference is</td><td>y 330</td></tr><tr class="row-data"><td>thone halfe is</td><td>y 165</td></tr></tbody></table></div></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">This 165 must be added to the 1320<sup>li</sup>, or els substracted from 1650 &amp; then our prom̄tion for 33000<sup>li</sup> wilbe 1485<sup>li</sup>.</p></div><h4 class="sec-h3"><em>An other Example.</em></h4><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">12000<sup>li</sup> make 58000<sup>li</sup> is 4½ proffitt the prom̄tio 9 p̄ Cent is — y 5220<sup>li</sup>. 14000<sup>li</sup> make 58000<sup>li</sup> is fower proffitt the prom̄tio 8 p̄ Cent is y 4640<sup>li</sup>.</p></div><div class="ledger-table-block" id="tbl-017"><div class="table-toolbar"><span class="table-id-badge">TBL-017 · FOLIO 460</span><span class="table-csv-links"><a href="/downloads/eic-first-letter-book-1600-1619/datasets/tables/tbl-017_p0460.csv" download>Diplomatic CSV</a> · <a href="/downloads/eic-first-letter-book-1600-1619/datasets/tidy/tbl-017_p0460.tidy.csv" download>Tidy CSV</a></span></div><div class="ledger-table-wrap" role="region" aria-label="Statistical table" tabindex="0"><table class="ledger-table"><tbody><tr class="row-data"><td>The difference is —</td><td class="num">580<sup>li</sup></td></tr><tr class="row-data"><td>Thone halfe is</td><td>y 290</td></tr></tbody></table></div></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">This 290<sup>li</sup> must be added to 4640<sup>li</sup>, or els substracted from 5220<sup>li</sup> and then o<sup>r</sup> prom̄tio for 58000<sup>li</sup> wilbe y 4930<sup>li</sup>.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p"><em>Lucas Antheumes Peter Floris.</em></p></div><div class="page-rail-marker" id="page-461"><button type="button" class="folio-trigger" data-page="461" data-folio="369" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n460_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect scan for page 369">[p. 369]</button></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">A Com̄ission<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-105" href="#sec-08-fn-105" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-105" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> graunted by vs the Gou<sup>r</sup>nor Deputy &amp; Comittees, for the Company of Marchauntꬴ of London, tradeinge [into] the East Indies vnto Anthony Hippon Capt <em>Peter Floris</em>, <em>Lucas Antheumes</em> &amp; Rob̄t <em>Browne</em> o<sup>r</sup> principall ffactors, together w<sup>th</sup> Thomas Essington,<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-106" href="#sec-08-fn-106" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-106" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> Symon Evans, <em>Adam Denton</em>, &amp; George <em>Chauncey</em> the rest of o<sup>r</sup> ffactors and all other o<sup>r</sup> Marriners employed in this o<sup>r</sup> seaventh voyadge in the good shipp called the <em>Globe</em>.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote"><strong>I.</strong></aside><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">A Comission graunted by the Comp: to Capt Hippon in the 7<sup>th</sup> voyadge &amp;c:</aside></div><p class="prose-p"><span class="sc">Nprimis</span> Whereas wee the Marchauntꬴ abouesaid w<sup>th</sup> o<sup>r</sup> greate chardge &amp; trouble haue prepared a shipp called the <em>Globe</em>, for a voyadge by Godꬴ grace in her to be made to the bay of <em>Bengala</em> &amp; other places in att or aboute the East Indies, &amp; haue furnished the same in sufficient &amp; plentifull manner, w<sup>th</sup> men, tackle, victualls, &amp; all other needfull puičons, fitt for such a voyadge.
