And haueinge beene pswaded by the good reporte of dyvers, &amp; alsoe of an espetiall trust and confidence wee haue of the integritie, wisdome and resolučons of o<sup>r</sup> loueinge freindꬴ Anthonie Hippon, <em>Peter Floris</em>, <em>Lucas Antheumes</em> &amp; Rob̄t <em>Browne</em> Doe appointe and authorize the said Anthonie Hippon to be o<sup>r</sup> cheefe Captaine &amp; master, duringe the said voyadge, &amp; to comaund o<sup>r</sup> shipp w<sup>th</sup> the men &amp; Marrin<sup>r</sup>s att Sea, And <em>Peter Floris</em>, <em>Lucas Antheumes</em> &amp; Rob̄t Browne, our principall ffactors &amp; Cape Marchauntꬴ for the orderinge &amp; disposeinge of such moneys goodꬴ &amp; Marchandize on shore, as shall be carried alonge w<sup>th</sup> them in the said shipp. And Thomas Essington, Symon Evans, <em>Adam Denton</em> &amp; George Chauncey, wee appointe to take their passadge in the said shipp as o<sup>r</sup> ffactors to keepe bookes &amp; accomptꬴ of all goodꬴ &amp; <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-462" data-page="462" data-folio="370" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n461_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 370">[p. 370]</button> Marchandize sould and delyuered, and bought and receiued from tyme to tyme w<sup>th</sup> the names of the portes, where any such trade or traffique is made. All w<sup>ch</sup> both Commanders Marchauntę ffactors and Marriners wee trust will soe carrie them selues w<sup>th</sup> due respect one to another, loue to their equalls &amp; obedyence to their Superio<sup>rs</sup> as loue &amp; kindnes may be contynued on all sides, and all whollie applie their witte and endevo<sup>rs</sup> for the due pformaunce of this voyadge in the best manner they may for the good of the Companie.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><ul class="editorial-list"><li>2 Item for that religious gou<sup>r</sup>nm<sup>t</sup> doth best binde men to pforme their duties, It is principally to be cared for, that prayers be said eu<sup>r</sup>y Morninge &amp; eueninge in the Shippe, &amp; the whole Company called therevnto w<sup>th</sup> dilligent Eyes that none be wantinge, soe as all may ioyntlie w<sup>th</sup> reuerence and humilitie pray vnto Almightie God to blesse &amp; preserue them from all dangers in this longe and tedious voyadge And for that purpose wee haue deliu<sup>r</sup>ed vnto the purser a faire Bible, w<sup>th</sup> the booke of Comon prayers.</li><li>3 Item That noe blasphemeinge of God, sweareinge, theft, drunkennes, or other like misdemeano<sup>rs</sup> be vsed, butt that the same be seuerelie ponished &amp; that noe diceinge or other vnlawfull games be pmitted for that the same are most comonlie the begynninges of quarrells, and many times murther a iust occasion of Godę wrath and vengeance from w<sup>ch</sup> the lord deliuer vs all. Wherefore wee doe order that the Captaine shall puide ponishm<sup>t</sup>ę to be sett vp &amp; published in the said shipp for such offences, whereby eu<sup>r</sup>y man may take notice what he ought to avoied.</li><li>4 Item. Thatt noe liquor be spilt in the ballast of the shipp, or filthines be left w<sup>th</sup>in bourd w<sup>ch</sup> in heate breedeth noysome smells, and infec̃cõn: Butt that the ou<sup>r</sup>lopps and other places of the shipp be kept cleane &amp; sweete w<sup>ch</sup> is a notable preseruãcõn of health</li><li>5 Item That contynuall &amp; true Iournalls be kept of eu<sup>r</sup>y dayes course &amp; nauigãcõn duringe the whole voyadge, w<sup>th</sup> the obseruãcõn of fallinge w<sup>th</sup> eu<sup>r</sup>y Coaste &amp; land, or porte where they come, for the depth of water, &amp; in what latitude yt lyeth, w<sup>th</sup> true relãcõn of eu<sup>r</sup>y thinge that passeth And this to be done seu<sup>r</sup>ally by Captaine ffactors, Pylott, M<sup>rs</sup> mate and purser And <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-463" data-page="463" data-folio="371" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n462_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 371">[p. 371]</button> they att conuenient tyme to conferr together aboute the same, To thend that yf any of them haue forgotten what an other obserued the same may be added whereby a discourse may be sett downe, to be presented by the Captaine to the Gou<sup>r</sup>nor and Company, when God shall graunte the said shipp safe retourne.</li></ul></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">6. Item Thatt all money &amp; Marchandizes w<sup>ch</sup> shall be shipt here by the Gou<sup>r</sup>nor and Companie shall be kept by the Captaine he giueinge a receipt for the same w<sup>ch</sup> said receipt shall be kept by <em>Peter Floris Lucas Antheumes</em> and Robt Browne thereby to demand the said money &amp; goode when they shall come to those places, where they shall vse the same And then the same shall be deliu<sup>r</sup>ed ouer by the Captaine vnto them from tyme to tyme duringe the said voyadge.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">7 Item Wee will that the Captaine shall cause the Boates or Sciffes to be alwaies in a readines to be ymployed to all such places as o<sup>r</sup> principall Marchauntę shall appointe them, And not to dispose of them any otherwyse soe longe as they shall haue occasion to vse them.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">8 Item such Marchandize wares &amp; money as the Capt shall deliu<sup>r</sup> into the handes of the principall Marchauntę he shall take a receipt from them thereof: And such wares and Marchandize as shall be brought into the shipp by the foresaid principall Marchauntę <em>Peter Floris</em>, <em>Lucas Antheumes</em> &amp; Robt Browne (or such as shall succeede in their places) beinge marked w<sup>th</sup> the Companies marke, they shall take a receipt of the Capt for the same. And this shall be done soe often as any goode shall be dischardged forth of the shipp, or laden into the shipp, And the Captaine to be carefull that the goode be well preserued &amp; kept drie. To w<sup>ch</sup> end he shall haue espetiall care to looke often tymes vnto the overloppe, that ytt be well kept.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">9 Item the principall Marchauntę shall not doe any thinge (w<sup>ch</sup> is w<sup>th</sup>out the compasse of their tradeinge) w<sup>th</sup>out the Counsaile of the Capt &amp; other ffactors whether yt be in setlinge of any factory, or any contract or agreement to be made w<sup>th</sup> any kinge or potentate in the Indies Neither shall the Captaine haue any comaund or authoritie on the land to haue any thinge to doe w<sup>th</sup> the trade of Marchandizeinge Butt therein whollie wee doe authorize &amp; put in trust o<sup>r</sup> principall Marchauntę <em>Peter Floris</em>, <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-464" data-page="464" data-folio="372" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n463_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 372">[p. 372]</button> <em>Lucas Antheumes</em> &amp; Robt Browne to be cheefe Comanders on the land, to thend they may pforme the busines accordinge to their owne inuention. And Thomas Essington, Symon Evans, <em>Adam Denton</em>, and George <em>Chauncey</em> shall be w<sup>th</sup> them to assist them both in keepinge the bookes of accomptes as alsoe the Cashe.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><ul class="editorial-list"><li>10 Item yf the principall Marchauntę shall haue occasion to vse 5 or 6 Marriners or more to helpe to watch and keepe the goodę on Land in their howses, then yo<sup>u</sup> Capt Anthonie Hippon shall (vpon their demand) lett them haue soe many as they shall need, and those of the best and trustiest men yo<sup>u</sup> haue.</li><li>11 Item To the end that the voyadge be not hindred through want of victualls, Wee doe ordeyne and requier that the Captaine shall not alter the daylie allowance of victualls, w<sup>th</sup>out first haueinge taken Counsell &amp; consent of o<sup>r</sup> principall Marchauntę, Whoe shall keepe Counter bookes of all the Victualls that are spent, and that shall remaine in <em>Esse</em>, the better to make their reckoninge for their pceedinge in the voyadge. And when yo<sup>u</sup> shall come vnto the Land then shall the victualls be lockt vp in the shipp, and the principall Marchauntę shall puide fresh victualls from the land, the better to enlardge the store, yf any may be pcured.</li><li>12 Item. That att any place where yo<sup>u</sup> shall water, and refresh yo<sup>r</sup> selues, or putt in for traffique and Marchandizeinge, you Capt <em>Anthonie Hippon</em> shall call the whole Companie together giueinge them most strict &amp; straight chardge and warninge not to straggle, but to behaue them selues peaceablie and Ciuillie towarde the people of that place, (yf any be there) the better to pcure their freindshipp, towarde the supplie of yo<sup>r</sup> wante, and in yo<sup>r</sup> course of trade least the losse of yo<sup>r</sup> liues and ou<sup>r</sup>throwe of o<sup>r</sup> voyadge pay for yo<sup>r</sup> disorders, beside an vtter discreditt to o<sup>r</sup> Nation, And that yo<sup>r</sup> men doe vse a discreete meane in eateinge of fruite, fresh victualls, or drinkinge of water, leaste they surfett and fall into diseases, whereof wee haue had too much experience. But as any fresh prouisions shall be pcured a shore by our principall ffactors, the same shall be first brought vnto the Captaine (whoe w<sup>th</sup> there advise) shall reparte the same vnto the Company as to yo<sup>u</sup> shall seeme good and needfull: And none presume to doe contrary w<sup>th</sup>out seueare ponishm<sup>t</sup> And that <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-465" data-page="465" data-folio="373" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n464_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 373">[p. 373]</button> espetiall care be had to releiue the sicke, w<sup>th</sup> such fresh meatę, and other Comfortable thinges, wherew<sup>th</sup> wee haue furnished yo<sup>r</sup> shipp for that purpose, and not otherwise to be spent in Ryott and banquettinge and soe the sicke p̱ishe for wante of thingę needfull.</li></ul></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">13. Item Whereas the stragglinge of yo<sup>r</sup> men, in any place where they shall arryue and come, may not onlie proue the losse of such, as shall soe straggle (notw<sup>th</sup>standinge they should be armed) but alsoe thereby much hurte might happen to the residue of o<sup>r</sup> people. It is ordered that yo<sup>u</sup> doe not p̱mitt any man in any place to straggle or goe from the rest of yo<sup>r</sup> Companie vnlesse vpon some spetiall occasions yo<sup>u</sup> giue Warraunt therevnto, vpon paine of seueare ponishm<sup>t</sup> to be inflicted vpon such as shall doe the Contrarie, still doubtinge the worst and standinge vpon yo<sup>r</sup> guarde, w<sup>th</sup>out giueinge any trust vnto the people of the place.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">14 Item fforasmuch as wee are well p̱swaded, of the discrec̃on, circumspection &amp; good vnderstandinge of o<sup>r</sup> principall Marchauntę <em>Peter Floris</em>, <em>Lucas Antheumes</em>, and Rob̃t Browne. Wee doe therefore requier yo<sup>u</sup> Capt <em>Anthonie Hippon</em> that yo<sup>u</sup> be alwaies readie to saile to all such hauens and portes, as o<sup>r</sup> foresaid principall Marchauntę shall direct yo<sup>u</sup> to sayle vnto, and there to remayne vntill the said principall Marchauntę, shall haue finished there busines, &amp; then from thence to saile vnto such other places as they shall requier yo<sup>u</sup>. And this shall be soe often by yo<sup>u</sup> p̱formed as they shall thinke fitt: for therein consisteth the welfare of the said voyadge And noe man shall counsaile or resolue to retourne homewardes, vntill such tyme as o<sup>r</sup> said principall Marchauntę shall haue fully finisht there intended trade, vpon forfecture of all their wages.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">15 Item It is expreslie ordered that by a generall Consent of yo<sup>u</sup> the Capt: Marchauntę, factor<sup>s</sup>: and Marriners, some good order be by yo<sup>u</sup> taken, and sett downe in writinge, soe soone as yo<sup>u</sup> come to Sea, conc<sup>r</sup>ninge the disposeinge of dead mens goodę in the voyadge eyther by Comittinge the same by Inventory to the p̱ticular keepinge of some men in the shipps (and that vpon some convenient considerac̃ons, for keepinge the same) or by sale of soe much thereof att the mayne maste as may not exceede a <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-466" data-page="466" data-folio="374" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n465_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 374">[p. 374]</button> third parte of the buyers wages, for either buyinge or borroweinge, to preuent such preiudice to o<sup>r</sup> Company as wee haue felt by Marriners buyinge soe much, as there whole wages came vnto: whereby they haue not onlie forsaken o<sup>r</sup> shipps before their dischardge, butt alsoe depriued vs of meanes to redresse such wronges, as they haue many waies done vs in the voyadge. Alsoe that there be noe dealeinge by Exchange betwixt ptie and ptie, neither mony for mony, Comoditie for Comoditie Comoditie for money outwardes nor homewardes (for such exchange giues way for them to take and purloyne of o<sup>r</sup> goode). ffor preuencon whereof, wee order That neither the purser (nor any other) doe register, witnes, or take knowledge of any such contract by exchange. And noe man to be admitted meanes, to bringe goode abourd, vpon paine of forfecture to the Company of that w<sup>ch</sup> shall be soe brought in. And yf any ffactor shall happen to die, his booke, accompte, and other thinges are to be ordered by the Capt<sup>ne</sup>: &amp; safelie reserued and brought home for England (an Inventory first taken thereof and registred in the pursers booke, to be witnessed by twoe or three others of the Companie[]). And that eu<sup>r</sup>y Marriner or other in the shipp, doe sett there handes or marke to the said Orders mentioned in this 15th Article.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">16 Item To the end that all difficult matters may the better be passed over by good endevo<sup>rs</sup> wee doe appointt a Counsell of Eight psonns whereof Capt<sup>ne</sup>: <em>Anthony Hippon</em> to be Presedent (in matters of the shipp or for Iustice) and <em>Peter Floris</em>, <em>Lucas Antheumes</em> &amp; <em>Robert Browne</em>, w<sup>th</sup> <em>Thomas Essington</em>, <em>Symon Euans Adam Denton</em>, and <em>George Chauncey</em> (beinge in all eight psonns, whereof one of them to recorde whatt matters shall passe) shall be a sufficient Counsell to determyne, all cryminall &amp; ciuill causes that shall come before them. Yett forasmuch as <em>Peter Floris</em> &amp; Lucas Antheumes haue excused themselues thatt they are not acquaintted w<sup>th</sup> the losse<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-107" href="#sec-08-fn-107" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-107" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> and Iustice of o<sup>r</sup> land, therefore they shall be exempted &amp; not required to come to the Councell, when any matter of Iustice is to be handled (for such matters shall be determined by the rest of the Counsell) But yf yt be for any matters on land (or for any thinge w<sup>ch</sup> conc<sup>r</sup>neth trade[]), <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-467" data-page="467" data-folio="375" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n466_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 375">[p. 375]</button> then one of the principall Marchantę shall be president (either <em>Peter Floris</em>, <em>Lucas Antheumes</em> or Roƀt Browne as they shall thinke fitt amongst themselues, to determyn for that purpose[]). And the foresaid Counsell shall allowe of the said pson for President, and shall hearken to his ppositions, and take Counsell and resolue vppon the same, as occasion shall be offerred. And all of them aforenamed (exceptinge such as shalbe lefte in the Country as ffactors) to be in like manner of the Counsell w<sup>th</sup> yo<sup>u</sup> homewardę.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">17. Item the Cap<sup>tne</sup>: shall haue espetiall care that noe man whatsoeu<sup>r</sup> (of whatt quallity or Condicõn soeu<sup>r</sup> he be) his owne pson beinge alsoe included herein) shall vse any priuate or secreete trade vpon peine of fforfecture of all thatt shall be found by any man soe doinge, and besides shall endure arbitrary ponishm<sup>t</sup>. And wee requier the principall Marchauntę to haue espetiall care hereof on the land, and to preuent yt soe much as they can or is possible. And the Cap<sup>tne</sup>: and other Marchauntę shall alsoe haue a vigillant eye, &amp; good reguard on the principall Marchauntę, to the end the Company may be the better serued [(]whoe haue beene at such excessiue chardges) and reape the benefitt of the trade, discou<sup>r</sup>ed by there expences.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">18 Item for better preseruacõn of health, o<sup>r</sup> order is, That from tyme to tyme as the water Caske shall be emptied of fresh water, yt be forthw<sup>th</sup> againe filled w<sup>th</sup> salt water, for preuencõn of leakeinge and mustines.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">19 Item No pson whatsoeu<sup>r</sup> w<sup>ch</sup> shall succeede by appointm<sup>t</sup> or otherwise, in any place of com̃and or office, either in o<sup>r</sup> shipp, or in any ffactory to be setled, shall take or be allowed the wages of his predecessors. But thoughe he be remoued, preferred, and come to any such place and office, he shall content him selfe w<sup>th</sup> such wages as wee haue form<sup>r</sup>lie agreed w<sup>th</sup> him for, w<sup>th</sup>out any further demand or incroatchm<sup>t</sup> vpon the Company for the same (vnlesse the Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> &amp; Comittees of the Company for the tyme beinge, shall be pleased to graunt the same att their retourne, or any other allowance of gratitude or rewarde vpon their desertes[]).</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">20 Item fforasmuch as wee are c<sup>r</sup>teinelie informed that when twoe of o<sup>r</sup> principall Marchantę <em>Peter Floris</em>, &amp; <em>Lucas Antheumes</em> shall come into the Indies, they are lyke to be much hated by the <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-468" data-page="468" data-folio="376" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n467_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 376">[p. 376]</button> <em>Dutchmen</em> there, whoe will seeke to doe them wronge and all the villanie they may therefore we expreslie chardge yo<sup>u</sup> the Captaine (and all others that are in o<sup>r</sup> shipp &amp; voyadge) to defend and preserue them from wronges, w<sup>th</sup>all the power and strength you may against any that shall offer to doe them wronge or iniury: Or shall goe aboute to hinder them in their busines, w<sup>ch</sup> might much endanger our voyadge. The lyke Comandem<sup>t</sup> wee doe expreslie giue vnto all others that are in any of o<sup>r</sup> shipps, &amp; imployed in o<sup>r</sup> busines in any places whatsoeu<sup>r</sup>.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">21 Item Wee doe expreslie chardge yo<sup>u</sup> Capt <em>Anthony Hippon</em> not to suffer any Cabbins to be new built or altered, nor pticõns pulled downe in o<sup>r</sup> shipp (except for enlarginge of the hould, or makeinge better puision for the bringinge home of o<sup>r</sup> goode, and that by the aduise of the Councell, or for the better accomodatinge of there fightes<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-108" href="#sec-08-fn-108" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-108" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> if need shall be[]], or changed from the state they nowe are in, att the settinge out of the same, w<sup>ch</sup> in former voyadges haue beene done to the greate hurte of o<sup>r</sup> Companie, and discontent and hazard of all.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">22 Item That noe wages to noe pson whatsoeu<sup>r</sup> be encreased or giuen contrary to whatt we haue alreadie appointed, but the pties to be referd to be rewarded by the Company att their retourne as they shall be found to haue deserued. Nor ffactors, mrs mates, nor other officers of espetiall place in o<sup>r</sup> shipp to be remoued w<sup>th</sup>out consent of such of the Councell as wee haue appointed to aduise w<sup>th</sup> yo<sup>u</sup> the Capt<sup>ne</sup>: vpon all occasions. And not to thinke vpon authoritie sett vppon yo<sup>u</sup> That the same should serue for any yo<sup>r</sup> pticular respecte, knowinge that a wise man is best tryed when he is putt in authoritie. And that he is accompted of more fortitude that ou<sup>r</sup>cometh himselfe then he that p<sup>r</sup>uaileth against a multitude.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">23. Item Wee forbid all vnnecessarie expence of shooteinge <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-469" data-page="469" data-folio="377" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n468_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 377">[p. 377]</button> of Ordinance, att drinkinge of healthes or att the Captaines or Cape Marchauntę goinge a shore, or such like, for that thereby much powder is wastfullie consumed and o<sup>r</sup> shipp and goode putt in much hazard and danger: And likewise by yt way is giuen to the Gunners (vnder colour thereof) to embecyll the same.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">24. Item for avoiedinge confuc̃on, in the gou<sup>r</sup>nm<sup>t</sup> of o<sup>r</sup> people, And for that nothinge is soe vnc<sup>r</sup>teine as the state of mans lyfe, Wee ordeyne that yf God shall call you Captaine <em>Anthonie Hippon</em> (duringe this voyadge) out of this world, (w<sup>ch</sup> God defend) then wee Comitt the same gou<sup>r</sup>nm<sup>t</sup> of o<sup>r</sup> shipp and Marriners therein, to such as wee haue nomynated in a wryteinge subscribed by the Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> and sealed w<sup>th</sup> the seale of the Company, putt vp into a Boxe N<sup>o</sup>: 1. sealed w<sup>th</sup> hard Waxe &amp; deliu<sup>r</sup>ed to Cap<sup>tne</sup>: <em>Anthony Hippon</em> to be kept close, and not opened soe longe as the said Capt <em>Anthonie Hippon</em> shall liue: Butt yf it shall please God to take him out of this world (that shall succeede Captaine <em>Anthony Hippon</em>) Then wee ordeine thatt o<sup>r</sup> M<sup>r</sup> and Marriners in o<sup>r</sup> said shipp shall submit themselues to the gou<sup>r</sup>nm<sup>t</sup> and Comand of such pson or psons as wee haue nominated therevnto, in a writeinge sealed vp in a Boxe No: 2. And remaineinge in the Custodie of the said Capt Anthonie Hippon &amp; those that shall succeede him, Prouided alwaies that noe pson whatsoeu<sup>r</sup>, w<sup>ch</sup> shall succeede in the foresaid place of gou<sup>r</sup>nm<sup>t</sup> shall demaund be allowed or expect the wages of his predecessor, but shall content himselfe w<sup>th</sup> his first wages promysed him by the Companie, or referr him selfe to be considered of accordinge to his deserte att his retourne by the Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> Deputy and Comittees of the Company for the tyme beinge whoe haue ever respected men that shall deserue well of them.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">25. Item for that in many voyadges heretofore the Company haue beene much wronged by the takeinge in of diu<sup>r</sup>s psonns into ther shipps [()not interteyned by them) w<sup>ch</sup> haue beene carried into the East Indies, for some mens pticular gaine and proffitt to the greate chardge and hindraunce of the Companie Itt is therefore o<sup>r</sup> expresse order &amp; direcc̃on to yo<sup>u</sup> Captaine Anthony Hippon, and to all other the Marchantę ffactors<sup>rs</sup> &amp; Marriners &amp; eu<sup>r</sup>y other pson or psonns, enterteyned &amp; hired to serue in this said voyadge, That neither yo<sup>u</sup> nor any of yo<sup>u</sup> shall admitte, or suffer to passe <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-470" data-page="470" data-folio="378" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n469_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 378">[p. 378]</button> or receiue into o<sup>r</sup> said shipp (in this o<sup>r</sup> nowe intended voyadge) any pson or psonns, of whatt degree or quallitie soeu<sup>r</sup>, other then such pson or psons as are interteyned and hired by the Gou<sup>r</sup>nor and Comittees of o<sup>r</sup> said Companie to passe therein.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">26. Item If it should happen (as God forbid) that o<sup>r</sup> shipp should haue wante of men or other necessary meanes (w<sup>ch</sup> such longe voyadges are subject vnto) then wee will that you desire to be releeued by any shipp yo<sup>u</sup> shall meete (w<sup>ch</sup> is appteyneinge vnto vs, or any other Englishe shipp, whatsoeu<sup>r</sup>) And wee hereby straightlie chardge and comand all Generalls, Captaines, Masters, or any other Officers (in any of the shipps whatsoeu<sup>r</sup>, appteyneinge to the Companie of Marchaunte of London, tradeinge to the East Indies[])], whome yo<sup>u</sup> shall chaunce to meete in any such yo<sup>r</sup> distresse, That they supplie yo<sup>u</sup>, ayde and releeue yo<sup>u</sup> w<sup>th</sup> such men, or any other necessaries, w<sup>ch</sup> yo<sup>u</sup> shall haue neede of, &amp; they may convenientlie spare yo<sup>u</sup> w<sup>th</sup>out endaungeringe themselues and their shipps. And that yo<sup>u</sup> pforme the like good Offices to any of o<sup>r</sup> shipps, that yo<sup>u</sup> shall meete dureinge the said voyadge, whome yo<sup>u</sup> shall finde distressed.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">27 Item If yo<sup>u</sup> shall finde in yo<sup>r</sup> trauaile or can convenientlie come by any faire birde or Beaste or any other thinges fitt for vs to present to his Ma<sup>tie</sup>: or any of the Noble Lorde, that are o<sup>r</sup> honorable freinde, Wee pray and requier yo<sup>u</sup> to be carefull to prouide, and send the same w<sup>th</sup> espetiall chardge to some carefull man or men that retourne to see them carefully kept, and deliu<sup>r</sup>ed to vs. And w<sup>th</sup>all that yf yo<sup>u</sup> shall knowe any of the Marriners or others to haue any such, w<sup>ch</sup> yo<sup>u</sup> cannot pcure from them for reason, then that yo<sup>u</sup> send vs notice thereof, to the end wee may take order therein accordinglie.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">28 Item And lastlie to preuent disorders and misbehaiuours of the Marriners of this o<sup>r</sup> shipp, either in their passadge out or retourne whome<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-109" href="#sec-08-fn-109" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-109" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> vpon o<sup>r</sup> owne Coastes, wee ordeyne that yo<sup>u</sup> suffer not o<sup>r</sup> foresaid shipp, either outwarde or homewardes, to goe into any of the portes of Faulmouth Plymouth or Darthmouthe (vnlesse necessitie compell yo<sup>u</sup> therevnto[]).</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">And soe haueinge remembred what wee thinke needfull for yo<sup>r</sup> direccon in this voyadge, and relyinge vpon yo<sup>r</sup> dilligence and <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-471" data-page="471" data-folio="379" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n470_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 379">[p. 379]</button> care in psueinge o<sup>r</sup> busines to o<sup>r</sup> most aduantage, Wee comitt yo<sup>u</sup> to the pteccon of the Almightie God, whome wee beseech to blesse yo<sup>r</sup> endevo<sup>rs</sup>, and graunte yo<sup>u</sup> a safe passadge &amp; safe retourne to all o<sup>r</sup> Comforte.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">Item Wheareas the stores appteyneinge to eu<sup>r</sup>y pticular Office are nowe sufficientlie furnished for this voyadge, and an Invoice deliu<sup>r</sup>ed vnto yo<sup>u</sup> the Cap<sup>tne</sup>: of eu<sup>r</sup>y pticular store: w<sup>ch</sup> heretofore in some of o<sup>r</sup> former voyadges haue (through the remisnes of o<sup>r</sup> Comanders[]), bene ymbecilled and appropriated to mens pticular vses. ffor the better preuentinge therefore of the lyke inconueniences hereafter, wee ordeyne and giue yt in chardge vnto yo<sup>u</sup> Cap<sup>tne</sup>: <em>Anthony Hippon</em>, that yo<sup>u</sup> cause trewe notice to be taken, and a iust accompt kept of the expence of the Gunners, Carpenters, Boateswaines Stewardes and eu<sup>r</sup>y such store, both by the purser and some others that yo<sup>u</sup> shall appointe therevnto. That att yo<sup>r</sup> retourne wee may receiue from yo<sup>u</sup> a iust accompt of eu<sup>r</sup>y pticuler to be awnswered accordinge to the Invoice nowe deliu<sup>r</sup>ed vnto yo<sup>u</sup>, And howe the same haue beene spent and disposed of.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">Anthonie Hippon <em>Pieter Floris Lucas Antheumes</em> Robt Browne Tho: Essington</p></div><figure class="archival-plate" id="fig-020"><img src="/figures/eic-first-letter-book-1600-1619/fig-020_p0471.webp" alt="Round seal or stamp containing the text: locus sigillo." loading="lazy"><figcaption>Round seal or stamp containing the text: locus sigillo.<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-110" href="#sec-08-fn-110" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-110" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup></figcaption></figure><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">A Comission<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-111" href="#sec-08-fn-111" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-111" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> graunted by vs the Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> Deputy &amp; Committees for the Company of Marchaunte of London tradeinge to the East Indies to be obserued and kept by <em>Anthonie Hippon</em> Cap<sup>tne</sup>: and <em>Pieter Floris</em>, <em>Lucas Antheumes</em> and <em>Robert Browne</em> Marchaunte, o<sup>r</sup> principall <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-472" data-page="472" data-folio="380" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n471_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 380">[p. 380]</button> ffactors together w<sup>th</sup> Thomas Essington, Symon Euans, <em>Adam Denton</em> and <em>George Chauncey</em> the rest of o<sup>r</sup> ffactor<sup>s</sup> nowe ymployed in this o<sup>r</sup> <em>Seauenth Voyadge</em>.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">I.</aside></div><p class="prose-p"><span class="sc">Inprimis</span> Whereas we the Marchauntę abouesaid w<sup>th</sup> o<sup>r</sup> greate chardge and trouble haue p<sup>r</sup>pared a shipp called the <em>Globe</em> for a voyadge by Godę grace in her to be made to the <em>Bay of Bengalla</em> &amp; other places in at or aboute the East Indies and haue furnished the same in sufficient and plentifull manner, w<sup>th</sup> men tackle victualls and all other needfull prouisions fitt for such a voyadge, And haueinge beene pswaded by the good reporte of diuers, and alsoe of an espetiall trust and Confidence wee haue of the integritie, wisdome and resolũcõns of o<sup>r</sup> loueinge freinde <em>Anthonie Hippon</em>, <em>Peter Floris</em>, <em>Lucas Antheumes</em> and <em>Robert Browne</em> doe appointe and authorize the said <em>Anthony Hippon</em>, to be o<sup>r</sup> cheef Capt and Maister duringe the said voyadge and ordeine him to haue the full gou<sup>r</sup>nm<sup>t</sup> over the shipp and Marriners, And <em>Peter Floris</em>, <em>Lucas Antheumes</em> and <em>Robert Browne</em> to be o<sup>r</sup> principall Marchauntę and ffactors. And haue appointed <em>Tho: Essington</em>, <em>Symon Euans</em>, <em>Adam Denton</em>, <em>George Chauncey</em> o<sup>r</sup> ffactors to be assistaunt to them, Ouer all w<sup>ch</sup> said Marchauntę and ffactors the Captaine shall haue noe Comand nor authoritie, neither by water nor land in any matters conc<sup>r</sup>ninge trade.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">2 Item Thatt all money and Marchandize w<sup>ch</sup> shall be shippt here, by the Gou<sup>r</sup>nor and Comp: shall be kept by the Captaine he geiueinge a receipt for the same, w<sup>ch</sup> foresaid receipt shall be kept by <em>Peter Floris</em>, <em>Lucas Antheumes</em> and <em>Robert Browne</em> thereby to demand the said money and goodę when they shall come to those places where they shall vse the same, and then the same shall be deliu<sup>r</sup>ed by the Capt vnto them from tyme to tyme duringe the said voyadge.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">3 Item To the end that <em>Peter Floris</em>, <em>Lucas Antheumes</em> and <em>Robert Browne</em> may the better pforme their busines in their trade (the w<sup>ch</sup> wee doe referr vnto their discrecõns []) Wee doe therefore expreslie chardge yo<sup>u</sup> <em>Thomas Essington</em>, <em>Symon Euans</em>, <em>Adam Denton</em>, and <em>George Chauncey</em> to be readie att all tymes to goe either by water or land wheresoeu<sup>r</sup> o<sup>r</sup> said principall Marchauntę <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-473" data-page="473" data-folio="381" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n472_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 381">[p. 381]</button> shall direct yo<sup>u</sup> and requier the same for the Companies occasions.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">4 Item We will that the Captaine shall cause the boatę or Sciffes to be alwaies in a readines, to be ymployed to all such places as o<sup>r</sup> principall Marchauntę shall appointe them, and not to dispose of them any otherwise soe longe as they shall haue occasion to vse them.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">5 Item such Marchandize wares and money as the Captaine shall deliu<sup>r</sup> vnto the handę of the principall Marchauntę, he shall take a receipt from them there, And such wares and Marchandize as shall be brought into the shipp by the foresaid principall Marchauntę <em>Peter Floris, Lucas Antheumes &amp; Robert Browne</em> or such as shall succeede in their places beinge Marked w<sup>th</sup> the Companies m<sup>r</sup>ke, they shall take a receipt of the Capt<sup>ne</sup>: for the same, and this shall be done soe often as any goodę shall be dischargded forth of the shipp or laden into the shipp. And the Captaine to be carefull that the goodę be well p<sup>r</sup>serued and kept drie, To w<sup>ch</sup> end he shall haue a spetiall care, to looke often tymes vnto the overlopps thatt yt be well kept.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">6. Item The principall Marchauntę shall neither doe nor pretend to begynne to doe any thinge (w<sup>ch</sup> is w<sup>th</sup>out the Compasse of their tradeinge) w<sup>th</sup>out the Counsell of the Captaine and other ffactors, whether ytt be in setling any ffactorie, or any contract or agreem<sup>t</sup> to be made w<sup>th</sup> any kinge or Potentate in the Indies, neither shall the Captaine haue any authoritie on the land, to haue any thinge to doe w<sup>th</sup> the trade of Marchandize. But therein whollie wee doe authorize and putt in trust o<sup>r</sup> principall Marchauntę Peter ffloris, Lucas Antheumes and <em>Robert Browne</em> to be cheefe Comanders on the Land to the end they may pforme their busines accordinge to their owne invention. And Thomas Essington, Symon Evans, Adam Denton, &amp; George Chauncey shall be w<sup>th</sup> them to assiste them both in keepinge bookes of accomptę, as alsoe the Cashe. Butt not meete to disburse any money (eyther in the chardges or otherwyse) w<sup>th</sup>out the knowledge and expresse Comandement of the principall Marchauntę.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">7 Item Itt is expreslie ordered, that by agenerall Consent of you the Captaine Marchauntę ffactors &amp; Marriners some good order be by yo<sup>u</sup> taken, and sett downe in writeing, soe soone as <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-474" data-page="474" data-folio="382" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n473_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 382">[p. 382]</button> yo<sup>u</sup> come to Sea, conc<sup>r</sup>ninge the disposeinge of dead mens goode in the voyadge, either by Comittinge the same by Inuentorie to the pticular keepinge of some men in the shipps &amp; this vpon Conuenient considerañōns for keepinge the same, or by sale of soe much thereof att the mayne maste, as may not exceede a third parte of the buyers wages, for either buyinge or borrowinge to preuent such preiudice to o<sup>r</sup> Company as wee haue felt, by Marriners buyinge soe much as their whole wages came vnto whereby they haue not onlie forsaken o<sup>r</sup> shipps before their dischardge butt alsoe depriued vs of meanes to redresse such wronges as they haue many wayes done vs in the voyadge. Alsoe that there be noe Dealinge by exchaunge betweene ptie and ptie neither money for money, com̄oditie for money, or com̄oditie for com̄oditie outwarde nor homewardes, for such exchaunge giues way for them to take and purloine of o<sup>r</sup> goode. ffor preuenc̃on whereof wee order that neither the Purser, nor any other doe register, witnes, or take knowledge of any such contract by Exchaunge. And noe man to be admitted meanes to bringe goode abourd vpon paine of forfeeture to the Company of that w<sup>ch</sup> shall be soe brought in, And yf any ffactor shall happen to die, his bookes accompte and other thinges are to be ordered by the Captaine, and safelie reserued and brought home for England (an Inuentorie first taken thereof and registred in the pursers booke, to bee witnessed by twoe or three others of the Companie[)]. And that eu<sup>r</sup>y Marriner or other in the shipp, doe sett their hande or marke to the said orders mentioned in this 7<sup>th</sup> article.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">8 Item If the principall Marchaunte shall haue occasion to vse 5 or 6 Marriners or more to helpe to watch &amp; keepe the goode on land in their howses, then yo<sup>u</sup> Captaine <em>Anthonie Hippon</em> shall vpon there demand lett them haue soe many as they shall need and those to be of the best and trustiest men alsoe.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">9 Item To the end that the Company may be the better in all thinge iustlie dealt w<sup>th</sup>all, and the true and vpright carriadge of the busines may be made manifest and appeare plainlie The foresaid principall Marchaunte <em>Peter Floris</em>, <em>Lucas Antheumes</em> and Rob̄t Browne shall keepe a iust and true accompt of all that shall be done by way of Marchandizinge And shall doe nothinge <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-475" data-page="475" data-folio="383" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n474_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 383">[p. 383]</button> att any tyme w<sup>th</sup>out haueinge the English bookep<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-112" href="#sec-08-fn-112" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-112" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> or the Cashere by them, And shall informe instruct and showe, them all such knowledge, misterie, or arte appteineinge to their tradeinge as shall be in their power to pforme, And the said pties shall all of them eu<sup>r</sup>y eight dayes (or soe often as possiblie they may) compare the bookes and the Cashe together, to the end that yf any error should be comitted by the one or thother the same might be amended, and the bookes kept in good ballance: Soe that by life or death of any of them the state of the bookes might be found pfect.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">10. Item to the end the voyadge be not hindred through want of victualls wee doe ordeine and requier that the Captaine shall not alter the dailie allowance of victualls, w<sup>th</sup>out first haueinge taken Counsell &amp; consent of o<sup>r</sup> principall Marchaunt<span class="sc">e</span> whoe shall keepe Counter bookes of all the victualls that are spent, and that shall remaine in <em>Esse</em> the better to make their reck[on]inge for their pceedinge in the voyadge. And when yo<sup>u</sup> shall come vnto the Land then shall the victualls be lockt vp in the shipp and the principall Marchaunt<span class="sc">e</span> shall prouide fresh victualls from the land the better to lengthen out their store soe much the longer. But yf they cannot prouide any, then yo<sup>u</sup> may vse the shipps victualls as tyme and occasion shall inforce you.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">11 Item forasmuch as we are pswaded of the discretion circumspection and good vnderstandinge of o<sup>r</sup> principall Marchaunt<span class="sc">e</span> <em>Peter Floris</em>, <em>Lucas Antheumes</em>, and Ro<span class="sc">b</span>t Browne wee doe ordeyne that their opinions &amp; direccõns in the busines of trade in Marchandizeinge, be followed w<sup>th</sup>out any controlm<sup>t</sup>, As likewise wee will and requier yo<sup>u</sup> Captaine <em>Anthony Hippon</em> that yo<sup>u</sup> be alwaies readie to saile to all such landes and portes as the foresaid <em>Peter Floris</em> and <em>Lucas Antheumes</em> shall direct yo<sup>u</sup> to saile vnto and there to remayne vntill the said principall Marchaunt<span class="sc">e</span> <em>Peter Floris</em> and <em>Lucas Antheumes</em> and Ro<span class="sc">b</span>t Browne shall haue finished there busines, And then from thence to saile to such other places as the said <em>Peter Floris</em> and <em>Lucas Antheumes</em> shall requier yo<sup>u</sup> And this shall be soe often by yo<sup>u</sup> pformed as they shall desire (for therein consisteth the welfare of the whole voyadge[)]. And noe man shall counsaile or resolue to retourne <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-476" data-page="476" data-folio="384" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n475_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 384">[p. 384]</button> homewardes vntill such tyme as o<sup>r</sup> said principall m<sup>r</sup>chaunte shall haue fullie finisht there intended trade vpon forfecture of all their wages, and seveare ponishment for the same.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">12 Item The łres from his Ma<sup>tie</sup>: w<sup>ch</sup> wee haue procured vnto the Kinges Princes and Potentates of the Indies,<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-113" href="#sec-08-fn-113" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-113" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> and haue made in the names of o<sup>r</sup> principall Marchauntes <em>Peter Floris</em>, <em>Lucas Antheumes</em> and <em>Robert Browne</em> together w<sup>th</sup> <em>Thomas Essington Symon Euans</em>, &amp; <em>Adam Denton</em> o<sup>r</sup> factors and deliu<sup>r</sup>ed vnto o<sup>r</sup> said principall Marchauntes shall be presented by them, in the presence of the rest of o<sup>r</sup> ffactors, [()or soe many of o<sup>r</sup> Englishe Marchante as shall be therevnto appointed) in the name of the kinges Ma<sup>tie</sup>: of England, and for the honor of the English Nation, by them to procure such freedome both of Customes and all other thinges as they shall finde to be proffitable for the Companie, and that the Companie may thereby be trewlie informed of whatsoeu<sup>r</sup> shall be done therein.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">13 Item To the end all difficult matters may the better be passed over by good endeuors wee doe appointe a Counsell of eight psonns whereof Capteine <em>Anthonie Hippon</em> to be president (in matters of the shipp or for iustice []), and <em>Peter Floris Lucas Antheumes</em> and Robt Browne w<sup>th</sup> Thomas Essington <em>Symon Euans</em> and <em>Adam Denton</em> and George Chauncey being in all eight psonns, whereof one of them to recorde whatt matters shall passe, shall be a sufficient Counsell to determyn all Cryminall and Cyuell matters that shall come before them yett forasmuch as <em>Peter Floris</em> and Lucas Antheumes haue excused them selues that they are not acquainted w<sup>th</sup> the lawes and iustice of o<sup>r</sup> land therefore they shall be exempted and not required to come to the Counsell when any matter of Iustice is to be handled (for such matters shall be determyned by the rest of the Counsell) But yf yt be for any matters on land (or for any thinge w<sup>ch</sup> conserneth trade) then one of the principall Marchaunte shall be President either <em>Peter Floris</em>, <em>Lucas Antheumes</em> or Robt Browne as they shall thinke fitt amongst themselues to determyne for that purpose, And the foresaid Counsell shall allowe of the said pson for president and shall hearken to his propoticõns, and take Counsaile, and resolue vpon the same as occasion shall be offerred.</p></div><div class="page-rail-marker" id="page-477"><button type="button" class="folio-trigger" data-page="477" data-folio="385" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n476_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect scan for page 385">[p. 385]</button></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">14 Item yf yt should happen that o<sup>r</sup> principall Marchauntę <em>Peter Floris</em>, <em>Lucas Antheumes</em> and Robt Browne, should not carrie them selues well in this voyadge, there faultes and misdoeinges shall be written vpp &amp; deliu<sup>r</sup>ed over vnto the Company in England for to be censured by them, butt they shall not be displaced duringe the voyadge.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">15. Item the Captaine shall haue aspetiall care that noe man whatsoeu<sup>r</sup>, of whatt quallitie or Condic̃on soeuer he bee (his owne pson beinge alsoe included herein) shall vse any priuate or secreete trade, vpon peine and<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-114" href="#sec-08-fn-114" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-114" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> forfecture of all that shall be found by any man soe doinge, and beside shall endure arbitrary ponishment, And wee requier the principall Marchauntę to haue a spetiall care hereof on the land, and to preuent yt soe much as is possible, And the Captaine and other Marchauntę shall alsoe haue a vigelent Eye &amp; good reguard on the principall Marchauntę to the end the Companie may be the better serued (whoe haue beene att such excessiue chardge) &amp; reape the benefitt of the trade discou<sup>r</sup>ed by their expences.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">16 Item fforasmuch as wee are c<sup>r</sup>teinelie enformed, that when twoe of o<sup>r</sup> principall Marchauntę <em>Peter Floris</em> &amp; Lucas Antheumes shall come into the Indies they are like to be much hated by the <em>Dutchmen</em> there whoe will seeke to doe them wronge and all the villanie they may, therefore wee expreslie chardge yo<sup>u</sup> the Captaine (and all others that are in o<sup>r</sup> shipp and voyadge[]) to defend and preserue them from wronges w<sup>th</sup>all the power and strength yo<sup>u</sup> may against any that shall offer to doe them wronge or iniurie: Or shall goe aboute to hinder them in their busines, w<sup>ch</sup> might much indanger o<sup>r</sup> voyadge, The like Comandem<sup>t</sup> wee doe expreslie giue vnto all others that are in any of o<sup>r</sup> shipps &amp; imployed in o<sup>r</sup> busines in any places whatsoeuer.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">17. Item for avoiedinge Confusion in the gou<sup>r</sup>nm<sup>t</sup> of o<sup>r</sup> people, and for that nothinge is soe vncerteine as the state of mans life: Wee ordeine that yf God shall call yo<sup>u</sup> Captaine <em>Anthonie Hippon</em> duringe this voyadge, out of the world, (w<sup>ch</sup> God defend) then wee Comitt the gou<sup>r</sup>nment of o<sup>r</sup> shipp &amp; Marriners therin to such as wee haue nominated in a writtinge subscribed by the Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> &amp; sealed w<sup>th</sup> the seale of the Company putt vp <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-478" data-page="478" data-folio="386" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n477_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 386">[p. 386]</button> into a Boxe N<sup>o</sup>: 1 sealed w<sup>th</sup> hard waxe &amp; deliu<sup>r</sup>ed to Captaine Anthonie Hippon to be kept close and not opened, soe longe as the said Captaine Anthony Hippon shall lyue Butt yf yt shall please god to take him out of this world that shall succeede Captaine Anthonie Hippon then wee ordeyne that o<sup>r</sup> Master and Marriners in o<sup>r</sup> said shipp shall submitt them selues to the gou<sup>r</sup>nm<sup>t</sup> and Comand of such pson or psons as wee haue nomynated therevnto in a writeinge sealed vp in a Boxe N<sup>o</sup>: 2 &amp; remayneinge in the Custodie of the said Captaine Anthonie Hippon &amp; those that shall succeede him. Prouided alwayes that noe pson whatsoeu<sup>r</sup> w<sup>ch</sup> shall succeede in the aforesaid place of gou<sup>r</sup>nm<sup>t</sup>, shall demand, be allowed, or expect the wages of his predecessor, butt shall content himselfe w<sup>th</sup> his first wages promised him by the Companie. Or referr himselfe to be Considered of accordinge to his deserte (att his retourne) by the Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> and Comittees of the Companie for the tyme beinge.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">18 Item If yt should happen (as God forbid) that o<sup>r</sup> shipp should haue waunte of men or other necessarie meanes (w<sup>ch</sup> such longe voyadges are subiect vnto) Then we will that yo<sup>u</sup> desire to be releeued by any shipp yo<sup>u</sup> shall meete w<sup>ch</sup> is appteineinge vnto vs, [()or any other Englishe shipp whatsoeu<sup>r</sup>) And wee doe hereby straightlie chardge all Generalls Captaines Masters or any other Officers (in any of the shippes whatsoeu<sup>r</sup> appteineinge to the Company of Marchaunt<em>e</em> of London tradeinge to the East Indies[)] whome yo<sup>u</sup> shall chaunce to meete in any such yo<sup>r</sup> distresse thatt they supplie ayde and releyue yo<sup>u</sup> w<sup>th</sup> such men or any other necessaries w<sup>ch</sup> yo<sup>u</sup> shall haue neede of, and they may conuenientlie spare yo<sup>u</sup> w<sup>th</sup>out endaungeringe them selues and their shipps. And that yo<sup>u</sup> pforme like good office to any of our shipps that yo<sup>u</sup> shall meete duringe the said voyadge whome yo<sup>u</sup> shall finde distressed.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">19 Item Whereas we haue sent alonge w<sup>th</sup> yo<sup>u</sup> one chest of money conteyneinge fower thowsand Ryalls of 8. w<sup>ch</sup> wee intend for a stocke to be lefte at <em>Bantam</em> against the tyme that yt shall please God to send o<sup>r</sup> shippinge thether Wee doe therefore ordeine that o<sup>r</sup> principall Marchaunt<em>e Peter Floris</em> Lucas <em>Antheumes</em> and Robt Browne shall leaue one or twoe of o<sup>r</sup> ffactors att <em>Bantam</em> [()whome they by their experience shall finde most <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-479" data-page="479" data-folio="387" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n478_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 387">[p. 387]</button> fitt to vndertake the same seruice) together w<sup>th</sup> the said Cheste of money intended for the foresaid stocke (or the valewe thereof) as alsoe halfe of the proceede w<sup>ch</sup> shall be raysed by the ymploym<sup>t</sup> thereof in their said voyadge thither, where o<sup>r</sup> said ffactors shall remaine, and make ymploym<sup>t</sup> of the said stocke and proceede thereof in such Comodities as by your discretion shall be thought most necessarie to be transported w<sup>th</sup> our shipping for o<sup>r</sup> best aduantage to such places as shall be found conuenient, And the other halfe of the gaine to be for the accompt of the <em>Globe</em>.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">20. Item yf yo<sup>u</sup> shall finde in yo<sup>r</sup> trauaile, or can conuenientlie come by any faire birdes or beastes, or any other thinge fitt for vs to present to his Ma<sup>tie</sup>, or any of the noble Lorde that are o<sup>r</sup> hono<sup>ble</sup> freinde, Wee pray and requier yo<sup>u</sup> to be carefull to prouide, &amp; send the same w<sup>th</sup> espetiall chardge to some carefull man or men that retourne, to see them carefullie kept and deliu<sup>r</sup>ed to vs. And w<sup>th</sup>all yf you shall knowe any of the Marriners or others to haue any such w<sup>ch</sup> yo<sup>u</sup> cannot ꝑcure from them for reason then that yo<sup>u</sup> send vs notice thereof, to the end we may take order theirein accordinglie.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">And soe haueing remembred what wee thinke needfull for yo<sup>r</sup> direcčon in this voyadge, and relyinge vpon yo<sup>r</sup> dilligence and care in pursuing o<sup>r</sup> busines to o<sup>r</sup> most aduantage Wee comitt yo<sup>u</sup> to the ptecčon of the Almightie God, whome wee beseech to blesse yo<sup>r</sup> endevo<sup>rs</sup> and graunte yo<sup>u</sup> a safe passadge and safe retourne to all o<sup>r</sup> comfortes.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">Item Whereas wee haue giuen direcčons that one or twoe of o<sup>r</sup> ffactors should be lefte at Bantam, whome o<sup>r</sup> principall Marchaunte <em>Peter Floris</em> Lucas Antheumes and Robt Browne by their experience shall finde most fitt for o<sup>r</sup> seruice there, vpon further aduise and Consideračon Wee doe now ordeyne that Thomas <em>Essington</em> shall passe from porte to porte w<sup>th</sup> yo<sup>u</sup> dureing the said voyadge, and retourne backe againe w<sup>th</sup> yo<sup>u</sup> in the same shipp into England: And that yo<sup>u</sup> take the oportunitie (from the most conuenient place yo<sup>u</sup> shall arriue att) to send one or twoe of o<sup>r</sup> ffactors in such conuenient shippinge as [yo<sup>u</sup>] may procure) to carrie his Ma<sup>te</sup> łres vnto the kinge of Iapan, together w<sup>th</sup> such stocke of Marchandize &amp; Comodities as wee haue appointed o<sup>r</sup> said principall Marchaunte to prouide for that purpose And yf <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-480" data-page="480" data-folio="388" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n479_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 388">[p. 388]</button> you should be driuē to any want of ffactors during this o<sup>r</sup> said voyadge then yo<sup>u</sup> may ymploye the purser or his mate, or both yf occasion require, soe that yo<sup>u</sup> be espetiallie carefull to supply the place of the purser w<sup>th</sup> some sufficient pson to vndertake that voyadge.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">Memorandū It is ordeyned by vs that yf God should take out of this world o<sup>r</sup> loueinge freind Robt Browne, Then wee doe referr y<sup>u</sup> vnto a Boxe N<sup>o</sup>. 3. to vnderstand o<sup>r</sup> will and pleasure, Whome wee haue appointed to succeede him in place, of o<sup>r</sup> principall ffactor, to be ioyned w<sup>th</sup> Peter ffloris and Lucas Antheumes, o<sup>r</sup> other principall ffactors.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">Anthonie Hippon Peter ffloris. Lucas Antheumes Robt Browne Thomas Essington</p></div><h4 class="sec-h3">Most highe and mightie kinge:<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-115" href="#sec-08-fn-115" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-115" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup></h4><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">Twoe of this draught are to be written wth Blanckes and ingrossed</aside></div><p class="prose-p">AS there is nothinge w<sup>ch</sup> encreaseth more the glory &amp; dignitie of Sou<sup>r</sup>eigne Princes vpon earth then to extend their renowne vnto farr discident Nations, Soe haueing vnderstoode of late yeares from some of o<sup>r</sup> loueinge Subiectę, that haue traded into diu<sup>r</sup>s Countries neere adioyneinge vnto yo<sup>rs</sup>, of the reputation and greatnes of yo<sup>r</sup> power and dominion. Wee haue incourradged o<sup>r</sup> said Subiectę to vndertake a voyadge into yo<sup>r</sup> Country, aswell to sollicitt yo<sup>r</sup> freindshipp and Amity w<sup>th</sup> vs, as to enteyne<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-116" href="#sec-08-fn-116" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-116" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> such Comodities of each others Countries as may be most of vse the one to the other, being nothinge doubtfull but such will be yo<sup>r</sup> princelie magnanimity and disposic̃on to imbrace this o<sup>r</sup> desire, and not onlie to receiue o<sup>r</sup> people w<sup>th</sup> yo<sup>r</sup> accustomed benignitie and favo<sup>r</sup>, but <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-481" data-page="481" data-folio="389" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n480_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 389">[p. 389]</button> for their better incourradgm<sup>t</sup> to afford yo<sup>r</sup> Ryall protecc̃on for the setlinge of a ffactorie there, w<sup>th</sup> such securitie and lib̃tie of Comerce, as shall be most conuenient for the aduancem<sup>t</sup> of the mutuall proffitt and Comoditie of each others subiectę; Wherein for o<sup>r</sup> parte wee doe willinglie offer o<sup>r</sup> selues &amp; the lib̃tie of o<sup>r</sup> kingdomes and Countries when soeuer any of yo<sup>r</sup> subiectę shall vndertake to haue any communicãcon w<sup>th</sup> vs. And in the meane tyme, as a pledge of o<sup>r</sup> Royall inclinãcon and desier towardę yo<sup>u</sup>, wee haue sent yo<sup>u</sup> a small present by our seruant <strong>A. B.</strong> w<sup>ch</sup> we pray yo<sup>u</sup> to accept, as an introducc̃on to that freindshipp w<sup>ch</sup> we hope will fructifie and encrease to each others Contentm<sup>t</sup>.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">Giuen att o<sup>r</sup> Pallace of Westm̄ the &amp;c.</p></div><h3 class="sec-h2">To the kinge of <em>Cambaia</em></h3><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">Iames by the grace of Almightie God kinge of greate Brittaine ffraunce and Ireland, defendo<sup>r</sup> of the Christian faith &amp;c: To the highe and mightie Prince the greate kinge of <em>Cambaia, Emperour</em> of Mogores &amp;c greetinge</p></div><h4 class="sec-h3">Most highe &amp; mightie Emperour.</h4><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">WEE can not but expresse vnto yo<sup>u</sup> the highe Contentm<sup>t</sup> wee haue receiued att the kinde reception and interteynem<sup>t</sup> of o<sup>r</sup> seruant Captaine William Hawkins, and his Company att yo<sup>r</sup> porte of Suratt, where they haue receiued soe many demonstrãcons by yo<sup>r</sup> principall Officers and Comanders there, of yo<sup>r</sup> desire to imbrace o<sup>r</sup> loue and freindshipp, and to interteyne a Correspondencie of peaceable trade and Comerce w<sup>th</sup> o<sup>r</sup> subiectę, as wee can not but thankfullie acknowledge the same, and reciprocallie offer the lyke effectę towardę yo<sup>u</sup> and yo<sup>r</sup> people, whensoeu<sup>r</sup> they shall vndertake to haue any Comunicãcon of trade w<sup>thin</sup> o<sup>r</sup> kingdomes and dominions: Butt wee were sorrie to vnderstand of that vnhappy <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-482" data-page="482" data-folio="390" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n481_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 390">[p. 390]</button> accident,<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-117" href="#sec-08-fn-117" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-117" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> w<sup>ch</sup> befell o<sup>r</sup> Subiecte, in their retourne from yo<sup>r</sup> Coastes towarde their Shipps, by being vnworthilie surprized w<sup>thin</sup> yo<sup>r</sup> streames by diu<sup>rs</sup> ffriggette of the Portingalls of <em>Goa</em>, Whoe vniustlie haue taken from them Nyneteene of o<sup>r</sup> Subiecte besides agreat quantitie of their Marchandize, and detaine them prisonners att <em>Goa</em>, vnder this sole pretext, as yf both yo<sup>r</sup> selfe, and yo<sup>r</sup> Countries were assubiected to the Crowne of Spaine, and therefore not lawfull for any other Christian people to trade w<sup>th</sup> yo<sup>u</sup>, w<sup>ch</sup> indignitie, thoughe Almightie God hath geuen vs power enoughe to reuenge against them, yett because ytt hath relacõn to yo<sup>r</sup> greate estate, as beinge done w<sup>thin</sup> the Compasse of yo<sup>r</sup> power and authoritie, wee haue thought good first to represent yt vnto yo<sup>u</sup>, and to seeke redresse of ytt by yo<sup>r</sup> meanes, then enter into any course of hostilitie in those partes contrary to the peaceable inclination of trade w<sup>ch</sup> o<sup>r</sup> Subiecte are willinge to interteyne w<sup>th</sup> all other nations. Notw<sup>th</sup>stand[ing] such hard successe o<sup>r</sup> subiecte are noe whitt discourradged from vndertakeinge (as nowe they doe) an other voyadge into yo<sup>r</sup> Countreys, w<sup>th</sup> three good shipps laden w<sup>th</sup> such Commodities of o<sup>r</sup> kingdomes as may be most of vse to yo<sup>r</sup> people, beinge Confident of yo<sup>r</sup> further favo<sup>r</sup> and pteccõn, w<sup>th</sup> such ym̃unities and priuiledges for their trade and Com̃erce w<sup>th</sup> yo<sup>r</sup> people, as may encourradge them more and more to fixe and contynue a firme Correspondencie there, to the encrease of proffitt of each others Subiecte. And for a pledge of o<sup>r</sup> Royall inclinacõn towarde yo<sup>u</sup>, wee haue sent yo<sup>u</sup> a small present by o<sup>r</sup> seruant <strong>A B</strong>. Generall of this o<sup>r</sup> voyadge w<sup>ch</sup> I pray yo<sup>u</sup> to accept in good parte, and to vse him w<sup>th</sup> all courtesie and respect, as is befittinge the quallitie of his place Giuen att o<sup>r</sup> Pallace of Westminster the &amp;c.</p></div><div class="page-rail-marker" id="page-483"><button type="button" class="folio-trigger" data-page="483" data-folio="391" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n482_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect scan for page 391">[p. 391]</button></div><h3 class="sec-h2">Com̄ander of <em>Cambaia</em></h3><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">Iames by the grace of Almighty God kinge of greate Brittane ffraunce and Ireland defendor of the Christian faith &amp;c: To the right hono<sup>ble</sup>: the Cheefe Com̄ander of the greatt Cittie of <em>Cambaia</em> greetinge.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">WHERE[AS] wee haue vnderstoode from some of o<sup>r</sup> Subiecte whatt greate Courteseys they haue receiued, and daily doe receaue att yo<sup>r</sup> hande, in their arriuall and settlement of their trade in the greate Cittie of <em>Cambaia</em> wee could not omitt att this second voyadge thither, butt to expresse vnto yo<sup>u</sup> o<sup>r</sup> thankfull acknowledgm<sup>t</sup> for the same w<sup>th</sup> o<sup>r</sup> constant hope of yo<sup>r</sup> further endevors towarde them, aswell for their future safeguard and protecc̃on in those places, against the violence and malice of any that should attempt to wronge them, as for their better incourradgm<sup>t</sup> to establishe and increase (as nowe they haue begun alreadie) a firme Correspondencie of trade in those partes; wherein for o<sup>r</sup> parte, wee will nott omitt any thinge that may tend to the furtheringe of soe greate a good for the aduancm<sup>t</sup> of o<sup>r</sup> Subiecte. And as for the iniurie and losse w<sup>ch</sup> o<sup>r</sup> Subiecte latelie receiued by the Portingalls of Goa (as is well knowne vnto yo<sup>u</sup>) thoughe wee want noe meanes to reuenge our selues against them, yett because wee would be loth to interrupt the peaceable course of trade in those partes, and for thatt the Iniurie doth alsoe redowne to yo<sup>u</sup>, as beinge done w<sup>th</sup>in the limitte of yo<sup>r</sup> Comand: Wee haue thought good first to requier yo<sup>r</sup> peacable intercession for the redresse thereof, before o<sup>r</sup> Subiecte should attempt any thinge hostilly against them, wherein yo<sup>u</sup> shall doe a worke worthy of yo<sup>r</sup> selfe, and by w<sup>ch</sup> yo<sup>u</sup> shall oblige o<sup>r</sup> people more and more vnto yo<sup>u</sup>. And for o<sup>r</sup> good inclinac̃on towarde yo<sup>u</sup>: wee haue sent yo<sup>u</sup> a token of o<sup>r</sup> loue and freindshipp by o<sup>r</sup> seruaunt <strong>A B</strong>. Generall of this our voyadge. Giuen att o<sup>r</sup> Pallace of Westminst<sup>r</sup> the &amp;c:.</p></div><div class="page-rail-marker" id="page-484"><button type="button" class="folio-trigger" data-page="484" data-folio="392" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n483_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect scan for page 392">[p. 392]</button></div><h4 class="sec-h3">To the K: of <em>Succatora</em>.</h4><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">Iames by the grace of Almightie God kinge of greate Brittaine ffraunce and Ireland Defendor of the Christian faith &amp;c: To the highe and Mightie Prince the kinge of <em>Succatora</em>.</p></div><h4 class="sec-h3">Most mightie Kinge</h4><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">HAUEINGE vnderstoode from some of o<sup>r</sup> loueinge Subiectę that in their course of Nauigačõn, and traffique into diu<sup>r</sup>s partes of the East Indies they haue alsoe visitted yo<sup>r</sup> Coastes and Territories where they haue not onelie refreshed them selues of such necessaries as are belongeinge to soe greate a Nauigation, butt haue alsoe receiued the best interteynem<sup>t</sup> thatt could be expected: Wee hould ytt o<sup>r</sup> parte to acknowledge the same w<sup>th</sup> thankes, and to ymbrace yo<sup>r</sup> Amity and freindshipp w<sup>th</sup>all the respectę of hono<sup>r</sup> thatt may tend to the establishinge and encreaseinge of the same to o<sup>r</sup> mutuall contentation and aduancm<sup>t</sup> of proffitt of each others subiectes: Wherein for o<sup>r</sup> parte, as wee doe readilie offer o<sup>r</sup> selues, and any thinge thatt shall be in o<sup>r</sup> power, soe wee doe expect the lyke effectę from yo<sup>u</sup> to redound vpon o<sup>r</sup> Subiectę, in yo<sup>r</sup> good vsage of them, and affourdinge them yo<sup>r</sup> Princelie ffavors and ptecčõn against such as should goe aboute to harme them. And for a pledge of this o<sup>r</sup> good inclination towardę yo<sup>u</sup>, wee haue sent yo<sup>u</sup> this small Present by o<sup>r</sup> seruaunt <strong>A B.</strong> w<sup>ch</sup> wee pray yo<sup>u</sup> to accept in good parte. Gyuen att our Pallace of Westminster the &amp;c:</p></div><h4 class="sec-h3">To the k: of <em>Bantam</em>.</h4><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">Iames by the grace of God kinge of greate Brittaine ffraunce and Ireland Defendor of the Christian faith &amp;c: To the most Highe and mightie Prince the Kinge of Bantam Greeteinge.</p></div><div class="page-rail-marker" id="page-485"><button type="button" class="folio-trigger" data-page="485" data-folio="393" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n484_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect scan for page 393">[p. 393]</button></div><h3 class="sec-h2">Right highe and mightie Prince.</h3><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p"><span class="sc">Itt</span> hath beene noe small contentm<sup>t</sup> vnto vs, to vnderstand from o<sup>r</sup> Subiecte retourned from their last voyadge into yo<sup>r</sup> Countreys, both howe favorablie they haue beene vsed by yo<sup>u</sup>, and yo<sup>r</sup> people, and howe willinge yo<sup>u</sup> haue beene to interteyne o<sup>r</sup> loue and freindshipp, both w<sup>ch</sup>, yo<sup>u</sup> haue beene Contented to confirme vnto vs, by a Present of fower balls<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-118" href="#sec-08-fn-118" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-118" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> of silke, w<sup>ch</sup> yo<sup>u</sup> haue sent vnto vs, ffor w<sup>ch</sup> wee cannott but retourne vnto yo<sup>u</sup>, o<sup>r</sup> harty thankes w<sup>th</sup>all the kinde demonstraẽons of o<sup>r</sup> reciprocall desier to encrease o<sup>r</sup> Amytie and Correspondency w<sup>th</sup> yo<sup>u</sup>, as farr as the same may be honorable vnto vs, and pffitable to each others Subiecte; In Confidence whereof, o<sup>r</sup> Subiecte haueinge vndertaken a newe voyadge into yo<sup>r</sup> Countrie for the reestablishinge of their trade and traffique w<sup>th</sup> yo<sup>r</sup> Subiecte, wee hope yo<sup>u</sup> will Contynue vnto them yo<sup>r</sup> accustomed favour, and afford them such liberties and immunities there, as they may be thereby both incourradged in their greate adventures, and secured from oppression or wronge of any that would goe aboute to harme them, And in thankfullnes of theis yo<sup>r</sup> kindnesses to our people, wee haue sent yo<sup>u</sup> a token by this bearer o<sup>r</sup> seruant 𝔄 𝔅 Generall of o<sup>r</sup> present voyadge. Gyven att o<sup>r</sup> Pallace of Westminster, the .</p></div><h3 class="sec-h2">K. of Tedore.</h3><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">Iames by the grace of Almightie God kinge of great Brittane, ffraunce and Ireland Defendor of the Christian faith &amp;c: To the most highe and mighty Prince, the kinge of Tedore Greetinge</p></div><h4 class="sec-h3">Most highe and mightie kinge</h4><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p"><span class="sc">As</span> wee haue taken greate contentm<sup>t</sup> to vnderstand from o<sup>r</sup> Marchaunte att their retourne from their last voyadge out of yo<sup>r</sup> Country, howe kindlie and favorablie they haue beene interteyned by yo<sup>u</sup>, and yo<sup>r</sup> people in their peacable trade, Soe wee cannot but reiteratiuely <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-486" data-page="486" data-folio="394" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n485_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 394">[p. 394]</button> expresse o<sup>r</sup> thankes for the same, w<sup>th</sup>all the respecte of honor, that can be expected from vs, being ready both to ymbrace your freindshipp and Amity, and to establish a constaunt entercourse of trade and traffique betwixt each others Subiecte, ffor w<sup>ch</sup> purpose our Subiecte haueinge vndertaken a new voyadge into yo<sup>r</sup> Countrey, wee could not butt take occasion to salute yo<sup>u</sup> &amp; to pray yo<sup>u</sup> to contynue yo<sup>r</sup> favor towarde them, w<sup>th</sup> such Condic̃ons of safetie and Imunitie to their psonns, shipps, and goode, as they may be both encouraged in their iust and honest course, and protected against any wronge and vyolence of any thatt would goe aboute to harme them. And for a pledge of o<sup>r</sup> princelie disposition towarde yo<sup>u</sup> Wee haue sent yo<sup>u</sup> a token by our servaunt 𝕬 𝕭 Generall of o<sup>r</sup> Marchaunte present voyadge Gyven att o<sup>r</sup> Pallace of Westm̄ the      .</p></div><h3 class="sec-h2"><em>Gouernor of Aden.</em></h3><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">Iames by the grace of Almightie God kinge of great Brittane ffraunce and Ireland defendor of the Christian ffaith &amp;c: To the right honorable Lord the Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> of the Cittie of Aden Greetinge</p></div><h4 class="sec-h3">Right honorable Lord.</h4><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">THE entercourse of Amity and freindshipp, w<sup>ch</sup> hath beene theis ma[n]y yeares betweene vs and o<sup>r</sup> Predecessors, and the mightie howse of <em>Ottoman</em>, confirmed by soe many demonstrãcõns of free comerce and liberty of trade through each others kingdomes and domynions, hath incourradged o<sup>r</sup> Marchaunte to frequent alsoe those partes, where yo<sup>u</sup> haue chardge, and the rather because they haue latelie vnderstoode thatt twoe of their shipps in their course of Nauigac̃on into diu<sup>r</sup>s partes of the East Indies, haue alsoe beene in the mouth of the red Sea, and founde oportunitie of traffique by enterteyneinge each others Comodities, w<sup>ch</sup> hath enduced them nowe to vndertake this voyadge, and prayed our intercession vnto yo<sup>u</sup> for them, that they might be favorablie receiued, and enioye <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-487" data-page="487" data-folio="395" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n486_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 395">[p. 395]</button> in those partes alsoe such libtie of trade w<sup>th</sup> safety and good vsage for their psonns shipps and goode as they nowe haue and inioye in the hither parte of the Grand Signio<sup>rs</sup> dominions, w<sup>ch</sup> wee doe not doubt but yo<sup>u</sup> will be willinge to afford vnto them, and the rather, because they bringe w<sup>th</sup> them a safe Conduct<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-119" href="#sec-08-fn-119" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-119" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> from the Grand Signior in that behalfe. And of o<sup>r</sup> good will and inclina~on towardes yo<sup>u</sup>, wee haue sent yo<sup>u</sup> a small token by o<sup>r</sup> Servaunt <strong>A B</strong> w<sup>ch</sup> wee pray yo<sup>u</sup> to accept in good parte. And soe wee Comitt yo<sup>u</sup> to the protec~on of the most highe and mightie God, the Creator and preseruer of all thinges. ffrom o<sup>r</sup> Pallace att Westminster the</p></div><h3 class="sec-h2"><em>Sabander of Nero.</em></h3><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">Iames by the grace of Almightie God kinge of greate Brittaine ffraunce and Ireland defendor of the Christian faith &amp;c: To the right ho<sup>ble</sup>: the Sabander of <em>Nero</em> Greeteinge</p></div><h4 class="sec-h3">Right hono<sup>ble</sup> Lord.</h4><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p"><span class="sc">Although</span> wee haue heretofore by o<sup>r</sup> letters vnto yo<sup>u</sup> expressed our acknowledgm<sup>t</sup> for the favorable vsadge and interteynem<sup>t</sup> yo<sup>u</sup> haue giuen to o<sup>r</sup> Subiecte in their trade and traffique w<sup>th</sup>in yo<sup>r</sup> Countreys, yett they haueinge nowe vndertaken alyke voyadge thither againe Wee can not but reiterate o<sup>r</sup> former acknowledgm<sup>t</sup> as a matter honorable vnto you, and acceptable to vs: And the rather because wee doe not doubt, but thereby yo<sup>u</sup> will be more and more induced to afford vnto o<sup>r</sup> Subiecte such further favour and protec~on w<sup>th</sup>all Condi~ons of honor and safetie to their psons, Shipps and goode, as may incourradge them to contynue their trade and Comerce w<sup>th</sup> yo<sup>r</sup> people, wherein yo<sup>u</sup> shall doe a worke worthy of yo<sup>r</sup> selfe, and much aduantagious to the proffitt of each others Subiecte. And for a demonstra~on of o<sup>r</sup> fauorable inclynation towarde you, and embraceinge of yo<sup>r</sup> loue and freindshipp wee haue sent yo<sup>u</sup> a token by o<sup>r</sup> servaunt <strong>A B</strong> Generall of this o<sup>r</sup> voyadge: w<sup>ch</sup> wee <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-488" data-page="488" data-folio="396" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n487_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 396">[p. 396]</button> pray yo<sup>u</sup> to accept in good pte, and to honor and respect him accordinge to the quallitie of his place Giuen att o<sup>r</sup> Pallace of Westminst<sup>r</sup> this &amp;c:</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">April 4<sup>o</sup>: 1611.</aside></div><p class="prose-p">A Comission<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-120" href="#sec-08-fn-120" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-120" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> sett downe by vs the Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> Deputy and Comittees for the Marchauntꬴ of London tradeinge to the East Indies for better direc̃cõn of o<sup>r</sup> loueinge freindꬴ Iohn Sairis<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-121" href="#sec-08-fn-121" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-121" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> Captaine and cheefe Comander and Gabriell Towerson<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-122" href="#sec-08-fn-122" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-122" role="doc-noteref">3</a></sup> Captaine of the Hector o<sup>r</sup> principall and cheefe Marchauntꬴ &amp; others the Marchauntꬴ M<sup>rs</sup>: &amp; Marriners in this o<sup>r</sup> intended eight Voyadge to the East Indies w<sup>ch</sup> wee humblie beseech Almightie God to prosp w<sup>th</sup> a happie Conclusion.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="sidenote-stack"><aside class="shoulder-sidenote">I</aside></div><p class="prose-p">INPRIMIS Wheares wee the Marchauntꬴ abouesaid w<sup>th</sup> o<sup>r</sup> greate chardge and trouble haue p<sup>r</sup>pared three shipps (to saye) <em>The Cloue Iames ffoster: M<sup>r</sup>: The Hector Thom: ffuller M<sup>r</sup>: The Thomas Rich: Dawes M<sup>r</sup>:</em> for avoyadge by godꬴ grace in them to be made to the East Indies, haue furnished the same sufficientlie and in plentifull manner w<sup>th</sup> men tackle and all other needfull puisions for such a voyadge. Of an espetiall trust &amp; Confidence wee haue of the integritie, wisdome, and resoluc̃on of o<sup>r</sup> loueinge freind Iohn Sairis gen̄ doe appointe and authorize him cheefe Capt &amp; Comander to goe in the Cloue, &amp; to Comand all o<sup>r</sup> said shipps &amp; in the men in them, haueinge p̄cured him sufficient authoritie from o<sup>r</sup> sou<sup>r</sup>eigne Lord the kinges Ma<sup>tie</sup>: for that purpose<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-123" href="#sec-08-fn-123" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-123" role="doc-noteref">4</a></sup>: Not <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-489" data-page="489" data-folio="397" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n488_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 397">[p. 397]</button> doubtinge butt thatt he will soe behaue himselfe in his chardge as he may be both feared and loued: And that he will w<sup>th</sup>all his dilligence and endevors prosecute our designes &amp;
