labour to bringe this costlie voyadge to a happie end.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">2 Item wee doe appointe and ordeyne o<sup>r</sup> loueinge freind Gabriell Towerson geñ to be Captaine of the Hector and men, &amp; to take his passadge in the said shipp And Tempest Peacocke to be cheefe Marchaunte in the Thomas: All w<sup>ch</sup> Comanders Marchauntę ffactors Masters &amp; Marriners we trust will soe carrie them selues w<sup>th</sup> due respect one to an other, &amp; obedience to eu<sup>r</sup>y one of their Superio<sup>rs</sup> as loue and kindnes may be continued on all sides, and all whollie applie their witte and endeuo<sup>rs</sup> for the due pformance of this voyadge in the best manner they may for the good of the Companie</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">3 Item and for that religeous gou<sup>r</sup>nm<sup>t</sup> doth best binde men to pforme their duties it is principally to be cared for: That prayers be said eu<sup>r</sup>y morninge and eueninge in eu<sup>r</sup>y shipp &amp; the whole company called therevnto w<sup>th</sup> dilligent Eyes that none be wantinge soe as all may ioyntlie w<sup>th</sup> reuerence and humilitie pray vnto Almightie God to blesse and preserue them from all dangers in this longe and tedious voyadge: ffor the better pformance whereof wee haue deliu<sup>r</sup>ed to each of the pursers a Bible wherein is conteyned the booke of comon prayer.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">4 Item That noe blasphemeinge of God, swereinge, thefte, drunkennes, or otherlike disorders be vsed but that the same be seu<sup>r</sup>lie ponished And thatt noe diceinge or other vnlawfull games be admitted for that most com̄only the same is the beginninge of quarrellinge, and many tymes occasion of murther, a iust puocation of Godę wrath &amp; vengeaunce, from w<sup>ch</sup> the Lord deliu<sup>r</sup> vs all. Therefore ytt shall be needfull that such orders be sett vpp &amp; published in eu<sup>r</sup>y shipp, and such ponishm<sup>t</sup> appointed for such offences as may giue notice to eu<sup>r</sup>y man whatt he ought to avoied.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">5 Item that spetiall order be giuen that noe liquor be spilt in the ballast of the shipps, or filthines be left w<sup>th</sup>in bourde w<sup>ch</sup> in heate breedeth noysome smells &amp; infec̃cõn, but thereby a dilligent care be had to keepe the ou<sup>r</sup>lopps &amp; other places of the shipps cleane and sweete w<sup>ch</sup> is a notable preseruation of health: Wherein the Officers of o<sup>r</sup> shipps in former voyadges haue beene <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-490" data-page="490" data-folio="398" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n489_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 398">[p. 398]</button> much to be blamed, to the greate spoile of o<sup>r</sup> shipps &amp; breedinge of sicknes, w<sup>ch</sup> wee trust in this will be carefullie reguarded.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">6 Item Thatt the cheefe Captaine &amp; Comander &amp; Capt of the Hector, and Cape Marchaunt of the Thomas &amp; M<sup>rs</sup> of the shipps doe take due notice by Inventorie from eu<sup>r</sup>y Officer of the shipps before they depte out of the riuer of Theames of the seu<sup>r</sup>all puisions deliu<sup>r</sup>ed into each shipp. And soe soone as they shall come to Sea &amp; not before to settle the same in the chardge of the Officers appointed for thatt purpose in each shipp, vntill w<sup>ch</sup> tyme all stores are to remaine vnder locke &amp; key, &amp; that the same be entred in the pursers bookes, vnderwritten by the said Officers, to thend that such thinges as are puided for their needfull supplies may not be ymbecilled &amp; that nothinge thereof of any valewe be ymployed for any vse whatsoeu<sup>r</sup> w<sup>th</sup>out a warrant vnder the hand of the cheefe Captaine and Comander Captaine of the Hector, Cape Marchaunt of the Thomas or cheefe Marchaunt of each shipp respectiuelie directed to the Officer that hath such chardge, w<sup>ch</sup> warrant shall serue for their dischargde in their accomptes, ells otherwise to be awnsweared for the same w<sup>th</sup> their wages. And eu<sup>r</sup>y, such warrant to be registred in the pursers booke, the day of the monneth and the occasion And this vpon forfecture of soe much out of the pursers wages yf it be throughe his neglecte, this order to be obserued in each shipp as aforesaid, that att the retourne of the said shipps a true accompt may be yealded, howe and what manner the same may be vsed and ymployed.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">7 Item to thend that all difficult matters may be the better passed over w<sup>th</sup> good aduise and discrecõn: wee doe appointe a Counsell to be called together vpon any spetiall occasions of trade &amp; Marchandizeinge (viz) Iohn Sayris cheefe Captaine and Comander, Gabriell Towerson Captaine of the Hector, Tempest Peacocke<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-124" href="#sec-08-fn-124" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-124" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> Comander of the Thomas, Richard Cockes,<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-125" href="#sec-08-fn-125" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-125" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> Ambrose <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-491" data-page="491" data-folio="399" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n490_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 399">[p. 399]</button> Armeway, Richard Wickham,<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-126" href="#sec-08-fn-126" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-126" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> Edward Cambden,<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-127" href="#sec-08-fn-127" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-127" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> and George Ball,<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-128" href="#sec-08-fn-128" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-128" role="doc-noteref">3</a></sup> whereof one of them to record whatt matters shall passe, all w<sup>ch</sup> are to aduise and giue their best Counsell and direccon in psecuteinge such courses for trade as may be conceiued most convenient for the good of the voyadge. And for all Sea-fareing causes whatsoeu<sup>r</sup> wee doe likewise appointe a Councell to be called together (viz) Iohn Sairis cheefe Capt &amp; Comander Gabriell Towerson Capt of the Hecto<sup>r</sup>. Thomas ffuller, Iames ffoster, Richard Dawes, Mathew Porter, Thomas Barwicke, Iohn Elmer, and Thomas Iones.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">8. Item that the cheefe Captaine &amp; Comander together w<sup>th</sup> the rest of the Counsell appointed for that purpose doe sett downe and deliu<sup>r</sup> such instruccons as may serue for the Comanders of thother shipps to keepe Company together for the better releefe and Comforte one of an other, And yf separacon of them should happen by constrainte of weather or tempest that they shall agree of a course before hand &amp; of a place whether to applie them selues to finde the rest.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">9 Item that contynueall &amp; true Iournalls be kept of eu<sup>r</sup>y daies Nauigacon dureinge the whole voyadge, w<sup>th</sup> a true relacon of eu<sup>r</sup>y materiall thinge that passeth &amp; this not onlie to be done by the cheefe Capt &amp; Comander, Captaine of the Hector but alsoe by the Marchaunte pursers, M<sup>rs</sup> &amp; M<sup>rs</sup> mates &amp; that some of the <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-492" data-page="492" data-folio="400" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n491_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 400">[p. 400]</button> principalls in each shipp may conferr their obseruacõns together att conuenient tymes, once or twise euʳy weeke att the least to the end yf any haue forgotten whatt an other hath obserued the same may be added, soe as a p̃fect discourse may be sett downe to be presented to the Gouʳnor &amp; Company when God shall graunte them a safe retourne to be kept for better direcc̃on of posteritie.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">10 Item Wee Comend vnto yoᵘ the cheefe Capt: &amp; Comander principallie, &amp; to the rest of the Comanders &amp; Masters as a spetiall care, that att euʳy place where yoᵘ shall water &amp; refresh yoʳ men yoᵘ call the Companies together, giueinge them expresse warninge to behaue them selues peaceablie and Ciuillie towarde the people of those places the better to p̃cure their freindshipp towarde the supplie of yoʳ wantes, &amp; the like in euʳy place where yoᵘ shall come leaste the losse of yoʳ liues &amp; ouʳthrowe of oʳ voyadge pay for their disorders beside an vtter discreditt to oʳ Nation. And that yoʳ men doe vse a discreete meane in eateinge of fruites and fresh victualls least they surfett and fall into diseases whereof wee haue had too much experience. And the better to restraine vnrulie appetites, wee hould ytt conuenient that the cheefe Captaine &amp; Comander appointe some of the discreetest in the Companies to p̃cure &amp; p̃uide in such places whatt yoᵘ want, &amp; the same beinge bought &amp; brought to the cheefe Captaine and Comander by him to be repted to each Companie whatt may be needfull &amp; that none presume to doe the contrarie vpon paine of seveare ponishmᵗ. And that espetiall care be had to releeue the sicke wᵗʰ such fresh meates &amp; other comfortable thinges wherewᵗʰ wee haue furnished each shipp for that purpose, &amp; not otherwise to be spent in Ryatt &amp; banquettinge &amp; soe the sicke p̃ishe for want of thinges needfull. Where espetiall care must be had that when those that are the most weake p̃sonns come to fresh Victualls after longe abstinence att Sea they be not suffered in any wise to eate of those fresh meate wᶜʰ shall be gotten on shore but that yoᵘ cause such fresh victualls as yoᵘ can prouide to be boyled in pottage, till yt be sodden in peeces &amp; the cheefe substance lefte in the broth &amp; giue them of that broth onelie to feede vpon moderatelie for twoe or three daies till their stomacke be somewhatt setled &amp; their bodies Comforted.</p></div><div class="page-rail-marker" id="page-493"><button type="button" class="folio-trigger" data-page="493" data-folio="401" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n492_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect scan for page 401">[p. 401]</button></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p"><span class="sc">11</span> Item and whereas the straglinge of yo<sup>r</sup> men in any place where they shall arriue or come, may not onlie proue the losse of such as shall soe straggle notw<sup>th</sup>standinge they should be armed, but alsoe thereby much hurte might happen to the residue of o<sup>r</sup> people. It is ordered that yo<sup>u</sup> doe not pmitt any man in any place to straggle or goe from the rest of yo<sup>r</sup> Companies vnlesse vpon spetiall occasion yo<sup>u</sup> giue warrant therevnto vpon paine of seueare ponishm<sup>t</sup> to be inflicted vpon such as shall doe to the Contrarie, still doubtinge the worste and standinge vpon your guard w<sup>th</sup>out giueinge any trust to the people of the place, haueing in remembrance the ou<sup>r</sup>sight of the Comanders of the Vnion to their owne destruction &amp; ou<sup>r</sup>throwe of the voyadge.<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-129" href="#sec-08-fn-129" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-129" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup></p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">12 Item yt is expreslie ordered by a gen<sup>r</sup>all Consent of yo<sup>u</sup> the cheefe Captaine and Com̃ander, Capt of the Hector, Cape Marchaunt of the Thomas Marchauntę ffactors M<sup>rs</sup> &amp; Marriners some good order be by yo<sup>u</sup> taken and sett downe in writeinge soe soone as yo<sup>u</sup> come to Sea conc<sup>r</sup>ninge the disposeinge of dead mens goode in the voyadge, either by comittinge the same by Inventorie to the pticular keepinge of some men in the shipps, and this vpon some convenient Considerac̃on for keepinge the same or by saile of soe much thereof att the maine maste as may not exceede a third pte of the buyers wages, for either buyinge or borrowinge to prevent such preiudice to o<sup>r</sup> Companie as wee haue felt by Mariners buyinge soe much as their whole wages come vnto, whereby they haue not onlie forsaken o<sup>r</sup> shipps before their dischardge, butt alsoe depriued vs of meanes to redresse such wronge as they haue many waies done vs in the voyadge. And to order what sallary shall be giuen the purser for keepinge accompt thereof to avoied question and Controu<sup>r</sup>sie hereafter, Alsoe that there be noe dealeinge by exchange betwixt ptie and ptie neither money for money, Comoditie for money Comoditie for Comoditie neither outwarde or homewarde, for such exchange giues way for them to bringe more then they should, or take or purloyne of o<sup>r</sup> goode they ought nott: ffor preuenc̃on whereof Wee order that neither the purser, or any other doe register witnes, or take knowledge of any such contract by exchaunge. <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-494" data-page="494" data-folio="402" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n493_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 402">[p. 402]</button> And noe man to be admitted meanes to bringe goode abourd in any the shipps more then their Chest appointed them will conteyne, and the same goode to be pticularlie entred in the pursers booke vpon paine of forfeeture to the Company, that w<sup>ch</sup> shall not be entred, eu<sup>r</sup>y Marriner &amp; other of the said shipps to sett their hande or marke to the said orders mentioned in this 12 article. And yf any ffactor shall happen to die, his goode, bookes accompte and other thinges are to be ordered of the cheefe Capt &amp; Comander &amp; Capt of the Hector &amp; Cape Marchaunte of the <em>Thomas</em> to be safelie reserued and brought home for England, an Inventorie first taken thereof &amp; registred in the pursers booke, to be witnessed by twoe or three other of the Companie.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">13   Item for better p<sup>r</sup>seruacõn of health o<sup>r</sup> order is that from tyme to tyme as the water Caske shall be emptied of fresh water, ytt be forthw<sup>th</sup> againe filled w<sup>th</sup> salt water for preuencon of leakidge &amp; mustines.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">14.   Item in yo<sup>r</sup> passadge betweene the Island of S<sup>t</sup> Lawrence &amp; the Coaste of <em>Sophola</em> beware of the flatte of Iudea<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-130" href="#sec-08-fn-130" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-130" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> vpon w<sup>ch</sup> flatte it is said thatt the Current doth stronglie run, whereby many shipps haue pished, therefore w<sup>th</sup> greate care to be avoieded.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">15   Item for the fittest place for refreshinge whether att <em>Saldania</em> or att the Bay of S<sup>t</sup> Augustine in S<sup>t</sup> Lawrence Island, wee referr the same to yo<sup>r</sup> discretions to doe therein as occasion may vrdge, still fixinge yo<sup>r</sup> thoughte and resolucõns to arriue att the Barr of Suratt by thend of Septemb̃ next accordinge to o<sup>r</sup> designes, w<sup>ch</sup> is the maine and principall scope of this o<sup>r</sup> voyadge, w<sup>th</sup> this further aduise that wheresoeu<sup>r</sup> yo<sup>u</sup> shall water yo<sup>u</sup> make noe longer staye then necessitie shall enforce, to thend yo<sup>u</sup> may haue some oportunitie for freshm<sup>t</sup> att <em>Succatora</em> there to buy some <em>Alloes Sacatrina</em> to the quantitie of fower Tonns, where we pray yo<sup>u</sup> take notice that in the third voyadge <em>William Kealeinge</em> bought there twentie fower hundredweight att xx Ryalls of eight the hundred pound w<sup>ch</sup> is 104<sup>li</sup> of o<sup>rs</sup>.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">16   Item wee desire that rather yo<sup>u</sup> touch att Succatora for that wee hope yo<sup>u</sup> shall meete w<sup>th</sup> Guzaratte att that place, from whome yo<sup>u</sup> may receiue some intelligence whether <em>William</em> <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-495" data-page="495" data-folio="403" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n494_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 403">[p. 403]</button> <em>Hawkins</em> &amp; the rest of o<sup>r</sup> ffactors lefte att Suratt out of the Hector, are liueinge or not, as alsoe of the state of S<sup>r</sup> Henrie Middleton, &amp; of those shipps thatt went w<sup>th</sup> him, &amp; in whatt state o<sup>r</sup> busines there standeth, whereby yo<sup>u</sup> may the better there gou<sup>r</sup>ne yo<sup>r</sup> selues for pformance of o<sup>r</sup> desires: Butt yf the time of the Monsone be soe farr spent that yo<sup>u</sup> cannot arriue att the Barr of Suratt by thend of Septemƀ or Middle of Octoƀ next att the farthest, then wee will yo<sup>u</sup> to take the first oportunitie either to saile to Aden or Mocha alls Mochowe w<sup>th</sup>in the mouth of the red Sea, aboute some tenn leagues distant from Aden as wee vnderstand. Att w<sup>ch</sup> porte of Mocha wee haue herd that one of the shipps in the fourth voyadge hath traded and where wee doubt not you may sell Cloth, leade, Iron, Tynn quicksiluer for money or gould for that wee thinke Comodities will be too deare in that place consideringe o<sup>r</sup> purpose that yo<sup>u</sup> goe to Suratt, except yo<sup>u</sup> may buy Cloues Maces or Nutmegge att a reasonable rate w<sup>ch</sup> are said to be good Comodities att Suratt as well as here in England. In this place yo<sup>u</sup> must spend the time till oportunitie doe serue to goe to Suratt w<sup>ch</sup> will not be till towarde the end of August followinge, soe as you may arriue there by the end of Septemƀ w<sup>ch</sup> we pray yo<sup>u</sup> to endevor by all possible meanes Butt stayinge att Aden or Mocha haue espetiall care that yo<sup>r</sup> men giue noe offence to the people there, still keepinge good watch &amp; standinge vpon yo<sup>r</sup> guard, and that out of any danger of any Castle, forte, or Gallies to preuent mischeefe</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">17 Item being arriued att Suratt We desier yo<sup>u</sup> vse the best meanes yo<sup>u</sup> can presentlie to vnderstand the estate of William Hawkins and the rest of o<sup>r</sup> people there, as alsoe whatt hath beene done by S<sup>r</sup> Hen: Middleton for the setlinge of a c<sup>r</sup>teine and safe trade there. And heareinge thatt all is well then either to send vp yo<sup>r</sup> longe boate in &amp; w<sup>th</sup> Tobias Cockes &amp; some others whome yo<sup>u</sup> shall thinke fitt, or finde some meanes to cause some of the ffactors there to come abourd yo<sup>u</sup>, either William ffinche or some others of whome take due obseruac̃on of the estate of the Countrie, howe they haue beene accepted of by the kinge, howe intreated by the people, whatt priuiledges they haue gott, what Comodities of ours are there requested, what they haue of their Countrie Comodities vpon their hande to be laden, acquainte <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-496" data-page="496" data-folio="404" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n495_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 404">[p. 404]</button> them alsoe whatt Comodities yo<sup>u</sup> haue brought &amp; the quantities, &amp; takeinge knowledge of their worth there, As alsoe whatt Comodities are there presentlie to be had and att what prices aswell fittinge for England as Priaman Bantam, Banda &amp; the Molloccoes &amp; whether the same may be had w<sup>th</sup> speede as you may take hould of the Monsone to goe into those p̄tes, What forces the Enemies are of, howe readie the Guzarattē are to helpe o<sup>r</sup> Nation, &amp; of all other needfull intelligences, by whome (yf yo<sup>u</sup> be incourradged) wee wishe yo<sup>u</sup> to send in the Thomas &amp; in her Tempest Peacocke o<sup>r</sup> ffactor w<sup>th</sup> such Comodities as may be most vendible w<sup>th</sup>all speede, giueinge them warninge to be readie to w<sup>th</sup>stand any Enemies &amp; keepe good watch to preuent any mischeefe of fire or otherwise, w<sup>ch</sup> lett them be assured will be practised against them.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">18. Item for that we haue p̄uided as yo<sup>u</sup> knowe and laden in theis shipps sundrie Comodities of good value principallie for that place, as leade, Tynn, sword bladē, peeces, Cloth, kersies and some other thinges accordinge to an Invoice herewith deliuʾed yo<sup>u</sup>. Wee hould yt convenient that as many of the same as yo<sup>u</sup> shall thinke fitt may be laden in such fryggottē as can be p̄uided for that purpose &amp; the same sent vp to Suratt. Wee pray yo<sup>u</sup> to Consult and take good aduise for the deliuʾy of his Ma<sup>ties</sup>: l̈res nowe sent, whatt presentē are to be deliuʾed, to whome, by whome, &amp; in what manner, the same to be done w<sup>th</sup> what speede and dilligence that shall be requisite, consideringe the shortnes of the tyme in keepinge the Monson for attaineinge yo<sup>r</sup> other portes for w<sup>ch</sup> presentē wee haue sent in the Cloue as appēth in the Invoice: Out of w<sup>ch</sup> yo<sup>u</sup> are to take for quantitie &amp; Coullor whatt shall be thought needfull for that purpose, the rest to be sould for o<sup>r</sup> Companies benefitt As alsoe wee haue sent in the same shipp 160<sup>oz</sup> ¾ ounzes of guilt plate together w<sup>th</sup> peeces out of w<sup>ch</sup> yf yo<sup>u</sup> thinke requisitt some alsoe may be giuen for presentē accordinge to aduise of m<sup>r</sup> Hawkins m<sup>r</sup> ffinche &amp; the rest. If any thinge be presented to the kinge Prince or greate Potentate it is to be done as yo<sup>u</sup> knowe w<sup>th</sup> state and comlines. ffor presentē for meaner p̄sonns wee wishe yo<sup>u</sup> to chuse out amonge o<sup>r</sup> Clothes such Colors as may be acceptable vnto them for vestes. And for the better p̄formance of this busines &amp; accompanyinge o<sup>r</sup> cheefe <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-497" data-page="497" data-folio="405" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n496_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 405">[p. 405]</button> ffactors wee leaue yt to yo<sup>r</sup> discretion w<sup>th</sup> the aduise of the rest of the Counsell to appointe such as yo<sup>u</sup> shall thinke fitt, and to leaue there soe many &amp; such for ffactors as thoccasions in yo<sup>r</sup> discretions shall necessarilie requier.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">19 Item the busines aforesaid beinge done Considerac̃on is to be had for the deliu<sup>r</sup>inge &amp; dispatche of o<sup>r</sup> shipps &amp; yf yo<sup>u</sup> shall finde sufficient meanes either by the helpe of stocke form<sup>r</sup>lie lefte in the Countrie or by the sale of such Marchaundize as yo<sup>u</sup> shall now carrie w<sup>th</sup> yo<sup>u</sup>, to lade all the three shipps w<sup>th</sup> such Comodities as may there be had fittinge to be retourned for England, wee will you to take that oportunitie to fraight them all and lett them be retourned as aforesaid: Or yf meanes may be had for the dispatch of twoe then that the Hector and Thomas be sent for England vnder the Comand of Gabriell Towerson Capt of the Hector in the ladeinge of w<sup>ch</sup>, or but one of them yo<sup>u</sup> are to obserue this a gen<sup>r</sup>all order that the goode of eu<sup>r</sup>y voyadge may be m<sup>r</sup>ked and nombred seu<sup>r</sup>ally and soe expressed in yo<sup>r</sup> Cargasons, and accompte that the goode of each voyadge may be knowne by them selues, for that they doe not conc<sup>r</sup>ne the selfe same Adventurers And hereof therefore you are to be very carefull to preuent wronges in ladeinge of goode. Wee note theis as spetiall Comodities w<sup>ch</sup> wee desier (viz) Indico of the very beste aswell flatt as round soe much as yo<sup>u</sup> may w<sup>th</sup> o<sup>r</sup> moneys well compasse, other Comodities alsoe beinge to be had, but lett yo<sup>r</sup> care be extraordinary to buy the very best that may be had for money, aswell Indico as of all other Comodities, Callicoes white tasselled booke good quantitie &amp; some of the courser sortes, Callicoes light colored alsoe as Carnatian,<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-131" href="#sec-08-fn-131" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-131" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> light Watchette, strawe Colors &amp; other light Colors, butt none of them <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-498" data-page="498" data-folio="406" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n497_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 406">[p. 406]</button> course or sad colors in any manner, in theis yo<sup>u</sup> may ymploye to the valewe of both soe much as yo<sup>r</sup> stocke will affoorde, reguardinge to haue some whatt of other Comodities Cotten yearne of the finest well requested    Tonns, greene ginger of the best and lardgest suchas that of Battecala Twoe Tonns, red Saunders wood aboute fyue Tonns Turbith .... Tonns Opium ffiue hundred weight. Beniamyn tenn Tonns, Salt Armoniacke not much, Olibanū (yf good) not much, Lignum Alloes asmuch as yo<sup>u</sup> can gett soe yt exceede not fiue Tonns, Wormeseede cleansed asmuchas possiblie may be from the chaffe &amp; dust, by sifteinge &amp; wynnowinge for that the dust &amp; chaffe is here worth nothinge, to the quantitie of ffortie kintolls, Gumlacke as much as yo<sup>u</sup> can gett of the best and second sorte but none of the worst att any <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-499" data-page="499" data-folio="407" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n498_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 407">[p. 407]</button> hand, Sugers of the best some fewe Chestes for a triall, silke of persia (yf to be had) a good quantitie  Theis Comodities being bought and packed w<sup>th</sup> m<sup>r</sup>chauntlike care to preuent damage nombred &amp; marked accordinge to former direcc̃on both of the inside and outside for better satisfacc̃on of the Company in the difference of the sorte and contente, the rather for that the outmost marke may be by longe lieinge at Sea worne out, the same to be laden &amp; the shipps to be dispatched away w<sup>th</sup>all expedic̃on for England.  And nowe as a first busines in dispatch to be remembred wee wishe yo<sup>u</sup> to prouide such Callicoes and other Comodities as may be fittinge for Priaman Bantam Banda &amp; the Molleccoes, &amp; the same being orderlie packed to be shipped w<sup>th</sup>all speede att yo<sup>r</sup> discrec̃ons for those places w<sup>ch</sup> w<sup>th</sup> Rialls of 8 could never with any propriety be applied to the carnation <em>saris</em> formerly, and I hope still, woven by the weavers, and worn by the women, of Ahmedabad, and Surat, and Ratnagheri.  Equally erroneous is the accepted lexicographical derivation of the name, “Garden Pink,” of <em>Dianthus hortensis</em>, from its pinked or jagged edges.  Until the Garden Pink became a familiar flower in this country, pinked work was known as pounced, <em>i.e.</em> punched, work.  Thus in <em>The Persones Tale</em> Chaucer writes of “pounsed and dagged clothing”; and evidently “the Garden Pink” gave its name not only to its characteristic colour, and to the cant words “pink” a stab, and “pink” in the sense of acme (<em>i.e.</em> “flower, bloom”), as in the phrase used by Shakespeare in <em>Romeo and Juliet</em>, IV., 2:—“The very <em>pink</em> of courtesy,” but also to the method of ornamenting any textile fabric by notching its edges, and piercing it over with eyelet-holes; a word corrupted from <em>œillet</em>, the French name of the Pink-worts generally, and which reappears in the English name, “Sweet <em>Willi-am</em>,” of <em>Dianthus barbatus</em>; a flower much esteemed, writes Gerarde, in 1597, “to deck up the bosoms of the beautiful, and garlands and crowns for pleasure.” In short, the English word pink, in its primary, and all its secondary meanings, is but a corruption from the Greek word Πεντηκοστή, the Harvest Festival held by the Jews on the “Fiftieth” day after their Passover, and identified by us with Whitsunday [Dominica in Albis], the first annual festival adopted by the Christian Church; and “the Garden Pink” is therefore nothing but “the Whitsuntide flower” [cp. German <em>Pfingstrose</em>, “the Peony”].  Chaucer is the first, so far as I know, to use the term in his description of the wife of the carpenter of Oxford, in <em>The Miller’s Tale</em>, l. 82:—</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><div class="verse-stanza"><span class="line">“Sche was a primerole, and a <em>piggesneyghe</em>.”</span></div></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">Some commentators explain the word as meaning that she was pig’s-eyed; while others, more sympathetically, but also quite wrongly, as a corruption of “Pansy”; but the word is really Pfingsten-eye [cp. Daisy, in Old English Daieseyghe, <em>i.e.</em> Eye of Day], the Eye of Whitsuntide; which, from the usual time of their flowering, is a most appropriate name for almost any European species of <em>Dianthus</em>.  What Chaucer means is that the carpenter’s young wife was sweet as a Primrose, and fair and rosy as a Garden Pink, in all the beauty of its full Whitsuntide bloom. <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-500" data-page="500" data-folio="408" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n499_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 408">[p. 408]</button> that wee wishe yo<sup>u</sup> to receiue for<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-132" href="#sec-08-fn-132" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-132" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> ymploym<sup>t</sup> att Suratt, as alsoe the Iron wee now send, w<sup>th</sup> lead, &amp; bales w<sup>th</sup> Cloth prouided for Bantam the said shipp to take her first oportunitie to saile for Priaman together w<sup>th</sup> thatt shipp of the other twoe not dispatched for England.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">20 Item being arriued att Priaman to make there sale of soe much of yo<sup>r</sup> Iron Callicoes &amp; Pintadoes as yo<sup>u</sup> shall finde fittinge, soe much for gould as yo<sup>u</sup> may &amp; the rest for large pepp, wherein haue espetiall care to buie none greene for breach in weight, but such as shall be drie and cleane from dust to the quantitie as yo<sup>u</sup> shall thinke fittinge and some Tenn Tonns of Beniamyn, theis Comodities to be weyghed by yo<sup>r</sup> owne waighte into yo<sup>r</sup> shipp, &amp; the purser to keepe due notice of the same w<sup>th</sup> such of the ffactors as shall be appointed for that purpose, that they may be able to giue vs a good accompt thereof. In execution of this busines take this for aduise that yo<sup>u</sup> make showe to be gone from thence w<sup>th</sup> a purpose to buy noe pepp, for that when they pceaue you purpose to depte they will offer yo<sup>u</sup> pepp att much lower rate then otherwise. Therefore yt is fitt to seeme vnwillinge to buie, &amp; sometymes to make refusall will not be amisse, beside make shewe that yo<sup>u</sup> haue but fewe Callicoes or other Comodities to sell the better to giue them edge to buie. In pformance of this busines remember still yo<sup>r</sup> purpose to goe for Bantam &amp; Banda that yo<sup>u</sup> slipp not the oportunitie of the Monson. And soe w<sup>th</sup> whatt yo<sup>u</sup> haue there taken in, &amp; restinge in the shipp for Bantam, there dischardge both Iron, leade, Callicoes, &amp; Cloth &amp;c: prepared for that place, for a stocke to puide Lamkin<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-133" href="#sec-08-fn-133" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-133" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> silke of the finest and whitest, but noe Canton silke att any hand w<sup>th</sup> what other thinges yo<sup>u</sup> shall hould needfull against you retourne from Banda, whether wee wishe yo<sup>u</sup> to make all possible hast takeing w<sup>th</sup> yo<sup>u</sup> such ffactors to leaue there as in yo<sup>r</sup> Iudgm<sup>t</sup> shall be thought meete. And for stocke of the Rialls before mentioned <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-501" data-page="501" data-folio="409" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n500_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 409">[p. 409]</button> what yo<sup>u</sup> thinke requisite, and such China stuffe and dishes as you may learne att Bantam to be fittinge for the place.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">21. Item being arriued at Banda present such guiftes to the Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> or Savander there as in yo<sup>r</sup> discretion shall seeme fittinge &amp; there prouide accordinge to your discretions of Nutmegge of the best and soundest that may be gotten freed from dust and Rumps all that may be, remembringe that the same will not be worth the freight and chardges. Alsoe Maces the largest and brightest thatt may be gotten, but none that is darke colored, red maces w<sup>ch</sup> are called feminine Maces &amp; heare little worth. ffor accomplishinge hereof as alsoe for to leaue stocke convenient there for puision for a future voyadge, keepe in yo<sup>r</sup> shipp soe much money &amp; Callicoes as you may deeme sufficient. And yf being arriued att Banda yo<sup>u</sup> hould yt possible &amp; finde yt convenient to send to the Molloccoes whilest the Nutmegge and Maces are puideinge to seeke for Cloues, and haueinge gotten to yo<sup>r</sup> Content therein, to retourne to Banda there to take in the Mace and Nutmegge &amp; w<sup>th</sup> speede to retourne to Bantam leaueinge a convenient numb of ffactors and stocke as shall be needfull at Banda as aforesaid. And beinge retourned to Bantam &amp; haueinge brought a true pticuler of whatt is lefte at Banda vnder the hande of the ffactors there, then to take in such silkes &amp; other thinge as shall in yo<sup>r</sup> absence be puided there for London. Where (haueinge setled the busines and ffactors in good order &amp; taken a pticular relac̃on of o<sup>r</sup> accompte and Comodities there lefte, the Coppies whereof to be signed by o<sup>r</sup> ffactors and to be brought alonge w<sup>th</sup> yo<sup>u</sup>, and order giuen by yo<sup>u</sup> to keepe accompt of eu<sup>r</sup>y voyadge by yt selfe, and soe of the retournes<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-134" href="#sec-08-fn-134" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-134" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> of eu<sup>r</sup>y voyadge may haue their right as they consiste of diu<sup>r</sup>sitie of Aduenturers, []) Then in Gode name w<sup>th</sup>all due speede to retourne for England. And haueinge thus farr pceeded in a direct course wee hould yt fitt againe to looke backe to the barr att Suratt and there soe<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-135" href="#sec-08-fn-135" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-135" role="doc-noteref">2</a></sup> Consider yf thinges fall out contrary to expectac̃on that neither o<sup>r</sup> people should be there in safetie, nor the place fitt for o<sup>r</sup> trade, nor yett incourradgm<sup>t</sup> <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-502" data-page="502" data-folio="410" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n501_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 410">[p. 410]</button> to pursue the same any farther. In reguard of the Comodities puided fitt for the seruice of thatt place and <em>Aden</em>, &amp; not els where w<sup>th</sup>in o<sup>r</sup> knowledge, o<sup>r</sup> expresse order is that vpon notice of the premisses yo<sup>u</sup> forthw<sup>th</sup> w<sup>th</sup> the Monson retourne for Aden or Moccha in the red Sea, yf in yo<sup>r</sup> passadge to Suratt yo<sup>u</sup> haue not visitted the same &amp; done what might be done for the vent of o<sup>r</sup> Comodities, and there to trie for dispatch and vent of o<sup>r</sup> said Marchandize, w<sup>ch</sup> beinge effected accordinge to our Desires yf possiblie you may be fitted by the Juncke arriueinge there from diu<sup>r</sup>s portes out of the Indies w<sup>th</sup> Marchandize fittinge for England to lade either one or twoe or all of o<sup>r</sup> shipps w<sup>th</sup> the same goode brought thither as namelie smooth pepp, drugge of all sorte fittinge for this place, Indico of the best sorte, Nutmegge, maces, Synamon, Callicoes, Beniamyn, Olibanū, Mirr, Opium, red Saunders, Lignum Alloes, &amp; such like, &amp; the shipp or shipps soe laden w<sup>th</sup> speede to direct their course for England. And yf any of the shipps remaine vnladen then w<sup>th</sup> such Callicoes as yo<sup>u</sup> can buie of the Iunckes fittinge for <em>Priaman</em> Bantam Banda or the Molloccoes to saile directlie for Pryaman &amp; there to pursue trade &amp; from thence further as aforesaid as in yo<sup>r</sup> discretion shall seeme best.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">22 Item wee thinke yt not amisse further to aduise yo<sup>u</sup> that findinge faile of trade at Suratt you endevo<sup>r</sup> whilest yo<sup>u</sup> are there, to inuite the Guzaratte w<sup>th</sup> such Comodities as you looke for, to repaire to the porte of <em>Delisha</em> or Tamarie att the Island of Succotara where you may agree to deale w<sup>th</sup> them for the same, w<sup>ch</sup> yf yo<sup>u</sup> doe, yo<sup>u</sup> and they alsoe shall saue much Customes yo<sup>u</sup> shall pay in other portes to the Gou<sup>r</sup>no<sup>r</sup> thereof, Itt is to be vnderstoode thatt yf noe trade be to be had att Suratt there shall be noe cause to settle any ffactors there, In such case wee appointe all o<sup>r</sup> ffactor<sup>s</sup> now sent to pceede w<sup>th</sup> yo<sup>u</sup> to Aden or Mocha, or to such other place as yo<sup>u</sup> shall thinke fitt to direct yo<sup>r</sup> course for benefitt of trade And finding any such place as may be beneficiall to o<sup>r</sup> Company aswell for vent of Cloth and other our English Marchandizes as for Comodities of those Countries fittinge for this wee referr to yo<sup>r</sup> discrec̃on the setlinge of o<sup>r</sup> ffactorie there, and the appointem<sup>t</sup> of any of o<sup>r</sup> ffactors to recide there w<sup>th</sup> such stocke as in yo<sup>r</sup> Iudgm<sup>t</sup> may seeme <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-503" data-page="503" data-folio="411" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n502_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 411">[p. 411]</button> Competent to defray their chardge &amp; make p̱uision of Comodities w<sup>ch</sup> that Countrie may yeald against the arriuall of a second supplie: Butt findinge noe such place, &amp; p̱ceedinge to Bantam o<sup>r</sup> order is thatt the ymploym<sup>t</sup> of o<sup>r</sup> shipps for p̱formance of their ladeinge bee att yo<sup>r</sup> direcc̃on to procure sufficient ladeinge for the Hector &amp; Thomas of such Comodities as shall be most fitt for sale in England &amp; to speede them away thither vnder the conduct &amp; Comand of Capt Towerson as was said before for Suratt. In the search of w<sup>ch</sup> trade yo<sup>u</sup> are to vnderstand that att Makassa an island neare <em>Celebres</em> there is greete store of Rice w<sup>ch</sup> beinge carryed to Banda will yeald three for one, att w<sup>ch</sup> Island there may be had 100 Tonns of Nutmegge &amp; Mace as is informed.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">23. Item the Hector and Thomas beinge fraighted &amp; sent for England then wee wishe yo<sup>u</sup> Capt Iohn Sairis to p̱ceede w<sup>th</sup> the Cloue, &amp; w<sup>th</sup>all possible speede thatt yo<sup>u</sup> may endevor yo<sup>r</sup> course for Iapan, haueinge before att Bantam or ells where dilligently aduised inquired &amp; conferred w<sup>th</sup> o<sup>r</sup> ffactors, or any other the best meanes yo<sup>u</sup> may attaine vnto the knowledge whereby to informe yo<sup>r</sup> selfe for or conc<sup>r</sup>ninge o<sup>r</sup> intended voyadge to Iapan, and to p̱cure in any of those p̱tę (yf you may[ ]) some skilfull pilate experienced in those Coastes, and take him alonge w<sup>th</sup> yo<sup>u</sup>, directinge yo<sup>r</sup> course from thence to Iapan, neglectinge noe tyme butt takeinge the benefitt and aduantage of those Wyndę and Monsons as yo<sup>u</sup> shall most c<sup>r</sup>teinlie &amp; trulie informe yo<sup>r</sup> selues, &amp; learne that they blowe in their seasons. And in all theis yo<sup>r</sup> passadges &amp; traffiques o<sup>r</sup> desire is that yo<sup>u</sup> should informe yo<sup>r</sup> selfe of safe harbors and places of repaire for vent of o<sup>r</sup> English Comodities either aboute the porte of Dabull neare Suratt, or the riuer Syndus &amp; Laree, or the Cape of Rosalgate or any Coaste of the p̱tę of Arabia where yo<sup>u</sup> may haue kinde interteynem<sup>t</sup> of<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-136" href="#sec-08-fn-136" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-136" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> freedome from Enimies, to w<sup>ch</sup> places yo<sup>u</sup> may assay to inuite Mores, Guzaratte or any other people tradeinge in those p̱tę w<sup>th</sup> such Marchandize as may be fitt for this Countrey, and be rendred for such as wee shall send att such times as yo<sup>u</sup> may suppose o<sup>r</sup> shippinge may w<sup>th</sup> Conveniencie arriue there. Amongst w<sup>ch</sup> portę wee hould the postę aforesaid in the Island of Succatora <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-504" data-page="504" data-folio="412" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n503_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 412">[p. 412]</button> &amp;c: to be most fittest aswell for the nearnes of the place as saueing of Customes &amp; the highe way for the Guzaratte &amp; other tradeinge into the red Sea att their ordinarie seasons.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">24. Item and being arriued att Iapan wee desier yo<sup>u</sup> to vse the best meanes to search out the most convenient and safest harbour to trade in, where you may sell Cloth leade, Iron &amp; such other of o<sup>r</sup> Natiue Comodities as by yo<sup>r</sup> obseruacõn yo<sup>u</sup> shall finde most vendible there, wherein yo<sup>u</sup> are to be very spetially carefull both whatt Colors and sortes of Cloth, and for whatt quantitie will there be vented, as alsoe to giue greate chardge that yo<sup>r</sup> men offer noe offence to the people there, still keepinge good watch and standinge vpon yo<sup>r</sup> guard &amp; that out of the danger of any Castle fforte or other Gallies or friggott<em>e</em> to prevent any mischeefe that may happen, &amp; to consult and take good aduise w<sup>th</sup> the rest of the ffactors &amp; espetiallie w<sup>th</sup> William Adams<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-137" href="#sec-08-fn-137" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-137" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> an English man nowe resident there, and in greate favo<sup>r</sup> w<sup>th</sup> the kinge as wee heare to desire his opinion whatt course should be held both for the deliu<sup>r</sup>y of his Mat<em>e</em> lres nowe sent, whatt present<em>e</em> are to geuen, to whome, by whome &amp; in whatt manner the same is to be done w<sup>th</sup>all dilligence that shall be requisitt, obserueing carefully the tymes in keepinge the Monsons in all places wheare you shall trade for attayneinge to other portes as occasion shall be offered And for presennte wee haue sent w<sup>th</sup> yo<sup>u</sup> as appeareth in the Invoice.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">25 Item and as yo<sup>u</sup> shall receiue incourradgm<sup>t</sup> in the progresse of yo<sup>r</sup> affaires by the pswasion of the foresaid William Adams grounded vpon his longe experience in those partes &amp; shall finde that ytt may be w<sup>th</sup> saffetie, as beinge ptected vnder the authoritie of the kinge of the Country, &amp; may proue Comodious and beneficiall for the good of the Company. Then <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-505" data-page="505" data-folio="413" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n504_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 413">[p. 413]</button> w<sup>th</sup> the Counsell of Richard Cockes and such principall men as are in yo<sup>r</sup> shipp, yo<sup>u</sup> may aduise resolue and leaue such ffactors as yo<sup>u</sup> in yo<sup>r</sup> discrec̃on shall thinke fitt w<sup>th</sup> such a quantitie of goode as shall be by yo<sup>u</sup> thought convenient for the setlinge of a factorie &amp; mainteyneing of a trade att Iapan together w<sup>th</sup> soe many and such sufficient Marriners to attend them and guard o<sup>r</sup> said goode as yo<sup>u</sup> shall finde to be needfull &amp; necessarie &amp; to puide them a good and stronge howse for the better saffetie of such goode as shall be lefte there in chardge w<sup>th</sup> them. Butt yf yo<sup>u</sup> shall finde att Iapan [no] convenient meanes of ventinge o<sup>r</sup> Comodities or the retourne of proffitt not to awnsweare the length and difficultie of the voyadge, or the dangers in psecuteinge this trade to exceede w<sup>th</sup> out pporc̃on the advantage to be hoped, then yo<sup>u</sup> shall frame yo<sup>r</sup> course &amp; seeke vent for such Comodities as yo<sup>u</sup> haue either att the Molloccoes, <em>Philippinas</em> or any other places thereaboute, whether you may be incourradged by any pbable hopes of peaceable and pfittable tradeinge. And yf att yo<sup>r</sup> depture from Iapan the said William Adams shall ymportune yo<sup>u</sup> to transporte him into his natiue Countrie to visitt his wife and Children, Wee pray yo<sup>u</sup> then to accomodate him w<sup>th</sup> as convenient a Cabben as yo<sup>u</sup> may and all other necessaries w<sup>ch</sup> yo<sup>r</sup> shipp may afford him.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">26 Item wee ordeine that such of the ffactors as shall remaine in the shipps at the buyinge of any goode shall be ymployed to weighe the same, pay the money &amp; keepe due accompte thereof, &amp; nothinge be done either in buyinge or sellinge any goode w<sup>th</sup>out their priuitie that they may write the same into their booke, and be able to giue vs reason for what is bought or sould, and to acquainte the Capt eu<sup>r</sup>y night w<sup>th</sup> what they haue done that day. That the purser be continuallie abourd the shipp <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-506" data-page="506" data-folio="414" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n505_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 414">[p. 414]</button> &amp; take true notice of all thinges whatsoeu<sup>r</sup> are brought into the shipps or carried out, &amp; to enter the same in his booke as hee will awnsweare ytt w<sup>th</sup> the losse of his wages And yo<sup>u</sup> the Capt to giue the purser stricte chardge in this behalfe, &amp; that the pursers booke and the ffactors shall then be compared together, soe thatt yt may appeare whether all that hath beene bought, hath beene receiued into the shipp, &amp; there weyed aswell as on the shore, notice to be taken what is wantinge, &amp; inquirie what is become thereof, and the same accompte for whatt hath beene then done att Iapan, the Molloccoes or ellswhere be registred in o<sup>r</sup> booke att Bantam.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">27 Item for that voyadges of this Condic̃on &amp; charge cannot admitt any priuate trade, but whatsoeu<sup>r</sup> is atchiued by this generall and greate charge should be applied to the benefitt of the whole Companie. Therefore wee sett ytt downe as an absolute order that noe man neither Captaines, Marchaunte, Masters or Marriners or any other pson or psons whatsoeu<sup>r</sup> in theis o<sup>r</sup> shipps doe trade or deale for any Marchandize whatsoeu<sup>r</sup> w<sup>ch</sup> wee shall prouide for o<sup>r</sup> retourne, or any other sorte of Marchandize more then will be conteyned w<sup>thin</sup> his pporcioned Chest of allowaunce for this voyadge and the same to be registred in the pursers booke to thend that yf any pson die vpon the voyadge, their freinde and Executors may knowe whatt they had, &amp; take notice what is become of the same, houldinge ytt most vnreasonable that those that liue by o<sup>r</sup> ymploym<sup>t</sup> should deale w<sup>th</sup> the Comodities w<sup>ch</sup> wee labor to gett to produce meanes for paym<sup>t</sup> of their wages, and defrayinge of the chardges of this o<sup>r</sup> greate and costlie voyadge.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">28 Item wee order that noe pson whatsoeu<sup>r</sup> w<sup>ch</sup> shall succeede by appointment other otherwise in any place of Comand or office either in o<sup>r</sup> shipps or in any of o<sup>r</sup> ffactories shall take or be allowed the wages of his predecessor, butt thoughe he be remoued, preferred, or come to any such place or office he shall content himselfe w<sup>th</sup> such wages as wee haue form<sup>r</sup>lie agreed w<sup>th</sup> him for, w<sup>th</sup>out any further demand or incroatchm<sup>t</sup> vpon the Company for the same, butt the pties to be referred to be rewarded by the Company att their retourne as they shall be found to haue deserued, Nor ffactors Masters, Mates nor other Officers of espetiall place in o<sup>r</sup> shipps to be remoued w<sup>th</sup>out <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-507" data-page="507" data-folio="415" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n506_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 415">[p. 415]</button> consent of such of the Counsell as wee haue appointed to aduise w<sup>th</sup> yo<sup>u</sup> the Captaine vpon all such occasions, and not to thinke vpon authoritie sett vpon yo<sup>u</sup> that the same should serue for any yo<sup>r</sup> pticuler respecte, knowinge that a wise man is best tried when he is putt in authoritie, and that he is accompted of more fortitude that ou<sup>r</sup>com̄eth himselfe then he that preuaileth against a multitude.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">29 Item wee doe expreslie chardge yo<sup>u</sup> the Captaines &amp; Comanders not to suffer any Cabbins to be new built or altered, nor pticions pulled downe, in o<sup>r</sup> shipps, nor chandged from the state they nowe are in, att the settinge out of the same, except for inlardgeinge of the houlde or makeinge better prouision for the bringinge home of o<sup>r</sup> goode, and that by the aduice of the Counsell, or for the better accomodateinge of their fighte<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-138" href="#sec-08-fn-138" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-138" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup> yf neede shall be.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">30 Item forasmuch as the tymes are very dangerous throughe piraces w<sup>ch</sup> doe dailie increase and abound in all partes vpon the Seas both att Saldania &amp; ells where, wee doe chardge yo<sup>u</sup> therefore to take espetiall care that dilligent &amp; contynuall watch be kept abourd to preuent all suddaine inuasion &amp; not to giue light Creditt to the faire speeches &amp; pretences of any straungers either English or others.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">31 Item wee forbid all vnnecessary expences of shooteinge of Ordinance att drinkinge of healthes or att the Captaines or cape Marchaunte goeinge a shore or such like for that thereby much powder is wastfully consumed and o<sup>r</sup> goode and shipps putt in much hazard and danger, and likewise by yt way is giuen to the Gunners vnder color thereof to ymbecill the same.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">32 Item for auoidinge confuc̃on in the Gou<sup>r</sup>nm<sup>t</sup> of o<sup>r</sup> people &amp; for that nothinge is soe vnc<sup>r</sup>teine as the estate of mans life Wee ordeine that yf God shall call yo<sup>u</sup> Captaine Iohn Sairis (w<sup>ch</sup> God defend) &amp; that before the sendinge away of the Hector then that o<sup>r</sup> loueinge freind Gabriell Towerson shall succeede you and exercise yo<sup>r</sup> chardge and after his decease m<sup>r</sup>: Rich: Cocke whoe wee trust will soe behaue them selues therein as shall awnswere the good opinion wee haue conceiued of them. Butt yf the said Capt Gabriell Towerson and Richard Cocke be depted out of this <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-508" data-page="508" data-folio="416" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n507_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 416">[p. 416]</button> world or be sent away vpon any other occasion, then we comitt the gou<sup>r</sup>nm<sup>t</sup> of o<sup>r</sup> shipps and Marriners therein, to such as wee haue nomynated in a wryting subscribed by the gou<sup>r</sup>nor and sealed w<sup>th</sup> the seale of the Comp: putt vp in a boxe N<sup>o</sup>: 1. sealed w<sup>th</sup> hard waxe &amp; deliu<sup>r</sup>ed to Captaine Iohn Sairis to be kept close and not openned soe longe as the said Capt Iohn Sairis &amp; Capt Gabriell Towerson, or either of them shall liue, or their shipps continue together. Prouided alwaies that noe pson whatsoeu<sup>r</sup> w<sup>ch</sup> shall succeede in the foresaid place of gou<sup>r</sup>nm<sup>t</sup>, or any other place or office, shall demand be allowed or expect the wages of his predecessor but shall content himselfe w<sup>th</sup> his first wages promysed him by the Company or referr him selfe to be considered of accordinge to his deserte att his retourne by the Gou<sup>r</sup>nor Deputy &amp; Comittees of the Company for the tyme beinge, whoe haue ever respected men that shall deserue well of them.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">33. Item and for that in sundrie voyadges heretofore the Company haue beene much wronged by the Generalls Captaines and Masters of their shipps in takeinge diu<sup>r</sup>s psons into the same shipps &amp; carrying them alonge w<sup>th</sup> them in the said voyadge to the East Indies for their pticuler gaine and proffitt &amp; haue lefte the same pties amonge o<sup>r</sup> Companies ffactors in the Indies where they haue driuen and handled for them selues and their freinde a pticular trade to the greate chardge and hindrance of o<sup>r</sup> Company &amp; haue practised w<sup>th</sup> o<sup>r</sup> ffactors there to deceiue vs, &amp; to convey sundry of the richest comodities of those ptes to their priuate vses. Itt is therefore o<sup>r</sup> expresse order and direccõn to yo<sup>u</sup> Captaine Iohn Sairis, Captaine Gabriell Towerson &amp; Tempest Peacocke, and to all other the Marchaunte ffactors Masters pursers &amp; Marriners &amp; eu<sup>r</sup>y other pson or psonns interteyned and hired to serue in this said voyadge, that neither yo<sup>u</sup>, or any of yo<sup>u</sup> shall admitt or suffer to passe in, or receiue into any of o<sup>r</sup> said shipps in this o<sup>r</sup> nowe intended voyadge any pson or psons of whatt degree or qualitie soeu<sup>r</sup> other then such pson or psons as are interteyned and hired by the Gou<sup>r</sup>nor &amp; Comittees of o<sup>r</sup> said Company to passe therein &amp; mentioned in the pursers booke.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">34 Item yf yo<sup>u</sup> shall finde in yo<sup>r</sup> trauells (or can convenientlie come by[]) any faire birde or beaste or any other rare thinge fitt <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-509" data-page="509" data-folio="417" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n508_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 417">[p. 417]</button> for vs to present to his Ma<sup>tie</sup>: or any of the noble Lordę that are o<sup>r</sup> honorable freinde, Wee pray and requier yo<sup>u</sup> all to be carefull to prouide &amp; send the same w<sup>th</sup> espetiall charge to some carefull man or men that retourne to see them carefullie kept &amp; deliu<sup>r</sup>ed to vs: And w<sup>th</sup>all yf yo<sup>u</sup> shall knowe any Marriners or others to haue any such, that yo<sup>u</sup> cannot ꝑcure from them for reason then that yo<sup>u</sup> send vs notice thereof to thend wee may take order therein accordinglie.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">35 Item in respect of the dangers o<sup>r</sup> men haue form<sup>r</sup>lie run into by vndertakinge their voyadges out of the Indies att vnseasonable times, therefore wee chardge yo<sup>u</sup> all expreslie that att what places soeu<sup>r</sup> the shipps shall be laden yo<sup>u</sup> be carefull presentlie to take the right Monsones in eu<sup>r</sup>y place &amp; not to beate vp &amp; downe, but rather to attend the true seasonns: &amp; haue yt spetially in remembrance that the portingalles make choise of the fine of Ianuarie &amp; prime of ffebruarie to beginne their voyadge from those parte, w<sup>ch</sup> tyme will bringe yo<sup>u</sup> vpon o<sup>r</sup> Coastes either in Maye or Iune: And att w<sup>ch</sup> tyme the weather is seasonable here in theis partes whereas by former wofull experience wee haue found the could att their retourne to be the ou<sup>r</sup>throwe of o<sup>r</sup> people.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">36 Item whereas wee haue giuen o<sup>r</sup> direccõns in former articles for the ꝑcuringe of trade att Suratt Bantam, the Molloccoes Iapan or els where: yett notw<sup>th</sup>standinge wee leaue yt to the discrecõn &amp; best iudgm<sup>t</sup> of our cheefe Captaine and Comander for the tyme beinge w<sup>th</sup> the rest of the Counsell appointed for that purpose, to take the oportunitie either of theis or any other places where by informacõn yo<sup>u</sup> may receiue the best hopes of trade in any parte of the Indies.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">37. Item yf yo<sup>u</sup> shall learne by true informacõn that yo<sup>u</sup> may haue safe passadge vp to Goga wee suppose yt will proue as beneficiall to shape yo<sup>r</sup> course directlie thither, as to putt ou<sup>r</sup> the barr to Suratt for that the Comodities of Cambaia are brought downe as plentifully to Goga as to Suratt: Butt herein as before wee referr yo<sup>u</sup> to further aduice &amp; to the due Consideracõn thereof as yo<sup>u</sup> shall be incourradged att yo<sup>r</sup> comeinge into those partes.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">38. Item whereas the stores appteyneinge to eu<sup>r</sup>y ꝑticuler office, are nowe sufficientlie furnished for this voyadge &amp; Invoices <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-510" data-page="510" data-folio="418" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n509_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 418">[p. 418]</button> deliu<sup>r</sup>ed vnto the Captaines and Comanders in eu<sup>r</sup>y shipp of eu<sup>r</sup>y pticular store, w<sup>ch</sup> heretofore in some of o<sup>r</sup> former voyadges haue throughe the remisnes of some of o<sup>r</sup> Comanders bene ymbecilled &amp; appropriated to mens pticular vses: ffor the better preuentinge therefore of the like inconveniences hereafter: wee ordeine and giue ytt in chardge vnto yo<sup>u</sup> all the Captaines &amp; Comanders of o<sup>r</sup> shipps thatt yo<sup>u</sup> cause true notice to be taken &amp; a iust accompt kept of the expence of the Gunners Carpenters Boatsonns Stewarde and eu<sup>r</sup>y such store both by the purser &amp; some others that yo<sup>u</sup> shall appointe therevnto That att yo<sup>r</sup> retourne wee may receiue from you a iust accompt of eu<sup>r</sup>y pticuler to be awnsweared accordinge to the Invoice nowe deliu<sup>r</sup>ed vnto yo<sup>u</sup>: And howe the same haue beene spent &amp; disposed of.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">39 Item forasmuch as o<sup>r</sup> ffactors att Bantam &amp; elswhere haue heretofore receiued and paid them selues such wages as haue beene due vnto them for their sallarie, &amp; ymployed there in priuate tradeinge for their owne pticuler gaine w<sup>th</sup> the greate neglect of o<sup>rs</sup>, appropriateinge all the riche and pretious thinge to their owne vse and nothinge for the Company butt pepp and grosse Comodities wee doe therefore resolue and sett yt downe for an expresse order hereafter to be obserued That no ffactor or other ymployed in o<sup>r</sup> said seruice shall be pmitted to receiue, or take in those partes such wages as shall growe due vnto them, [()] but onlie soe much as shall be necessarie for their apprell) &amp; such vses accomptinge &amp; alloweinge after the rate of v<sup>s</sup> for eu<sup>r</sup>y Ryall of eight. And the rest of their wages to be paid in England to such as they shall consigne them vnto, accordinge to the agreem<sup>t</sup> made w<sup>th</sup> eu<sup>r</sup>y one in pticuler att the tyme of their interteynem<sup>t</sup>.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">40 Item to prevent disorders &amp; misbehauio<sup>rs</sup> of the Marriners of theis o<sup>r</sup> shipps, either in their passadge out, or retourne home, vpon o<sup>r</sup> Coastes: wee ordeyne that yo<sup>u</sup> suffer none of o<sup>r</sup> foresaid shipps either outwarde or homewarde to goe into any of the porte of <em>ffalmouth, Plimouth</em>, or <em>Darthmouth</em> vnlesse greate necessitie compell yo<sup>u</sup> therevnto.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">And lastlie, haueinge remembred whatt wee thinke needfull for yo<sup>r</sup> direccõn in this voyadge and relyinge vpon yo<sup>r</sup> dilligence &amp; care in pursueinge our busines to o<sup>r</sup> most advantage, Wee <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-511" data-page="511" data-folio="419" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n510_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 419">[p. 419]</button> comitt yo<sup>u</sup> to the protecc̃on of the Almightie God whome wee beseech to blesse yo<sup>r</sup> endevo<sup>rs</sup> &amp; graunte yo<sup>u</sup> a safe passadge &amp; safe retourne to all o<sup>r</sup> Comfortes.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">41 Item for the better comforte and recreation of such of o<sup>r</sup> ffactors as are recideinge in the Indies: wee haue sent the worke of that worthie seruant of Christe M<sup>r</sup>: W<sup>m</sup>: Perkins to enstruct their mynde &amp; feede their soules w<sup>th</sup> thatt heauenlie foode of the knowledge of the trueth of Gode word, &amp; the booke of Martirs in twoe voleumes, as alsoe M<sup>r</sup> Hackluite Voyadge to recreate their spiritte w<sup>th</sup> varietie of historie: w<sup>ch</sup> bookes wee desier to haue lefte w<sup>th</sup> m<sup>r</sup> Lawrence ffemell o<sup>r</sup> cheefe Agent or some other o<sup>r</sup> cheefe ffactor in those partes where most of o<sup>r</sup> people shall be resident to remaine there for the Comforte and vses before mentioned, where wee requier that they should haue espetiall care to sanctifie the Sabboth daye, &amp; to reade vpon those devyne booke for the instrucc̃on and comforte of all those that shall be there remayninge.<sup class="fn-ref"><a id="ref-sec-08-fn-139" href="#sec-08-fn-139" data-fn-target="sec-08-fn-139" role="doc-noteref">1</a></sup></p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">42 Item whereas by wofull experience wee found a mayne error comitted by the Commanders, &amp; all others that were in the Assention, for that seinge her in the extremitie of dangers vpon her casteinge, they did not vse such present and speedie meanes for the safeguard of such moneys as was remayneinge in her, ffor <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-512" data-page="512" data-folio="420" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/registerofletter00eastuoft/page/n511_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 420">[p. 420]</button> prevencoñ therefore of the like error &amp; maine oversight hereafter, wee aduise &amp; expreslie giue ytt in chardge, that yf ytt should please God to send the like Crosse vpon any of theis o<sup>r</sup> shipps w<sup>ch</sup> he for his mercies sake, w<sup>th</sup>hould, that then in the like extremitie The Comanders Officers &amp; all others doe vse their best care skill and power, to convaye away to land all such Chestes of money w<sup>ch</sup> shall then be restinge or remayneinge in the said shipps by there boate or for want of such Conveyance, to boye ytt into the seas, that afterwarde some better meanes may be p̄cured for recou<sup>r</sup>y and safeguard thereof.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">43 Item wee requier yo<sup>u</sup> that you send vs a p̄ticular Invoice of eu<sup>r</sup>y shipps Cargason by the rest of the shipps, And [of] that yo<sup>u</sup> leaue on gen<sup>r</sup>all Cargason att Bantam to be sent by the next shipps thatt shall come from thence, that thereby the Company may be satisfied, whatt goode you haue sent yf ought should happen to the other shipps, (then well) w<sup>ch</sup> God forbid. And therefore faile yo<sup>u</sup> not in any wise, as you respect vs &amp; o<sup>r</sup> loue.</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><p class="prose-p">Iohn Sayris ⊕ Gabriell Towerson ⊕</p></div><div class="prose-unit"><blockquote><p>“but belonging to a private Library. Our desire is, y<sup>t</sup> you would be pleased to” furnish y<sup>e</sup> Library with a supply of some of the Ancient Writers, and if you shall “thinke it good with these, or some part of them y<sup>t</sup> follow.” Bibliotheca veterum Patrum, Hieronymi opera “Justini Martyris opera Gregorij magni opera” Tertulliani opera Clementij Alexandrini “Ambrosij opera opera, ex editione heasij” And because wee have not any thing in y<sup>e</sup> Library of the Ancient Councells, wee “could wish for the Collection of them by Binnius; unto w<sup>ch</sup>, if you please to add a” booke entitled Pugio fidei, Authore Raymundo Martini, ordinis Cecistrensis, with “the Corpus Juris Canonici, wee shall thankfully receive them; as being Bookes” here very usefull. There are in your Library 6 volumes fairely bound in blew “Turkey Leather and gilded on the Leaves, the gift of Thomas Rich, Esq<sup>r</sup>, containing” the holy Bible with its severall ancient versions, into the orientall Languages. Wee “are now given to understand y<sup>t</sup> there is a Lexicon printed in two volumes designed” as a helpe for the easier attainment of the said Languages, Our request is y<sup>t</sup> you “will be pleased to send us y<sup>t</sup> Lexicon, whereby the booke will be perfected, &amp; that” Bible rendered aboundantly more usefull then at present it is.” (<em>O. C. 3185.</em>)</p></blockquote></div><section class="apparatus-zone" aria-label="Footnotes"><h2 class="apparatus-heading">Critical Apparatus &amp; Notes</h2><ol class="apparatus-list"><li id="sec-08-fn-001" class="apparatus-item" role="doc-endnote"><a class="fn-backlink" href="#ref-sec-08-fn-001" aria-label="Return to text">1.</a> <span class="fn-body">There is another copy of this document among the <em>Marine Records</em> at the India Office (marked C 1).  The two have been carefully compared, and differences of any importance noted.</span></li><li id="sec-08-fn-002" class="apparatus-item" role="doc-endnote"><a class="fn-backlink" href="#ref-sec-08-fn-002" aria-label="Return to text">2.</a> <span class="fn-body">“Wee haue only thought it fit for” &amp;c. (<em>Mar. Rec.</em> copy).</span></li><li id="sec-08-fn-003" class="apparatus-item" role="doc-endnote"><a class="fn-backlink" href="#ref-sec-08-fn-003" aria-label="Return to text">3.</a> <span class="fn-body">Bordering upon.</span></li><li id="sec-08-fn-004" class="apparatus-item" role="doc-endnote"><a class="fn-backlink" href="#ref-sec-08-fn-004" aria-label="Return to text">1.</a> <span class="fn-body">Marseilles.</span></li><li id="sec-08-fn-005" class="apparatus-item" role="doc-endnote"><a class="fn-backlink" href="#ref-sec-08-fn-005" aria-label="Return to text">2.</a> <span class="fn-body">Mocha.</span></li><li id="sec-08-fn-006" class="apparatus-item" role="doc-endnote"><a class="fn-backlink" href="#ref-sec-08-fn-006" aria-label="Return to text">3.</a> <span class="fn-body">Partes (<em>Mar. Rec.</em> copy).</span></li><li id="sec-08-fn-007" class="apparatus-item" role="doc-endnote"><a class="fn-backlink" href="#ref-sec-08-fn-007" aria-label="Return to text">1.</a> <span class="fn-body">See page 252, note.</span></li><li id="sec-08-fn-008" class="apparatus-item" role="doc-endnote"><a class="fn-backlink" href="#ref-sec-08-fn-008" aria-label="Return to text">2.</a> <span class="fn-body">Barred.</span></li><li id="sec-08-fn-009" class="apparatus-item" role="doc-endnote"><a class="fn-backlink" href="#ref-sec-08-fn-009" aria-label="Return to text">1.</a> <span class="fn-body">Acctions (<em>Mar. Rec.</em> copy).</span></li><li id="sec-08-fn-010" class="apparatus-item" role="doc-endnote"><a class="fn-backlink" href="#ref-sec-08-fn-010" aria-label="Return to text">1.</a> <span class="fn-body">Rates (<em>Mar. Rec.</em> copy).</span></li><li id="sec-08-fn-011" class="apparatus-item" role="doc-endnote"><a class="fn-backlink" href="#ref-sec-08-fn-011" aria-label="Return to text">2.</a> <span class="fn-body">Receyue (<em>Mar. Rec.</em> copy).</span></li><li id="sec-08-fn-012" class="apparatus-item" role="doc-endnote"><a class="fn-backlink" href="#ref-sec-08-fn-012" aria-label="Return to text">3.</a> <span class="fn-body">Allowe (<em>Mar. Rec.</em> copy),—an evident blunder.</span></li><li id="sec-08-fn-013" class="apparatus-item" role="doc-endnote"><a class="fn-backlink" href="#ref-sec-08-fn-013" aria-label="Return to text">4.</a> <span class="fn-body">Them (<em>Mar. Rec.</em> copy).</span></li><li id="sec-08-fn-014" class="apparatus-item" role="doc-endnote"><a class="fn-backlink" href="#ref-sec-08-fn-014" aria-label="Return to text">1.</a> <span class="fn-body">Omitted in <em>Mar. Rec.</em> copy.</span></li><li id="sec-08-fn-015" class="apparatus-item" role="doc-endnote"><a class="fn-backlink" href="#ref-sec-08-fn-015" aria-label="Return to text">2.</a> <span class="fn-body">The reference is to the famous pearl fishery of the Bahrein Islands.</span></li><li id="sec-08-fn-016" class="apparatus-item" role="doc-endnote"><a class="fn-backlink" href="#ref-sec-08-fn-016" aria-label="Return to text">3.</a> <span class="fn-body">Streames (<em>Mar. Rec.</em> copy). For the occurrence referred to, see page 219, note.</span></li><li id="sec-08-fn-017" class="apparatus-item" role="doc-endnote"><a class="fn-backlink" href="#ref-sec-08-fn-017" aria-label="Return to text">4.</a> <span class="fn-body">To bee donne or obtained (<em>Mar. Rec.</em> copy).</span></li><li id="sec-08-fn-018" class="apparatus-item" role="doc-endnote"><a class="fn-backlink" href="#ref-sec-08-fn-018" aria-label="Return to text">5.</a> <span class="fn-body">For such goodes onlie as you shall land att Surratt (<em>Mar. Rec.</em> copy).</span></li><li id="sec-08-fn-019" class="apparatus-item" role="doc-endnote"><a class="fn-backlink" href="#ref-sec-08-fn-019" aria-label="Return to text">1.</a> <span class="fn-body">This seems to be a mistake for “unlanded.” <em>Mar. Rec.</em> copy has “unladed.”</span></li><li id="sec-08-fn-020" class="apparatus-item" role="doc-endnote"><a class="fn-backlink" href="#ref-sec-08-fn-020" aria-label="Return to text">2.</a> <span class="fn-body">Engaged as factor, 16th Nov., 1609; died at Bantam, apparently in 1613.</span></li><li id="sec-08-fn-021" class="apparatus-item" role="doc-endnote"><a class="fn-backlink" href="#ref-sec-08-fn-021" aria-label="Return to text">3.</a> <span class="fn-body">Engaged, 15th Jan., 1610, to serve seven years and to adventure 100<em>l.</em>; he was an active servant of the Company, and for some time was chief of the factory at Patani, where he died, 12th May, 1616.</span></li><li id="sec-08-fn-022" class="apparatus-item" role="doc-endnote"><a class="fn-backlink" href="#ref-sec-08-fn-022" aria-label="Return to text">4.</a> <span class="fn-body">Glemham (<em>Mar. Rec.</em> copy).</span></li><li id="sec-08-fn-023" class="apparatus-item" role="doc-endnote"><a class="fn-backlink" href="#ref-sec-08-fn-023" aria-label="Return to text">5.</a> <span class="fn-body">Engaged, 8th Jan., 1610; died towards the end of 1613, while on a voyage to Sukadana.</span></li><li id="sec-08-fn-024" class="apparatus-item" role="doc-endnote"><a class="fn-backlink" href="#ref-sec-08-fn-024" aria-label="Return to text">6.</a> <span class="fn-body">Apparently an error for Rowland Webb.</span></li><li id="sec-08-fn-025" class="apparatus-item" role="doc-endnote"><a class="fn-backlink" href="#ref-sec-08-fn-025" aria-label="Return to text">7.</a> <span class="fn-body">Lyeinge (<em>Mar. Rec.</em> copy).</span></li><li id="sec-08-fn-026" class="apparatus-item" role="doc-endnote"><a class="fn-backlink" href="#ref-sec-08-fn-026" aria-label="Return to text">1.</a> <span class="fn-body">Accident (<em>Mar. Rec.</em> copy).</span></li><li id="sec-08-fn-027" class="apparatus-item" role="doc-endnote"><a class="fn-backlink" href="#ref-sec-08-fn-027" aria-label="Return to text">2.</a> <span class="fn-body">Housekeepper (<em>Mar. Rec.</em> copy).</span></li><li id="sec-08-fn-028" class="apparatus-item" role="doc-endnote"><a class="fn-backlink" href="#ref-sec-08-fn-028" aria-label="Return to text">3.</a> <span class="fn-body">Anie rare thinges as fine birdes (<em>Mar. Rec.</em> copy).</span></li><li id="sec-08-fn-029" class="apparatus-item" role="doc-endnote"><a class="fn-backlink" href="#ref-sec-08-fn-029" aria-label="Return to text">4.</a> <span class="fn-body">Other order (<em>Mar. Rec.</em> copy).</span></li><li id="sec-08-fn-030" class="apparatus-item" role="doc-endnote"><a class="fn-backlink" href="#ref-sec-08-fn-030" aria-label="Return to text">1.</a> <span class="fn-body">The <em>Mar. Rec.</em> copy concludes with— To this have firmed S<sup>r</sup> Thomas Smith gou<sup>r</sup>nor S<sup>r</sup> John Wattes   Hughe Hamersly Robert Bell   Robert Middellton William Harryson   Thomas Stiles Robert Ofley Of these the copyist of the text has entered only the governor’s name; the others being copies of the signatures of the factors concerned, appended as an acknowledgment that they had made themselves acquainted with the instructions contained in the document (see page 38, note).</span></li><li id="sec-08-fn-031" class="apparatus-item" role="doc-endnote"><a class="fn-backlink" href="#ref-sec-08-fn-031" aria-label="Return to text">2.</a> <span class="fn-body">The manuscript in the <em>Marine Records</em> referred to on page 319, note, contains also a copy of this document, which has been compared in similar manner with the present text, and variations noted.</span></li><li id="sec-08-fn-032" class="apparatus-item" role="doc-endnote"><a class="fn-backlink" href="#ref-sec-08-fn-032" aria-label="Return to text">3.</a> <span class="fn-body">Died in the voyage, together with his son, and two servants.</span></li><li id="sec-08-fn-033" class="apparatus-item" role="doc-endnote"><a class="fn-backlink" href="#ref-sec-08-fn-033" aria-label="Return to text">1.</a> <span class="fn-body">Died on the homeward voyage of the <em>Peppercorn</em>, 27th July, 1613.</span></li><li id="sec-08-fn-034" class="apparatus-item" role="doc-endnote"><a class="fn-backlink" href="#ref-sec-08-fn-034" aria-label="Return to text">2.</a> <span class="fn-body">See page 356.</span></li><li id="sec-08-fn-035" class="apparatus-item" role="doc-endnote"><a class="fn-backlink" href="#ref-sec-08-fn-035" aria-label="Return to text">1.</a> <span class="fn-body">Pilot (<em>Mar. Rec. copy</em>).</span></li><li id="sec-08-fn-036" class="apparatus-item" role="doc-endnote"><a class="fn-backlink" href="#ref-sec-08-fn-036" aria-label="Return to text">2.</a> <span class="fn-body">Discouery (<em>Mar. Rec. copy</em>).</span></li><li id="sec-08-fn-037" class="apparatus-item" role="doc-endnote"><a class="fn-backlink" href="#ref-sec-08-fn-037" aria-label="Return to text">1.</a> <span class="fn-body">“Tenth” in <em>Mar. Rec.</em> copy).</span></li><li id="sec-08-fn-050" class="apparatus-item" role="doc-endnote"><a class="fn-backlink" href="#ref-sec-08-fn-050" aria-label="Return to text">4.</a> <span class="fn-body"><em>Mar. Rec.</em> copy).</span></li><li id="sec-08-fn-061" class="apparatus-item" role="doc-endnote"><a class="fn-backlink" href="#ref-sec-08-fn-061" aria-label="Return to text">2.</a> <span class="fn-body">London (<em>Mar. Rec.</em> copy).</span></li><li id="sec-08-fn-062" class="apparatus-item" role="doc-endnote"><a class="fn-backlink" href="#ref-sec-08-fn-062" aria-label="Return to text">3.</a> <span class="fn-body">Reserue from (<em>Mar. Rec.</em> copy). Rec.</em> copy).</span></li><li id="sec-08-fn-065" class="apparatus-item" role="doc-endnote"><a class="fn-backlink" href="#ref-sec-08-fn-065" aria-label="Return to text">6.</a> <span class="fn-body">Freed (<em>Mar. Rec.</em> copy).</span></li><li id="sec-08-fn-066" class="apparatus-item" role="doc-endnote"><a class="fn-backlink" href="#ref-sec-08-fn-066" aria-label="Return to text">1.</a> <span class="fn-body">Cotton (<em>Mar.
